[p. 242]

# 4 Annotated translation of the Rādhā Tantra

## First Paṭala

*Oṃ.* Obeisance to the supreme goddess!

*Śrī Pārvati said:* <sup>1</sup>‘God of gods, great god, conqueror of death, eternal one, ruler of Ganeśa, Nandi and Candra, you whom Viṣṇu serves – <sup>2</sup>Lord Śaṅkara, Īśāna! Vāsudeva’s secret, the delightful Rādhā Tantra, has previously been hinted at just in the form of a story.[^a] Please retell it as an extremely rare Tantra!’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>3</sup>‘Beautiful one, Rādhā Tantra, Vāsudeva’s secret, is an extremely secret Tantra, pure and always spotless. <sup>4</sup>Dear goddess, as the Kālī and the Toṣaṇa Tantras,[^b] it is full of all power and will perfect all *vidyās*.[^c] <sup>5</sup>I will now speak, beautiful one; listen carefully! Greatest mistress, Vāsudeva Hṛṣīkeśa once hastened to my company. <sup>6</sup>Dear one, hear what he said!
‘ “Conqueror of death, mighty armed one, master, I bow to you. Please tell me what kind of recitation I should do, fortunate one![^d] <sup>7</sup>Lord rich in [p. 243] penance, you are the boat for crossing birth and death; <sup>8</sup>for without you, there can be no perfection.”
‘Listen now Pārvati to all of what I said to Vāsudeva, great mistress, <sup>9</sup>after I had heard the nectar-filled words of Vāsudeva Viṣṇu of unlimited splendour. <sup>10</sup>“Do not fear, Viṣṇu, beautiful one; worship Tripurā, for without the Ten *vidyās*, there can be no perfection. <sup>11</sup>Therefore, among the ten *vidyās*, this supreme Tripurā is the foremost. This goddess and mistress awards the four goals of life, she enchants the world, <sup>12</sup>she is beautiful, most worshippable and devoted to protecting the world. She constantly resides in my heart; I bow to her and speak.
<sup>13</sup>‘“First extract the Brahmāṇī, then the Bhaga-seed, the Rati-seed, the Pṛthvī-seed <sup>14</sup>and at the end add Māyā: thus carefully make the Vāgbhava. This Vāgbhava *kūṭa* perpetually enchants the three worlds.<sup>ǁ</sup>
<sup>15</sup>“Goddess, extract the Śiva-seed, then the Bhṛgu-seed, then Kumudvatī, then emptiness, <sup>16</sup>then the Pṛthvī-seed and at the end Māyā, the supreme syllable. This Kāmarāja *kūṭa* is most rare.[^x]
<sup>17</sup>“Extract the Bhṛgu-seed, then Kumudvatī, then the Indra-seed and at the end Vikaṭaparā.”[^ba]
<sup>18</sup>‘Greatest mistress, this fivefold *vidyā* is the threefold *kūṭa*.[^bb] Dear one, previously I explained this practice to him; I have now explained everything. [p. 244] <sup>19</sup>‘Vāsudeva heard this and quickly left for the city of Kāśī, where Kāśī, Mahāmāyā eternally dwells in the form of a *yoni*.[^bc] <sup>20</sup>This Kāśī is most worshippable and is served by Brahmā and others. If one performs recitations there for one watch,[^bd] one will gain the fruit of a hundred thousand years. <sup>21</sup>There Vāsudeva went, and after offering his worship, began his recitation. Properly he worshipped goddess Bhavānī, the greatest mistress, <sup>22</sup>making his body, mind and words one, beautiful one, at powerful Puṣkara<sup>†ǁ</sup> in this delightful city of Sadāśiva. <sup>23</sup>He placed his head on the ground and stretched his feet upwards, greatest mistress;[^bx] but even after this terrible penance, he gained no perfection. <sup>24</sup>Doing this, great mistress, Vāsudeva Viṣṇu of unlimited splendour started to shine like a thousand suns, <sup>25</sup>but still, greatest mistress, he did not gain perfection.
‘At that moment, lotus-eyed one, Mahāmāyā appeared, <sup>26</sup>and once this Mahāmāyā Tripurā, mistress of the world, had appeared, she glanced at Vāsudeva, who could only hold his breath. Dear one, looking at him with compassionate glances, she became as if drenched with nectar.[^ca]

*‘Tripurā said:* <sup>27</sup>“Get up, child, son; why does he perform such penance? Get up quickly, son, and choose a boon, child!”
<sup>28</sup>‘Sweetly smiling one, hearing this supreme utterance, this flood of nectar of Tripurā, and having heard her words, Vāsudeva immediately gave up his yoga <sup>29</sup>and threw himself at the feet of Tripurā.
‘ “Obeisance to you, mother Tripurā! Obeisance to you, remover of afflictions!
<sup>30</sup>Obeisance to you, who is worshipped by Śaṅkara! Obeisance to you, who is worshipped by Kṛṣṇa! Obeisance to you, giver of nectar! Mother, you are of the origin of the three worlds. The goddess that has appeared resides in Viṣṇu’s heart.”’

## Second Paṭala

*‘Tripurā said:* <sup>1</sup>“Mighty-armed Vāsudeva, listen to my supreme words. You are a god, best of sons, so what is the use of engaging in austerities? <sup>2</sup>My son, [p. 245] without the Clan behaviour,[^cb] there will be no perfection. When you are without a Śakti,[^cc] my son, how can I promise perfection? <sup>3</sup>Lakṣmī is born from a portion of me; why have you given her up to engage in austerities? Son, your stage of life is useless, your worship is useless, and your recitations will bear no fruit. <sup>4</sup>My son, without the Clan behaviour, everything will be useless.
‘“You should zealously engage in congress with a Śakti, <sup>5</sup>for without enjoyment, best of sons, rich in penance, there can be no perfection of *vidyā*. When a practitioner is agitated, the divinity will also be agitated. <sup>6</sup>Therefore, find yourself enjoyment and then engage in the rite of recitation, for without enjoyment, best of sons, there can be no liberation.
<sup>7</sup>‘“Listen, best of sons, to the truth of initiation in the correct order. Son, when one has turned ten years but before twelve, <sup>8</sup>one should hear the sixteen names of Hari, one after the other. My son, without Hari’s name, the ears cannot be purified.”

*āsudeva said:* <sup>9</sup>“Listen, mother Mahāmāyā, the embodiment of the seed of the universe! Please explain step by step your understanding of Hari’s name.”

*‘Tripurā replied:*

<sup>10</sup>*‘ “Hare Kṛṣṇa Hare Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa Hare Hare.*
*‘ “Hare Rāma Hare Rāma Rāma Rāma Hare Hare.*
<sup>11</sup>‘“Best of sons, listen to the thirty-two syllables of Hari’s name, to the parts that accompany it and to its eternal Metre,[^cd] <sup>12</sup>for this Metre is most confidential, an undecaying great abode. You who are rich in penance! Hari’s name is a mantra full of all power.
<sup>13</sup>‘“The seer of the mantra of Hari’s names is Vāsudeva; the metre is Gāyatrī;<sup>‡ǁ</sup> the divinity is blessed Tripurā; and the application is attaining perfection in my Mahāvidyā.
<sup>14</sup>‘“Best of sons, first a man should hear this mantra. Son, rich in penance, when he hears it from the mouth of a Brahmin with his right ear, <sup>15</sup>he should first hear the Metre and then the mantra; thus he will become pure. When one hears it before the age of twelve, the ears will become purified.
<sup>16</sup>‘“Son, a man or woman who engages with a Mahāvidyā without purification of the ears will immediately become an inhabitant of hell. [p. 246] <sup>17</sup>‘“You who are honoured by the gods! When one comes to one’s sixteenth year, one should hear the pure and eternal Mahāvidyā, the very form of Brahman, <sup>18</sup>from the mouth of a Brahmin of the Clan, and one will become directly made of Brahman. You who are rich in penance! One who also performs the secret of the Clan as described by Śiva[^cx] <sup>19</sup>will attain the perfection of the *vidyā* and the eight majesties,[^da] for without this secret, son, everything will just be useless labour. <sup>20</sup>Therefore, best of sons, keep this secret confidential and never recite the *vidyā* without the secret.
<sup>21</sup>‘ “Best of sons, rich in penance, this is the highest secret of Hari’s name: HA is without a doubt Śiva himself; <sup>22</sup>R is eternally goddess Tripurā, the embodiment of the ten *vidyās*; you who are rich in penance, E should be understood as the vulva, as the *yoni* itself. <sup>23</sup>HA is also he who has a form of emptiness and RE she who takes on a body. Hari is Tripurā herself − he is without a doubt a form of myself.
<sup>24</sup>‘“K refers to she who awards desires and who takes whatever form she desires; best of sons, Ṛ is known as the foremost Śakti. <sup>25</sup>K and Ṛ are Kāminī, a portion of Vaiṣṇavī; Ṣ is the Moon god with his sixteen parts; <sup>26</sup>best of sons, ṆA is directly she who has the form of emancipation; the combination of these two is directly Tripurā Bhairavī. <sup>27</sup>Best of sons, KṚṢṆA KṚṢṆA is Mahāmāyā, the world itself; HARE HARE is the goddess who is the very form of Śiva and Śakti. <sup>28</sup>The words HARE RĀMA are directly the supreme goddess made of light. R Ā is Tripurā herself, accompanied by the nectar of bliss, but MA is Mahāmāyā, an eternal Yoginī of Rudra’s.[^db] The *visarga*,[^dc] best of sons, is directly the supreme Kuṇḍalinī; <sup>30</sup>the words RĀMA RĀMA are directly Śiva and Śakti, and the words HARE HARE contain two Śaktis.
<sup>31</sup>‘“Best of excellent sons, the twice-born one who recites this mantra, adding *oṁ* at the beginning and end, becomes beautiful among the Mahāvidyās.
<sup>32</sup>This initiation should be known as the supreme, as wedded to the foremost power. Best of sons, Hari’s name is the foremost Vaiṣṇavī herself.
<sup>33</sup>‘“Without this blessed Vaiṣṇavī initiation and without the grace of a true preceptor, one will go to the Raurava hell[^dd] along with a hundred thousand [p. 247] relatives. <sup>34</sup>These sixteen names have thirty-two syllables and they become an incomparable thirty-four when *oṁ* is added at the beginning and at the end.
<sup>35</sup>‘“Without Hari’s name, my son, initiation is always useless. Having heard the supreme syllables of Hari’s name from a divinity of the Clan; <sup>36</sup>having heard the supreme syllables, Brahmins, Kṣatriyas, Vaiśyas and Śūdras should accept initiation and be beautiful among the Mahāvidyās.
<sup>37</sup>‘“But listen to the sinful result for one who unwittingly accepts initiation or Hari’s name from the mouth of a Śūdra! <sup>38</sup>If even a Śūdra hears this *vidyā* or the supreme mantra from the mouth of a Śūdra he will go to the Raurava hell along with a hundred thousand relatives. <sup>39</sup>Both the giver and the receiver will attain the same result: one attains the same result from each syllable as from killing a Brahmin.
‘“My son Vāsudeva, hear what else I said on this occasion!”’

## Third Paṭala

*‘Tripurā said:* <sup>1</sup>“When one attains the age of sixteen, one must devotedly take initiation. My son, if one does not, when one attains the age of sixteen years, <sup>2</sup>Hari’s name becomes useless when the sixteenth year passes. Therefore, beautiful one, one should diligently get oneself initiated at the age of sixteen, <sup>3</sup>for otherwise all one’s actions become like those of an animal.
‘“Mighty-armed Vāsudeva, listen to my supreme secret! <sup>4</sup>If one openly utters Hari’s name in an assembly or wherever, best of sons, then a Mahāvidyā will become concealed. <sup>5</sup>My son, rich in penance, incessantly recite a Mahāvidyā, whether pure or impure, walking, sitting or lying down. <sup>6</sup>Mādhava, a thoughtful person should recite a Mahāvidyā everywhere. After one has worshipped the Śivaliṅga, one should recite the Mahāvidyā. <sup>7</sup>Dear one,<sup>§ǁ</sup> my son, one should variously worship the Liṅga with Bel-leaves and other items and continuously meditate on a Mahāvidyā with heart and mind. <sup>8</sup>Mādhava! One who at night accompanied by a Śakti variously worships and recites and constantly follows all of the Clan behaviour as explained in the Tantras proclaimed by Śiva, <sup>9</sup>will attain perfection. My son, you can attain no perfection without the Clan behaviour.”

*‘Tripurā continued:* “ <sup>10</sup>Listen, mighty-armed son, to my delightful words, to a secret most confidential, most closely guarded in the three worlds! <sup>11</sup>I will narrate to you a tale wonderful and variegated of the wonderful and variegated garland lying on my chest. <sup>12</sup>On my chest shines eternally an unwithering garland of rubies, red like china-roses. <sup>13</sup>A garland with the gem called [p. 248] Kaustubha shines in the middle of another garland. It is the origin of various gems, elephants, horses, chariots and foot-soldiers. <sup>14</sup>My son, this great garland called Hastinī is eternally one of my *dūtīs*.[^dx] Another garland made of lotuses shines on my chest. <sup>15</sup>It is called Padminī and is most wonderful, directly a form of Padminī herself. Son, the garland that is bright, wonderful and variegated <sup>16</sup>is called Citriṇī, and attends to wonderful deeds. The garland that is called Gandhinī carries a most wonderful fragrance.
<sup>17</sup>‘“These *dūtīs*, best of sons, always stay on my chest. These *dūtīs*, best of sons, have all the eight majesties: <sup>18</sup>Hastinī, Padminī, Citriṇī and Gandhinī.[^a-2] My son, gallant Mādhava, this is the Padminī garland. <sup>19</sup>Citriṇī of bright form stays here after pervading the whole universe, and Gandhinī, my son, also pervades all and blossoms here. Best of sons, Hastinī gives birth to the elephants of the quarters.”
<sup>20</sup>‘Thus spoke Mahāmāyā Tripurā of rolling eyes. “You who are rich in penance! <sup>21</sup>This garland is made of Pārijāta garlands and a lotus, without a thread but strung on a thread of desire; <sup>22</sup>a garland that will lead the imperfect to perfection, strung on a thread of desire; this garland consists of all kinds of gems, shining as millions of bolts of lightning, <sup>23</sup>and containing all the fifty letters, son, enchants the world and awards *dharma*, *artha*, *kāma* and *mokṣa*!
<sup>24</sup>‘“Listen, my son, rich in penance, Vāsudeva, Mahāviṣṇu! My garland, the imperishable power of the Mātṛkās,[^b-2] is very hard to overcome. <sup>25</sup>But Mādhava! Pay attention and see this supreme wonder!”
‘Having said this and shown Janārdana Kṛṣṇa some of her supreme wonder, <sup>26</sup>goddess Tripurā, Viṣṇu’s mother and the world itself, took off one of her garlands and quickly gave it to him.’

*Mahādeva continued:* <sup>27</sup>‘Great mistress, I cannot describe this wonder! The fifty Mātṛkās beginning with *a* and ending with *kṣa* are imperishable, <sup>28</sup>imperishable and unlimited they stay around the neck of Tripurā. From the letter *ka* comes millions of heaps of universes – <sup>29</sup>and once born, everything is also in a moment destroyed. In this way, dear one, step by step, the fifty Mātṛkās perpetually <sup>30</sup>create, maintain and destroy.
‘Great mistress, when Hari saw this progression and its inversion, he became bewildered. <sup>31</sup>In this state, the lotus-eyed Vāsudeva, Janārdana rich in penance, [p. 249] saw all the universes. <sup>32</sup>When imperishable Viṣṇu had seen all of this, he settled his heart on the supreme abode of India, with her fifty Seats.[^c-2] <sup>33</sup>There, the eternal Bhagavatī, Mahāmāyā, the world itself, gave up the Satī body and then became Pārvatī. <sup>34</sup>He saw all the places where the parts from your body fell – greatest mistress, Pārvati, daughter of the mountain! – <sup>35</sup>Kāmākhya and so on, great Seats of a terrible appearance, <sup>36</sup>but, great mistress, he also saw that the wonderful, supreme place of Mathurā, the circle of Vraja, had a gentle appearance. <sup>37</sup>When they had showed this to Janārdana, the original Mother and the Mātṛkās all immediately vanished.

*‘Tripurā said:* <sup>38</sup>“Vāsudeva, best of sons, why do you doubt? Why are you dejected, my son? Wear the garland around your neck. <sup>39</sup>Through the power in this garland you will attain everything, the supreme, confidential secret, along with the fifty principles.[^d-2] <sup>40</sup>The great Kalāvatī garland always hangs around my neck. It is white, red, yellow and black. <sup>41</sup>The garland that is made of lotuses has the colour of asparagus flowers; Hastinī is white as pure crystal. <sup>42</sup>Best of sons! Citriṇī, the giver of all auspiciousness, is yellow, while Gandhinī, best of sons, is the colour of the Moringa plant.”<sup>ǁ</sup>
<sup>43</sup>‘Great mistress, thus spoke Mahāmāyā, the eternal, original Śakti, the splendour of whose nails is the highest Brahman; <sup>44</sup>a millionth part of whose nails is the ancient Brahman; on the edge of a millionth part of whose nails stand the five divinities, <sup>45</sup>Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Rudra, Īśvara and Sadāśiva – these five divinities eternally consist of light, great mistress, <sup>46</sup>and they are waking, sleep, deep sleep and the fourth, greatest mistress, but Sadāśiva is the sleeping Brahman. <sup>47</sup>To my knowledge, great mistress, there is nothing higher than this. This god Vāsudeva is imperishable Viṣṇu himself.
<sup>48</sup>‘Fawn-eyed goddess Pārvatī, listen to what mother Tripurā, the form of primeval Prakṛti, whose form is pure *sattva*, then composedly spoke to Vāsudeva!

*‘Tripurā said:* <sup>49</sup>“My son Vāsudeva, mighty-armed one, do not fear! Best of sons, this garland is a form of me, an embodiment of my shape. <sup>50</sup>It will afford you perfection in your rites, best of sons. Fear not, fear not, best of sons, you will attain perfection in *vidyā*!”

*Śiva continued:* <sup>51</sup>‘Fully satisfied, Vāsudeva bowed at the feet of the highest goddess Tripurā and pleased her by reciting the Devī Sūkta.[^x-2] <sup>52</sup>“Will I ever [p. 250] forget the joy of serving your feet, my Mother, greatest goddess? What should I do? Where should I go?”

*‘Tripurā replied:* <sup>53</sup>“Listen Viṣṇu, mighty armed Vāsudeva, chastiser of enemies! The garland that you carry around your neck is the omniscient Kalāvatī. <sup>54</sup>My son, ocean of merits, I have told you everything. Best of sons, you have heard her words; now do the needful.”
<sup>55</sup>‘Having said this, Mahāmāyā Tripurā, the mistress of the world, the mother of the worlds, suddenly disappeared.’

## Fourth Paṭala

*Pārvatī said:* <sup>1</sup>‘God of gods, great god, master! Tell me in detail! The supreme master, eternal Vāsudeva then <sup>2</sup>placed the garland of goddess Kalāvatī around his neck. You whom the gods worship, I ask you about this secret with the greatest devotion!’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>3</sup>‘Listen, impudent woman, and I will tell you that which increases endless knowledge! Listen now carefully, doe-eyed Pārvatī, to what goddess Kalāvatī said to Vāsudeva.

*‘Kalāvatī said:* <sup>4</sup>“Mighty-armed Vāsudeva, worshipped by the gods, choose a proper boon! I will now do your work for you. Beautiful one, quickly remember all that which is easily seen in the garland!”

*‘Vāsudeva replied:* <sup>5</sup>“Highest mistress, one cannot say what can be seen, but I do again and again recollect the worship of your feet.”’

*Pārvati asked:* <sup>6</sup>‘Master, tell me all that Vāsudeva saw; the wonder, the highest abode that he saw in the Padminī garland, <sup>7</sup>what this Kṛṣṇa, the supreme self, saw in the Hastinī garland, master, what he saw in the Gandhinī garland and what he saw in the Citriṇī garland. Please tell me all of this wonderful narration, Lord and master!’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>8</sup>‘Highest mistress, listen carefully to this secret, this most wonderful, most confidential advice dripping with nectar, <sup>9</sup>this most meritorious great place of pilgrimage, embodying all the Vedas!
‘The garland around Vāsudeva’s neck was Kalāvatī, <sup>10</sup>a part of the imperishable and undecaying line of fifty letters, the witness and eternal form of the supreme letters. <sup>11</sup>Goddess, these fifty letters have here taken a form, a body that is dark, that is golden, that shines like clear crystal, <sup>12</sup>that takes the colour of molten gold, that is black, my beautiful goddess, that is multicoloured, that is freshly youthful, <sup>13</sup>that is always sixteen years, whose eyes are always decorated with collyrium, whose face is like a blossoming lotus and who is always slightly smiling. <sup>14</sup>Her unsurpassed teeth are like pomegranate seeds, her long, slender arms are slender like lotus fibres, <sup>15</sup>she is decorated with armlets and bracelets made of shells and other ornaments, she delights all the directions with different kinds of fragrances, <sup>16</sup>and she [p. 251] carries a rosary made from Rudrākṣa seeds.[^ba-2] Highest mistress, all of these supreme goddesses, these Mātṛkās, <sup>17</sup>now always stay around the neck of Viṣṇu in the form of the garland.
‘Listen, mistress of the gods, to the names of the Mātṛkās, one after the other! <sup>18</sup>Pūrṇodarī, Virajā, Śālmalī, Lolākṣī, Bāhulākṣī, <sup>19</sup>Sudīrghaghonā, Gomukhī, Dīrghjihvā, Kumbhodarī, Ūrdhvakeśī, Vikṛtamukhī, <sup>20</sup>Jvālāmukhī, Ulkāmukhī, Suśrīmukhī and Vidyotamukhī – these are the powers of the vowels – <sup>21</sup>Mahākālī and Sarasvatī, full of all perfections, Gaurī, Trailokyavidyā, Mantraśakti, <sup>22</sup>Ādyaśakti, Bhūtamātā, Lambodarī, Drāviṇī, Nāgarī, Khecarī, Mañjarī, <sup>23</sup>Rūpiṇī, Vīriṇī, Kākodarī, Pūtanā, Bhadrakālī, Yoginī, Śaṅkhinī, Garjinī, <sup>24</sup>Kālarātri, Kubjinī, Kapardinī, Vajrayā, Jayā, Sumukhī, Īśvarī, Revatī, Mādhavī, <sup>25</sup>Vāruṇī, Vāyasī, Brahmavidāriṇī, Sahajā, Lakṣmī, Vyāpinī and Māyā[^bb-2] – <sup>26</sup>these Mātṛkā goddesses always stay within the garland, but when they are at the Rudra Seats, they are red as vermillion and their bodies are decorated with red lotuses and skulls.’

## Fifth Paṭala

*Īśvara continued:* <sup>1</sup>‘Dear one, when Vāsudeva Mahāviṣṇu saw this, he became astonished, for from each one,[^bc-2] great mistress, sweetly smiling one, millions of heaps of universes <sup>2</sup>were born, one after the other, like heaps of eggs. Greatest mistress, goddess, this universe is made up of *rajas, sattva and tamas,* <sup>3</sup>*rajas, sattva and tamas* corresponding to Rudra, Viṣṇu and grandfather Brahmā.[^bd-2] One cosmic egg, greatest mistress, is covered by seven layers, <sup>4</sup>and they easily held up cosmic eggs by the millions. When Viṣṇu saw this wonder he was bewildered, <sup>5</sup>for, great mistress, greatest goddess, in each egg were Brahmā and the others.
‘Beautiful, dear one, lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa saw that every egg was the same as this world, <sup>6</sup>and when he had seen everything, he also saw the division of India with its fifty Seats. <sup>7</sup>There, all the Seats are most frightening. The circle of Mathurā, goddess, where one always finds Mount Govardhana, <sup>8</sup>Vṛndā and Mahāmāyā as goddess Kātyāyanī, there Mahāmāyā always resides with Śiva. <sup>9</sup>Mathurā, the circle of Vraja, contains both Śiva and Śakti.
‘From your limbs, great mistress, come all the various Seats, <sup>10</sup>but gentle is Mathurā, great mistress, the circle of Vṛndāvana. In Mathurā, great mistress, [p. 252] is found a direct form of Śakti, <sup>11</sup>and in the Yamunā, great mistress, sweetly smiling one, is Śakti herself. Govardhana, great mistress, is the upper Śakti.
<sup>12</sup>‘Filled with different kinds of forests, endowed with all kinds of *rasa,*<sup>†ǁ</sup> overspread with all kinds of birds and full of creepers and trees, <sup>13</sup>full of most delightful cottages made of different kinds of creepers, attended by divine women − this delightful Vraja is also called Mathurā.
<sup>14</sup>‘Enchanting all, it stands in the middle of a thousand-petalled lotus and is full of cowherds and milkmaidens and herds of cows. <sup>15</sup>Great and beautiful mistress! When lotus-eyed Viṣṇu saw this Vraja in India, he was amazed.
<sup>16</sup>‘Greatest mistress! Mathurā is joined to your hair; Mathurā, the circle of Vṛndāvana is the Seat of your hair. <sup>17</sup>Your hair, great mistress, is fragrant with different kinds of scents, it is decorated with different kinds of flowers and bound up with fragrant garlands. <sup>18</sup>Your enchanting hair is surrounded by bees, dear one, and your braid, mistress of the gods, enchants even the gods! <sup>19</sup>Filled with different kinds of gems and always full of all kinds of pleasures, this circle of Vraja is made from the bundle of your hair.
<sup>20</sup>‘In this land of India, Viṣṇu is the embodiment of *sattva*, and Vraja, the circle of Mathurā, full of unlimited pleasures, <sup>21</sup>the fifty Tattvas, the Mātṛkās and the waters of the Kālindī, is where Kātyāyanī Śivā is. <sup>22</sup>Indra and the other gods come to the banks of the Kālindī and there, great mistress, they do their recitations in the proximity of Kātyāyanī. <sup>23</sup>Goddess Kātyāyanī is the divinity of your hair. In a grove by the Yamunā beautified by Aśoka trees and blossoms, Kātyāyanī Mahāmāyā eternally stays.’

## Sixth Paṭala

*‘Kātyāyanī said:* <sup>1</sup>“Mighty-armed Vāsudeva, my son, do not fear! Go to Mathurā; there you will quickly attain perfection. <sup>2</sup>Go, go, and have congress with Padminī! Lord of gods, my Padminī will in Vraja become Rādhā, and the others, the Mātṛkā goddesses, will always wait upon her.”

*‘Vāsudeva replied:* <sup>3</sup>“Listen, mother Mahāmāyā, giver of the four goals of life! Without you, greatest mistress, there can be no perfection. <sup>4</sup>Beautiful one, quickly show me the greatest mistress Padminī, so that I will be able to remember her every day.”
<sup>5</sup>‘When she heard these words of Vāsudeva, immediately goddess Padminī appeared, seated on a lotus, <sup>6</sup>with slender arms like red bolts of lightning, fragrant like a lotus, enchantingly beautiful, surrounded by her friends, [p. 253] <sup>7</sup>seated always in the middle of a thousand-petalled lotus. Together with her friends, the goddess was reciting the supreme syllable, <sup>8</sup>the mantra of one syllable[^bx-2] – that is, great mistress, the supreme syllable − for Mahāvidyā Kālikā is the cherished goddess of Padminī.
‘Seeing this, mighty-armed Vāsudeva was amazed.

*‘Padminī said:* <sup>9</sup>“Quickly go to Vraja, mighty-armed one, to the clan of the Yādavas, and I will engage in the Clan behaviour with you.”

*‘Vāsudeva said:* <sup>10</sup>“Listen to me, Padminī, when will I see you? Please also tell me, mistress of the gods, what kind of recitation I should do.”

*‘Padminī replied:* <sup>11</sup>“Lord of god of gods, I will take birth before you in Gokula, in the Seat of Mathurā, in the house of Vṛkabhānu.[^ca-2] <sup>12</sup>You will experience no suffering by intercourse with me, mighty-armed one, and whatever undecaying material for the Clan behaviour that you will need, Kṛṣṇa, Lord of gods, will always be there in the garland and nowhere else.”
<sup>13</sup>‘When she had said this, Padminī, Tripurāsundarī’s *dūtī*, suddenly disappeared into the garland, <sup>14</sup>and when Vāsudeva saw this, he left the delightful city of Kāśī, the great and unparalleled Seat, and quickly went to the ocean of milk.[^cb-2] <sup>15</sup>Padminī, the greatest goddess, went to the Seat of Mathurā, where Kātyāyanī Durgā is a form of Mahāmāyā. <sup>16</sup>Worshipped and praised by Nārada and other great sages, Kātyāyanī Mahāmāyā stays in the waters of the Yamunā. <sup>17</sup>There, the water of the Yamunā is a direct form of Kālī, it is delightful and full of lotuses, white, yellow and greatly shining, <sup>18</sup>red, black, green, variegated and enchanting all. Great mistress, where supreme Kātyāyanī is called Kālindī,[^cc-2] <sup>19</sup>there mother Kālikā, praised by great sages, eternally resides as Kālindī, to benefit the worlds.
<sup>20</sup>‘The circle of Mathurā is situated in the middle of a thousand-petalled lotus. Great mistress, this is the lotus that always stays in your hairband. <sup>21</sup>In the middle of the lotus is this enchanting Seat of the hair; in your hair-band, great mistress, is Vraja, the circle of Mathurā. <sup>22</sup>Goddess, Vraja Vṛndāvana is where Kātyāyanī Māyā, Mahāmāyā, the world itself, with all her powers stays. <sup>23</sup>Greatest mistress, by just a part of her, Śakti remains the witness, but without Śakti, great mistress, Brahman is like a dead corpse.’

[p. 254]

## Seventh Paṭala

*The goddess said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Great god, after Padminī went to Vraja, what then did she do? In whose house was this supreme Padminī born? <sup>2</sup>Tell me all of this in detail, greatest master Śaṅkara, for if you do not tell me, I will give up my body!’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>3</sup>‘Dear one, goddess Padminī, fragrant as a lotus, appeared before Kṛṣṇa in the house of Vṛkabhānu. <sup>4</sup>In the month of Caitra, on the ninth lunar day of the bright fortnight, when the moon was in the Puṣya asterism,[^cd-2] Padminī appeared within an egg of Māyā on the waves of the Kālindī, surrounded by various lotus flowers. <sup>5</sup>Having made an egg in the middle of the flowers, Padminī entered into it; an egg endowed with Māyā, shining like millions of moons. <sup>6</sup>Padminī, the best of lovers, having the colour of an asparagus flower, appeared among the lotuses at midnight on the ninth lunar day, when the moon was in the Puṣya asterism, <sup>7</sup>in a lotus shining like a reddish sun.
‘Goddess, Vṛkabhānu’s village lay on the bank of the Kālindī. <sup>8</sup>It was called Padmapura, and it was delightful, awarding the four goals of life. <sup>9</sup>Great mistress, the light from this egg was like that from a thousand suns; greatest mistress, it dispelled all darkness in an instant.
‘The noble Vṛkabhānu was seated on the bank of the Kālindī, <sup>10</sup>intently and incessantly reciting the Mahāvidyā of Mahākālī. At that moment, Mahāmāyā Kātyāyanī appeared.

*‘Kātyāyanī said:* <sup>11</sup>“Listen, mighty-armed son, glorious Vṛkabhānu! You have attained perfection. Now ask for a boon, best among men!”

*‘Vṛkabhānu replied:* <sup>12</sup>By your mercy, goddess, mistress of heaven, I am always perfected; by your mercy, Mahāmāyā, I become like a liberated one. <sup>13</sup>By your mercy, nothing remains for me to attain on earth. Give me a daughter similar to yourself!”
<sup>14</sup>‘Listen, great mistress, beautiful woman, to the nectarlike words that Kātyāyanī spoke to Vṛkabhānu <sup>15</sup>in a voice deep like thunder when she heard this!
‘“Vaiśya, beautiful one, I am pleased with the devotion of your wife, <sup>16</sup>and this request of yours accords well to your wife.”
‘Greatest mistress, after Mahāmāyā, the world itself, had said this, <sup>17</sup>she gave him the enchanting egg. Noble Vṛkabhānu immediately went into his house. <sup>18</sup>His wife, of wide eyes and enchantingly wide hips, had lit a jewelled lamp [p. 255] and was resting on a jewelled couch. <sup>19</sup>Greatest mistress, Vṛkabhānu then placed the enchanting egg into her hand, and when she saw it, she was most astonished. <sup>20</sup>She held the egg in her hand and looked at it again and again.
‘The egg had all kinds of fragrances and was endowed with all powers. <sup>21</sup>Suddenly this egg of all kinds of luminance split into two and there she saw a great girl, Padminī, enchanter of Kṛṣṇa, <sup>22</sup>with slender arms like red bolts of lightning, increasing all fortune. Greatest mistress, when she saw her, she became completely astonished.

*‘Kīrtidā said:* <sup>23</sup>“O mother in the form of Padminī! Withdraw, withdraw this form!” Greatest mistress, immediately the goddess then withdrew that form and took on an ordinary form. <sup>24</sup>Kīrtidā then observed this form of her, coloured like an asparagus flower and shining like a red bolt of lightning.

*‘The girl said:* <sup>25</sup>“Hey beautiful mother Kīrtida, fortunate one! Give me your breast, give me your breast, so that I can drink milk! I have become your daughter.”
<sup>26</sup>‘Lotus-eyed daughter of the mountain! When she heard these words of Padminī, she gave her breast to Padminī. <sup>27</sup>Daughter of the mountain, he<sup>‡ǁ</sup> then gave a name to Padminī. “Sweetly smiling one, since this goddess gives out (*dhā*) the luster of a red bolt of lightning (*rakta-*), she will be celebrated as Rādhikā in all the worlds!”

*Īśvara continued:* <sup>28</sup>‘Dear one, day by day this resident of Vraja then grew in the house of Vṛkabhānu, in the Seat of Mathurā. Then, lotus-eyed one, in the month of Bhadrapada, Kṛṣṇa appeared.’

## Eight Paṭala

*Īśvara said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Listen, lotus-eyed one, to the secret of Padminī! Greatest mistress, when she attained her second year, <sup>2</sup>she earnestly worshipped the Śivaliṅga, mistress of the gods, and recited the supreme *vidyā* of Kālī, the enchantress of the world. <sup>3</sup>My dear, she worshipped with various flowers and enchanting fragrances, she worshipped the greatest mistress with all kinds of fruits.

*‘Padminī said:* <sup>4</sup>“Kātyāyanī Mahāmāyā, great *yoginī* and supreme mistress! Mahāmāyā, give, give, please award me perfection in the *vidyā*! <sup>5</sup>Mother of Vāsudeva, give me perfection! I bow to you. Without you Brahman is perpetually silent and inert. <sup>6</sup>You are Kṛṣṇa’s form – Kṛṣṇa eternally consists [p. 256] of light – for without a form, mother, Brahman is like a dead corpse. <sup>7</sup>Therefore, Mahāmāyā, you are the supreme cause of Brahman.”
‘Great mistress, when she had incessantly prayed in this way to the greatest mistress, <sup>8</sup>she worshipped with the highest devotion and performed one hundred thousand recitations in her mind. Great mistress, in the proximity of Kātyāyanī she then attained a boon.

*‘Kātyāyanī said:* <sup>9</sup>“Listen to my words, Padminī! You will soon attain Keśava.” Having said this, greatest mistress, she then disappeared. <sup>10</sup>Kātyāyanī Mahāmāyā is always the mistress of Vṛndāvana; without Kātyāyanī, Mathurā becomes useless.
<sup>11</sup>‘Dear one, at all times surrounded by her friends, Rādhā then grew up in Vṛkabhānu’s village, like the moon in the bright fortnight. <sup>12</sup>Pārvatī! Richly decorated with fine garments and all kinds of ornaments and with trembling, timid eyes, she was just like Lakṣmī herself. <sup>13</sup>‘Padminī of supreme beauty once went into a frightening thicket. I will tell you this secret, mysterious and supremely wonderful!
<sup>14</sup>‘Greatest mistress, she who is Rādhā is the greatest goddess Padminī. She entered into a lotus forest and always remained there. <sup>15</sup>There she saw another form similar to herself and created another Rādhā, looking just like herself. <sup>16</sup>It was this replica Rādhā who then always stayed in Vṛkabhānu’s house, for Padminī is not born from a womb; she is the supreme syllable. <sup>17</sup>Listen now, great mistress, to the story of the replica!
‘Beautiful one, when she turned five, Noble Vṛkabhānu carefully arranged for her marriage. <sup>18</sup>Now carefully listen to the lineage of both, the lineage of her father-in-law and that of Vṛkabhānu![^cx-2]

*Īśvara continued:* <sup>19</sup>‘Her mother-in-law is called Jaṭilā, he who thinks himself her husband is little Abhimanyu,[^da-2] her sister-in-law has the name Kuṭilā and her brother-in-law is called Durmada.
<sup>20</sup>‘Her *tilaka*[^db-2] is called Smaramantra; her necklace, Harimanohara; her jewelled earrings, Rocana; her nose-pearl, Prabhākarī; <sup>21</sup>her parasol, Kṛṣṇapratichāya; her locket, Madana. Another name for her Syamantaka jewel is Śaṅkhacūḍaśiromaṇi. <sup>22</sup>By its lustre, a jewel by the name Saubhāgya [p. 257] puts the sun and moon to shame. Her golden bracelets are called Caṭakārādhā;[^dc-2] her upper bracelets, Maṇikarbura; <sup>23</sup>her signet ring, my goddess, Vipākṣāripumardinī; her girdle, Kāñcanacitrāṅgī; her anklets, Citragopura − <sup>24</sup>their jingling sweetness stuns Madhusūdana.[^dd-2] Her clothing is called Meghasvara and then Kuruvindanibha. <sup>25</sup>The first is the colour of a thundercloud and dear to herself; the second red and dear to Hari. Her jewel-studded mirror is Sudhāṁśudarpaharaṇa; <sup>26</sup>her golden hairpin is Narmadā; her comb is called Svastikā; her garden, decorated with flowers, is called Kandarpakuharī; <sup>27</sup>her golden jasmine, Taḍidvallī; her pond is known by her own name,<sup>§ǁ</sup> and by the bank of it, by the platform under a Kadamba tree, is a place for confidential talks. <sup>28</sup>The rāgas Mallāra and Dhanaḥśrī delight her mind; her favourite dance is Chālikya;[^dx-2] most dear of lutes is to her the Rudravallakī.[^a-3]
<sup>29</sup>‘Now, a description of her eternal friends, led by lovely Candrāvalī! Gandharvā friends are Kalākaṇṭhī, Sukaṇṭhī, Pikakaṇṭhikā, <sup>30</sup>Kalāvatī, Rasollāsā, Guṇavatī and others, who sing songs written by Viśākhā for the pleasure of Hari. <sup>31</sup>Mānikyā, Narmadā, Premavatī and Kusumapeṣalā play flutes, lutes, drums and cymbals. <sup>32</sup>The girls Divā and Kīrti, Sugandhā and Nalinī, Mañjiṣṭhā and Raṅgavatī, the daughters of Rajaka, <sup>33</sup>Pālindhī, Sairindhrī, Vṛndā, Kundalatā, Dhaniṣṭhā, Guṇavatī and others live in the house of the king of the cowherds.[^b-3]
<sup>34</sup>‘Kāmadā, her foster-sister, has a special kind of friendship to her. Lavaṅgamañjarī, Rāgamañjarī, Guṇamañjarī, <sup>35</sup>Śubhānumatī, Anupamā, Supriyā, Ratimañjarī, Rāgalekhā, Kalākelī, Bhūridā and others are heroines.[^c-3] <sup>36</sup>Nandīmukhī, Bindumatī and others arrange for meetings. Śyāmalā, Maṅgalā and others are known to be on her side.[^d-3]
<sup>37</sup>‘The best in the opposing sides are Rādhā and Candrāvalī, and they have in their groups of millions of fawn-eyed women, <sup>38</sup>but because of her complete sweetness, Śrī Rādhā, the *dūtī* of Tripurā, beloved of the primeval Puruṣa, is supreme. [p. 258] <sup>39</sup>‘The son of the king of cowherds, foremost for his incomparable, supreme sweetness, <sup>40</sup>is billions times billions times more dear to her than her own life. Better than millions of mothers, again, is the wife of the king of the cowherds.
<sup>41</sup>‘Her father, Vṛṣabhānu, is greater than the moon or the sun in Taurus (Vṛṣabhānu). Her aging mother Kīrtidā is famous also in the world as Ratnagarbhā.
<sup>42</sup>‘Her worshippable Lord is the eye of world and the friend of the lotus.<sup>ǁ</sup> The mantra that she recites to fulfill her wishes is the great mantra of Kātyāyanī. <sup>43</sup>The venerable Paurṇamāsī increases all of her fortune.
‘Her paternal grandfather is Mahībhānu and her maternal grandfather is Bindu. <sup>44</sup>Her maternal and paternal grandmothers are known as Mukharā and Sukhadā. Ratnabhānu, Svabhānu and Bhānu are her father’s brothers, <sup>45</sup>and Bhadrakīrti, Mahākīrti and Kīrticandra are her maternal uncles. Kīrtimatī is her mother’s sister and Bhānumudrā is her father’s sister. <sup>46</sup>Her paternal aunt’s husband is Kāśya and her maternal aunt’s husband is Kṛśa. The wives of her maternal uncles are Menakā, Menā, Ṣaṣṭhī, Dhātrī and Dhātukī. <sup>47</sup>Her older brother is Śrīdāman and her younger sister is Anaṅgamañjarī.
‘Her supremely dear friends are Lalitā, Viśākhikā, <sup>48</sup>Vicitrā, Campakalatā, Raṅgadevī, Sudevikā, Tuṅgavidyā and Aṅgalekhā – this famous group of eight.
<sup>49</sup>‘Her dear friends are Kuraṅgākṣī, Maṇḍalī, Mānakuṇḍalā, Mālatī, Candralatikā, Mādhavī, Madanālasā, <sup>50</sup>Mañju medhā, Śaśikalā, Sumadhyā, Mahurekṣaṇā, Kamalā, Kāma latikā, Kāntacūḍā, Varāṅganā, <sup>51</sup>Madhurī, Candrikā, Prema-mañjarī, Tanumadhyamā, Kandarpa sundarī, Mañjukeśī and others by the millions.
<sup>52</sup>‘Some of her life friends are Lāsikā, Kelisundarī, Kādam barī, Śaśimukhī, Candrarekhā, Priyamvadā, <sup>53</sup>Madon mādā, Madhumatī, Vāsantī, Kalabhāṣiṇī, Ratnaveṇī, Mānavatī, Karpūratilakā and others.
<sup>54</sup>‘All of these look almost like the mistress of Vṛndāvana.[^x-3]
‘Her eternal friends are Kastūrī, Manojñā, Maṇimañjarī, Sindūrā, Candanavatī, Kaumudī, Mudirā and others. They move around in the forests and elsewhere for the purpose of her play, like the moon with its digits.[^ba-3] [p. 259] <sup>56</sup>‘Now the great wonder of his beloveds are eulogised, for they are adorned with an excess of the loveliness of supreme love, greater than even Lakṣmī’s: <sup>57</sup>Candrāvalī, Padmā, Śyāmā, Śaibyā, Bhadrikā, Tārā, Citrā, Gandharvī, Pālikā, Candraśālikā, <sup>58</sup>Maṅgalā, Vimalā, Nīlā, Bhavanākṣī, Manoramā, Kampalatā, Mañjubhāṣiṇī, Kañjanekṣaṇā, <sup>59</sup>Kumudā, Kairavī, Pārī, Śāradākṣī, Viśāradā, Śaṅkarī, Kusumā, Kṛṣṇā, Śārāṅgī, Drāviṇī, Śivā, <sup>60</sup>Tārāvalī, Guṇavatī, Sumukhī, Kelimañjarī, Hārāvalī, Cakorākṣī, Bhāratī and Kāminī. <sup>61</sup>These lovely-browed maidens have hundreds of other groups. Divided up into one group after the other, the amount of these beautiful women number in the hundreds of thousands. <sup>62</sup>Principal among all these groups, however, beloved and full of excellent qualities, are Rādhā, Candrāvalī, Bhadrā, Śyāmalā, Pālikā and others.
<sup>63</sup>‘Sweetly smiling one, she who is known by her birth name was born on the ninth lunar day of the bright fortnight of the month of Caitra, when the moon was in the Puṣya asterism. This Rādhā, great mistress, is none other than Prakṛti Padminī. <sup>64</sup>Among them, great mistress, Kṛṣṇa himself enjoyed, for Vāsudeva’s enjoyment is the cause of his perfection of mantra.’

<sup>65</sup>*Devī said:* ‘Lord, best of ascetics, master! Please tell me in detail why this Padminī Rādhā always remained in that lotus forest, why this resident of Vṛndāvana gave up her father and mother, created another form similar to herself, <sup>66</sup>master of gods, entered into a lotus and always remained there. Please reveal this secret, Lord and great master!’

## Ninth Paṭala

*Īśvara said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Fawn-eyed woman, Rādhā is Padminī, dear to Viṣṇu. Tripurā, the greatest mistress, is Mahāmāyā Jagaddhātrī. <sup>2</sup>Padminī, fragrant as a lotus, is her *dūtī*, and because of her firm devotion to Viṣṇu, Padminī is dear to him. <sup>3</sup>Sweetly smiling one, great mistress, Vṛkabhānu’s devotion is firm; Padminī of the fragrant garland became his daughter, <sup>4</sup>but after she had suckled the breast, goddess, she created another Rādhā and entered into a thicket of lotuses in the middle of the waters of the Yamunā. <sup>5</sup>In that secluded place she recited the great mantra of Mahākālī; another, Candrāvalī Rādhā, stayed in the house of Vṛkabhānu.[^bb-3]
<sup>6</sup>‘Lotus-eyed goddess, greatest mistress! All of the previously mentioned companions of Padminī were created by Padminī, not by anyone else. <sup>7</sup>Rādhikā should be understood as having two forms: Candrāvalī and Padminī. Greatest mistress, sweetly smiling one, the sun or the moon does not see her, <sup>8</sup>greatest mistress, let alone wretched humans! Padminī concealed herself and took shelter within a lotus, for Padminī, great mistress, is the assistant of Tripurā.’

[p. 260]

## Tenth Paṭala

*Īśvara said:* <sup>1</sup>‘And now, great mistress, I will tell you the supreme deeds of Vāsudeva, most wonderful, a tonic in the world of men. <sup>2</sup>Listen carefully, handsome woman! When one has heard the story of Viṣṇu, greatest mistress, one will find no pleasure in other topics, for one will find no pleasure in anything else, great mistress.’

*Īśvara continued:* <sup>3</sup>‘Great mistress, goddess, under the pretense of removing the burden of the earth, he appeared in Mathurā, in the circle of Vraja. <sup>4</sup>Greatest mistress, Mathurā is Mahāmāyā, the world itself; handsome woman, Mathurā, the circle of Vraja, is the Seat of the hair. <sup>5</sup>In Mathurā, the circle of Vraja, does Candrāvalī, Mahāmāyā and lotus-eyed Rādhā eternally stay.[^bc-3] <sup>6</sup>Goddess! Mathurā, the circle of Vraja, is full of all powers; it is exceptionally sweet, peaceful, lovely and charming. <sup>7</sup>In Mathurā, the circle of Vraja, among these people, Mahāmāyā, Rādhā and Candrāvalī appeared, my dear; <sup>8</sup>among the houses of Mathurā; elsewhere they are rarely found, goddess.
‘Greatest mistress, sweetly smiling one, what is impossible in Mathurā, the circle of Vraja, in this Seat of Mathurā, <sup>9</sup>full of all powers, greatest mistress, where the supreme Kātyāyanī herself resides? <sup>10</sup>Great mistress, in each and every house spring and all the seasons bring happiness to Mathurā with fragrances of many kinds. <sup>11</sup>Great mistress, what is impossible in Mathurā, the circle of Vraja? In Mathurā, Mahāmāyā <sup>12</sup>has appeared with eight arms to protect Kṛṣṇa, by your power, sweetly smiling one.
‘Great mistress, lotus-eyed Viṣṇu appeared, <sup>13</sup>and after Mahāviṣṇu, great mistress, lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa, had appeared from Vasudeva and the cage of Devakī’s womb, <sup>14</sup>Mahāmāyā appeared from the cage of Yaśodā’s womb.[^bd-3] This great tale is narrated in the Mahābhārata; <sup>15</sup>it is all told by Vyāsa, and Vyāsa is perpetually my body, for, greatest mistress, Vyāsa always bears my body.
<sup>16</sup>‘My dear, fair-complexioned one! At midnight on the eight lunar day of the dark fortnight of the month of Bhadra, when the moon was connected with the Rohiṇī asterism, Hari appeared. <sup>17</sup>As Viṣṇu, so also Māyā appeared, handsome woman; Mahāmāyā, the goddess who keeps misfortune away from Kṛṣṇa.’

*Īśvara continued:* <sup>18</sup>‘My dear, Hari is always beyond the *guṇas*; he has no body. Greatest mistress, it is the greatest mistress Prakṛti who is the body. <sup>19</sup>Lord Hari consisting of light took on a form made of Māyā;<sup>†ǁ</sup> with four arms, eyes like blooming lotuses, <sup>20</sup>with ornaments resembling *makaras*[^bx-3] in his ears, with the Śrīvatsa and Kaustubha shining like lightning on his chest, [p. 261] <sup>21</sup>the Lord dressed in yellow garments, with a skin as smooth as powdered eye ointment, with a smiling face like the autumnal moon, who carries the conch, disk and so on, <sup>22</sup>the Lord decorated with a garland, who always has four arms, with bells at his waist and hips beautiful and delightful, <sup>23</sup>extremely beautiful with bracelets and armlets of different types, my dear – and, great mistress, the delightful garland that Tripurā gave – <sup>24</sup>when Kṛṣṇa, higher than the highest, had taken on such a form of Māyā, lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa appeared from the cage of Devakī’s womb in the house of Vasudeva.
<sup>25</sup>‘In this way, Kṛṣṇa, who is subtle, supreme and imperishable, took on a form of Śabda.[^ca-3] <sup>26</sup>Therefore, great mistress, Hari is always Śabdabrahman. When it comes to cause and effect, he is always accompanied by Mahāmāyā,
<sup>27</sup>for there is no cause and effect in the lotus-eyes Lord;[^cb-3] Mahāmāyā, the world itself, is cause and also effect.
<sup>28</sup>‘Having taken shelter of this form of Māyā, Hari himself appeared, and when they saw this wonderful body, they were amazed; great mistress, his father and mother were wonderfully amazed!’[^cc-3]

<sup>29</sup>*‘Vasudeva said:* “Obeisance to you, Lord Kṛṣṇa of undecaying understanding! Mighty-armed one, greatly powerful one, quickly withdraw this form!”
<sup>30</sup>‘Pārvatī, when he heard these words of Vasudeva, he took on a form of Prakṛti, one that could deceive the world of men; <sup>31</sup>for, great and beautiful mistress, this form of Prakṛti is that of the supreme Prakṛti Māyā that pervades the universe.
<sup>32</sup>‘Once Kṛṣṇa of eyes like lotus petals had taken on this body of Prakṛti, this lotus-eyed one performed day by day the acts of intelligent Hari that pertained to his infancy, boyhood and adolescence. <sup>33</sup>I will now tell you a secret most confidential, the essence of all essences, higher than the highest! Listen carefully.’

<sup>34</sup>*Devī said:* ‘Highest, ancient lord! Kṛṣṇa’s form has all kinds of characteristics and all kinds of signs. Highest master, Śaṅkara, tell me all about this, in detail!’ [p. 262] <sup>35</sup>*Īśvara replied:* ‘A line going upwards, a barleycorn, a disk, a parasol, a lotus, a flag, a hook, a bolt of lightning, an eight-pointed star, four svastikas <sup>36</sup>and five rose apple fruits are there under the right foot of Hari. A conch, the sky, a rainbow, a cow’s footprint, a triangle, <sup>37</sup>three half-moons, a waterpot, four rose apple fruits and a fish at the bottom of his foot – these are the thirty-two secondary signs.
<sup>38</sup>‘Now listen to another indication of a form of Brahman, sweet-limbed one, mistress of the gods! Kṛṣṇa’s body has all the different powers. <sup>39</sup>A barley-corn, a disk, a flower garland, a supremely beautiful bracelet, a half-moon within a garland, a lotus, a flag, <sup>40</sup>a line going upwards in the middle of the foot, a hook in the lotus-like foot, and at the right, great mistress, a conch and a fish under both feet. <sup>41</sup>And then, a great sacrificial altar with a bosom, a chariot and a mountain over the fish, a pair of earrings, an excellent spear <sup>42</sup>and a splendid club: these are the seventeen signs, my dear.[^cd-3]
‘Such are the various and wonderful signs, my dear. <sup>43</sup>These signs, great mistress, possess all the different powers. This body is made of different kinds of light; it is the principal, highest Prakṛti, <sup>44</sup>for light, greatest mistress, is a form of eternal Prakṛti.
‘Thus, my dear, is it marked by different kinds of signs of Śakti.’

## Eleventh Paṭala

*Īśvara said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Great mistress, that which a practitioner attains after hearing to this most confidential secret called ”perplexing the world” – <sup>2</sup>when the best practitioners hear that as well, greatest mistress, they attain the eight perfections. Greatest mistress, now listen to all of that which was told in the Varāha, the primeval boar;<sup>‡ǁ</sup> I will narrate it to you, faultless one!
<sup>3</sup>‘More confidential than all that is confidential, pleasant, the cause of the highest bliss, most wonderful, the secret of all secrets, the highest auspiciousness, <sup>4</sup>most rare of all rarities, enchanting all, possessing all powers, goddess, hidden in all the Tantras, <sup>5</sup>topmost of all the places of the Sātvatas, alone dear to Kṛṣṇa – this place is called eternal Vṛndāvana, and it stands on Satī’s hair. Full of the majesty and happiness of the complete Brahman, eternal, blissful, unchanging – <sup>6</sup>Vṛndāvana is just like Vaikuṇṭha itself on earth. Whatever majesty can be found in Vaikuṇṭha, all of that is placed in Gokula. <sup>7</sup>Goddess, the grandeur of Vaikuṇṭha is revealed in Dvārakā, but that which has the power of Brahman has always taken shelter of Vṛndāvana. [p. 263] <sup>8</sup>Great mistress! In Jambudvīpa, India enchants Viṣṇu, and particularly so the land of Mathurā in his community, in the middle of Vṛndāvana.
<sup>9</sup>‘This land of Mathurā is a great abode, exclusively dear to Kṛṣṇa. The circle of Mathurā is his own abode, nothing less than exceedingly dear. <sup>10</sup>The circle of Mathurā is hidden, various, delightful, situated with the town in the middle,[^cx-3] appearing as if in the middle of a lotus with a thousand petals. <sup>11</sup>This blessed, wonderful abode of Viṣṇu is above the disk of Śakti.[^da-3] The secret description of the petals of the lotus has been given step-by-step.
<sup>12</sup>‘The greatness of the primary twelve forests has been narrated, step-by-step: Bhadra-, Śrī-, Lauha-, Bhaṇḍīra-, Mahā-, Tāla-, Khadiraka-, <sup>13</sup>Bahulā-, Kumuda-, Kāmya-, Madhu- and Vṛndāvana. Listen, supreme beauty, and I will now narrate their particulars. <sup>14</sup>‘Bhadra is a form of Tapinī and Śrīvana is Tāpinī. Lauhavana is auspicious Dhūmrā, my dear, and Bhaṇḍīra is the topmost Marīci. <sup>15</sup>Mahātālavana, my dear, is the highest part of Jvālinī, but the most beautiful Khadiravana, my dear, is Ruci. <sup>16</sup>Bahulā is Suṣumnā, my dear, Kumuda is Bhogadā; Madhuvana is Viśvā and Vṛndā is Dharaṇī.
<sup>17</sup>Goddess! Kāmya is Mālinī and Mahadvana is Kṣamā.[^db-3] ‘Of these twelve principal forests, seven are on the western side of the Kālindī. <sup>18</sup>Five are on the eastern side. Goddess! In this regard there is a great secret: Mahāvana is also known as Gokula and Madhuvana is Mathurā. <sup>19</sup>In the east are the five starting with Bhadra; in the west are the seven beginning with Tāla.
‘Dear one, there are also the secondary forests, places of Kṛṣṇa’s adventures of *rasa*! <sup>20</sup>Kadambakhaṇḍika, Nanda’s forest, Nandīśvara, Nandānandakhaṇḍa, Palāśaśokaketakī, <sup>21</sup>Sugandhimodana, Kaula,[^dc-3] nectarean Bhojanasthala, Sukhaprasādana, Vatsaharaṇa, Śeṣaśayika, <sup>22</sup>Śyāmapūrya, Dadhigrāma, Vṛkabhānu’s town, Saṅketa, Dvipada, Rāsa krīḍa, Dhūṣara, <sup>23</sup>Kemudrama and new Sarovīna, the best place of longing.
‘In this manner, the divisions of this forest are counted as thirty-two; it is known as practice.[^dd-3] <sup>24</sup>The twelve forests that I mentioned first are the [p. 264] principal, supreme forests, but above them is the forest that is known as the fourth.<sup>§ǁ</sup> <sup>25</sup>The secret of this place, full of different kinds of play of *rasa* and different kinds of adventures, spread out on the filaments of the petals, has been narrated, one after the other. <sup>26</sup>Sweetly smiling one, this thousand-petalled lotus is known as Gokula, and its pericarp is his great abode, Kṛṣṇa’s peerless place.’

## Twelfth Paṭala

*Īśvara said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Above that, on a golden seat, decorated with jewelled pavillions, are the petals. I will describe them clockwise around the pericarp. <sup>2</sup>The petals in the South, etc – the cardinal and intermediate directions – are described, one after the other.
‘The petal that is in the South, my dear, is said to be supremely confidential. <sup>3</sup>There is the great seat of the Rāsa dance, beautifully present in the Nigama and Āgama,[^dx-3] difficult to reach even for the masters of Yoga; certainly beyond the range of men.
<sup>4</sup>‘My dear, the first and the second petals are secret; on these petals called Dhīrasamīra you will find two cottages in small groves.
<sup>5</sup>‘The third petal is in the East; this is where Keśin was killed.[^a-4] Here Gaṅgā and all the holy places eternally stay with their companions.
<sup>6</sup>‘The fourth petal is in the Northeast; it awards the fruit of perfect Seats. There the cowherdess achieved Kṛṣṇa as her husband through worshipping Kātyāyanī. <sup>7</sup>On this petal it is also said that the stealing of the clothes and ornaments took place.[^b-4]
‘The petal that is called the fifth is in the North; it is the best of all petals. <sup>8</sup>There are the twelve Ādityas; this petal is like the pericarp itself.
‘The sixth petal is in the Northwest; it is known as the good lake of Kālī.[^c-4] <sup>9</sup>Goddess! It is the best of the best petals and said to be the principal petal. [p. 265] ‘The seventh petal is in the West; it is the best petal, highest of all. <sup>10</sup>There the wives of the Brahmins attained the boons they desired.[^d-4] My dear, on this petal Aghāsura also attained liberation.<sup>ǁ</sup> <sup>11</sup>On this petal, around the lake of Brahmā, Brahmā was bewildered.[^x-4]
‘The eighth petal is in the Southwest; here Vyoma was slain.[^ba-4] <sup>12</sup>Here Śaṅkhācūḍa was killed.[^bc-4] This is a petal of all kinds of adventures of *rasa*.
‘These eight petals are all situated within the Vṛndā forest. <sup>13</sup>Delightful and blessed Vṛndāvana is on the left side of the Yamunā. The Protector[^bb-4] there is Śambhu and his Liṅga is called Gopīśvara.
<sup>14</sup>‘Now, in order, I will explain the greatness of the sixteen petals outside of this. I will describe it in a clockwise order, beginning from the Southwest. <sup>15</sup>These sixteen principal petals are a great station, a great abode.
‘The first petal is the greatest; its greatness is like that of the pericarp. <sup>16</sup>On this petal lies Madhuvana; there Hari appeared. The Protector there, on this supremely great among all great petals, <sup>17</sup>is the worshippable primeval Lord Keśava, the Lord beyond the three *guṇas*, the four-armed Viṣṇu, the cause of all causes.[^bd-4] <sup>18</sup>The Lord of the Land is there Lord Bhūteśvara, the great master.
‘The second petal is said to be the abode of some adventures of *rasa*. <sup>19</sup>This petal is also called Khadira; it is said to be the best of all petals. Its greatness is like that of the pericarp. <sup>20</sup>There, you who are dear to Lakṣmī, on the Govardhana hill, you will find eternal delight.
‘The third petal, my dear, is supreme great among all great petals, <sup>21</sup>for there is Govardhana itself, whose eternal master is Hari.<sup>†ǁ</sup>
‘The fourth petal is said to be a place of greatly wonderful *rasa*. <sup>22</sup>There is Kadambakhaṇḍī, an abode of completely blissful *rasa*. This petal is also called sweet, lovely, dear and delightful. [p. 266] <sup>23</sup>‘Nandīśvara is the best petal; there is Nanda’s house, my dear. It is called the fifth petal and its greatness is like that of the pericarp.
<sup>24</sup>‘The sixth petal, which is undecaying, is known as Nandavana. Its Protector is Gopāla, devoted to herding the cows.
<sup>25</sup>‘The seventh, delightful petal is called the Bahula forest.
‘The eighth petal is Tālavana; there it is said the killing of the Ass took place.[^bx-4]
<sup>26</sup>‘Sweetly smiling one! The ninth, delightful petal is the Kumuda forest.
‘The Kāmya forest is a lovely petal, the principal cause of all. <sup>27</sup>This petal is the place where Brahmā was surrounded by innumerable Viṣṇus.[^ca-4] This place of the *rasa* of Kṛṣṇa’s play is called the tenth petal.
<sup>28</sup>‘The eleventh petal is said to promote the benefits of the devotees. Here, in this place of *rasa* of various jewels, the bridge was built.[^cb-4]
<sup>29</sup>‘Delightful and enchanting Bhāṇḍīravana is the twelfth petal. There Kṛṣṇa engaged in the *rasa* of play together with Sudāman and others.
<sup>30</sup>‘The thirteenth petal is the best; there it is said Bhadravana lies.
‘The fourteenth petal is said to be the place which affords all perfections. <sup>31</sup>There lies Śrīvana, splendid and the cause of all majesty. This petal is full of Kṛṣṇa’s play, and it increases fortune, beauty and fame.
<sup>32</sup>‘The fifteenth petal is the best; there lies beautiful Lauhavana.
‘It is said that the greatness of the sixteenth petal is the same as that of the pericarp. <sup>33</sup>This petal is known as Mahāvana; there lies a wonderful secret. There one finds the *rasa* of childhood adventures together with the calves and children. <sup>34</sup>There Pūtanā was killed[^cc-4] and the two Arjuna trees broken.[^cd-4] The Protector there is the child Gopāla, five years old, <sup>35</sup>also known by the name Dāmodara, an ocean of the *rasa* of the bliss of love.<sup>‡ǁ</sup> This celebrated petal is known as supremely great among all great petals. <sup>36</sup>There one finds the *rasa* of Kṛṣṇa’s play; it is known as the petal of delight, and that forest is also Siddhapradhānakiñjalka.’

*Pārvatī said:* <sup>37</sup>‘Master, tell me all − the greatness or the wonderful secret of Vṛndāvana, its *rasa* of love and its bliss!’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>38</sup>‘Where even the Vṛndā and other plants shed tears of bliss of love, what can one say about the conscious devotees of Viṣṇu? <sup>39</sup>I have told [p. 267] you this dearest secret of secrets, more confidential than the confidential, rarest of the rare! <sup>40</sup>Goddess, this enchanting Seat of the hair has been hidden in India; it is a place that Brahmā and the others long for, that the gods and Gandharvas serve. <sup>41</sup>My dear, it contains the fifty Mātṛkās, it is full of eternal bliss. Where Kātyāyanī Māyā, Mahāmāyā, the world itself dwells, <sup>42</sup>what is impossible in such a place, great mistress?
‘Great mistress, my dear, the creepers and roots are called Vṛndā; great mistress, <sup>43</sup>the creepers and roots are the highest Kātyāyanī herself. Therefore, great mistress, it is praised by the masters of yoga and others; <sup>44</sup>it is filled with the unceasing song and dance of the Apsarasas and Gandharvas.
‘Blessed Vṛndāvana is delightful, full of the *rasa* of complete bliss. <sup>45</sup>There the soil is thought-gems;[^cx-4] water, nectar full of *rasa*; <sup>46</sup>and the trees fulfill all wishes. It is full of Surabhī cows.
‘But, greatest mistress, *complete*[^da-4] means that it has all fifty parts;[^db-4] <sup>47</sup>*bliss*, mistress of gods, refers to Prakṛti, the greatest mistress. That which is *soil* is the earth; <sup>48</sup>the *water* or *rasa* is the highest Prakṛti herself. The *trees* are Prakṛti Māyā and the *Surabhī* is Caṇḍikā herself.
<sup>49</sup>‘Woman is Lakṣmī, man is Viṣṇu, born from a portion of his portion.
‘But, greatest mistress, it is the eldest Śakti that is known as *Viṣṇu*. <sup>50</sup>And the *portions*, greatest mistress, are parts of Prakṛti.
‘There, the age is that of adolescence, an eternal, blissful form. <sup>51</sup>Walk is dance, talk is song, an eternally smiling face! The people who have taken shelter of this forest are made of pure *sattva* and full of love. <sup>52</sup>They have sunk into the happiness of the complete Brahman, engrossed in the flashing forth of his form.
‘Lotus-eyed one! *Walk, the smiling face*, the *pure sattva* and all of that is always a form of Prakṛti.
‘It is enchantingly filled with the sounds of exited cuckoos, bees and others, <sup>54</sup>the song of doves and parrots, thousands of black bees and the dance of peacocks, intensifying the happiness of their lovers, <sup>55</sup>and strewn all over with the pollen from flowers of all kinds of colours.
‘Great mistress! Happiness and distress is Prakṛti, the greatest goddess. <sup>56</sup>Greatest mistress, the *cuckoos*, the *bees* making honey from flowers and whatever was mentioned, all of that is Prakṛti, the greatest goddess. <sup>57</sup>Therefore, great mistress, she is the auspicious cause of Brahman, very tender and fragrant – and the three worlds are enveloped and enchanted by her. [p. 268] <sup>58</sup>‘It is filled with gentle breezes and attended by the winds of spring, there the full moon is always risen and only a slight part of the sun shines. <sup>59</sup>There is no suffering, no sorrow, no old age or death, no anger, no envy, no fractions, no ego. <sup>60</sup>This great abode is the *rasa* of the nectar of complete bliss, an ocean of the nectar of complete love. It is beyond the three *guṇas* and filled with complete devotees, <sup>61</sup>more confidential than the confidential, hidden in the middle of Vṛndāvana.
<sup>62</sup>‘Because it is touched by the dust from Govinda’s feet, Vṛndāvana remains eternally on earth, and just by touching it, its soil is also fortunate in India. Govinda’s imperishable place stands in the shadow of great wish-fulfilling trees. <sup>63</sup>Liberation comes by just touching it, so what can one say about its greatness? Therefore, goddess, with all of your being, fix your heart on this forest.’

## Thirteenth Paṭala

*Pārvati said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Lord, carrier of the trident! If Vṛndāvana is devoid of old age and death, without suffering, sorrow and anger, <sup>2</sup>why then, greatest master, were Pūtanā, the Vṛṣa demon, Keśin, Śaṅkhacūḍa and all the others killed there? <sup>3</sup>Why, greatest master, did Kṛṣṇa become angry? If, greatest master, the circle of Vraja always <sup>4</sup>and in all ways is the enchanting Seat of the hair, liberated, full of all powers and full of all bliss, <sup>5</sup>why then, greatest master, did calamities appear within the circle of Vraja? And why, my dear, greatest master, did the cowherdesses become lusty? <sup>6</sup>And why is Kṛṣṇa, the son of Devakī, always lusty? And how, great god, did the water of the Yamunā become filled with nectar? <sup>7</sup>Tell me, eternal Lord of gods, for you are omniscient. Lord, ocean of compassion, destroy, destroy this doubt!’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>8</sup>‘You have asked well, my dear! This secret is most wonderful. Listen to this secret, mistress of the gods, this most confidential secret! <sup>9</sup>Cause and effect can be found in the states of waking and so on: waking, dreaming, deep sleep and the fourth, supreme state. <sup>10</sup>Sweetly smiling one, the fourth is Brahmanirvāṇa, Mahāviṣṇu. Goddess, Viṣṇu is imperishable, made of light, always pure, without cause and effect, <sup>11</sup>perpetually inactive and immovable. Mistress of gods! Vāsudeva, however, always consists of pure *sattva*. <sup>12</sup>By the grace of Tripurā, he has attained the company of Padminī and taken on the form of Kṛṣṇa in a cottage of Vṛndāvana.
<sup>13</sup>‘The syllable *Kṛṣ* indicates existence and *ṇa* indicates liberation;[^dc-4] when the two are combined, one gets Hari made of pure *sattva*. <sup>14</sup>Goddess! Then only this Śabdabrahman[^dd-4] immediately comes to mind, for the Śabdabrahman, [p. 269] mistress of the gods, indicates Kṛṣṇa sheltered in the quality of *sattva*, <sup>15</sup>when the fourth, mistress of gods, comes together with Prakṛti. She is always called Prakṛti since she is said to bring forth (*prakaroti*). <sup>16</sup>The Puruṣa, however, of unchangeable form is devoid of cause and effect. Mistress of the gods, because in the city (*pur*) of the body he rests (*śete*), he is Īśvara.<sup>§ǁ</sup>
<sup>17</sup>‘Therefore, Viṣṇu, with a form of eternity, cognition and bliss, is the Puruṣa, while Prakṛti, greatest mistress, has a form of cause and effect. <sup>18</sup>Goddess! There is never cause and effect in Īśvara, but when Īśvara comes together with Prakṛti, Īśvara becomes endowed with cause and effect.
<sup>19</sup>‘Your eternal Māyā is hard to understand, greatest mistress!
‘This eternal city of Vraja has come out of your hair, goddess. <sup>20</sup>Dear one, great mistress, whatever has been said about lust, anger and so on, all of that is the greatest goddess Prakṛti. <sup>21</sup>Great mistress, Vāsudeva’s birth into the world of men of little understanding is just for the sake of attaining perfection in *vidyā*, <sup>22</sup>and, mistress of the gods, whosoever attains perfection in *vidyā*, becomes a divinity. <sup>23</sup>In the Seat of the hair in the world of men, greatest mistress, he attained the company of Padminī to perfect his Clan behaviour.’

## Fourteenth Paṭala

*Īśvara said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Vṛndāvana, the seed-vessel of the thousand-petalled lotus, is the undecaying, eternal, blissful and unchanging abode of Govinda. <sup>2</sup>It has come from Satī’s hair and is full of the happiness of complete love. His infancy, boyhood and youth are found in other forests and in other places, <sup>3</sup>but in his form as an adolescent, Kṛṣṇa stays only among the pleasure-grounds of Vṛndāvana.
‘The soft and pleasing water of the Kālindī is enchantingly fragrant: <sup>4</sup>dark, pure and deep is the water, enchantingly fragrant, full of honey mixed with the nectar of bliss, splendidly coloured by water-lilies, lotuses and other flowers, <sup>5</sup>resounding with the various sweet calls of Cakravākas and other birds. <sup>6</sup>This splendid water is delightful, and its waves are enchanting. Both of its banks are delightful and made of pure gold. <sup>7</sup>Wherever this seed-vessel touches is said to be millions of times that of the Ganges, and wherever Hari delights in his play is millions of times that of the pericarp. <sup>8</sup>The pericarp, the Kālindī and Kṛṣṇa are one body, non-different from each other – he who knows this is indeed fortunate! Goddess, I tell you this.’ [p. 270] *Devī asked:* <sup>9</sup>‘God of god, great god, Śaṅkara, you who have the bull as your banner! Tell me this secret, greatest master: who is Kṛṣṇa and who is Kālindī? <sup>10</sup>Describe the pericarp in detail, greatest master Śaṅkara; great god, master, be gracious and explain this truth.’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>11</sup>‘Kālindī is Kālikā herself, who in a coiled up form envelops Vraja and stays there, in order to show Kṛṣṇa her favour. <sup>12</sup>And Kṛṣṇa, greatest mistress, is always Prakṛti and Puruṣa. The pericarp is Mahāmāyā, the mother of the world, the world itself. <sup>13</sup>Therefore, great mistress, Viṣṇu became Kṛṣṇa; therefore, the greatest goddess Kālikā is Kālindī; <sup>14</sup>the pericarp is the eternal Kuṇḍalī, Kṛṣṇa is Hari consisting of *sattva*.
‘Great mistress, the word Kṛṣṇa indicates simply attachment to liberation; goddess, when one attains oneness that is known as Kṛṣṇa.’

*Devī asked:* <sup>15</sup>‘What is so wonderful about the beautiful, adolescent form of Govinda? I want to hear all of this, so please speak, ocean of compassion!’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>16</sup>‘In the middle of delightful Vṛndāvana, beautified with sweet Mandāra trees, adorned with the branches and twigs of these trees, *yojanas*[^dx-4] wide, <sup>17</sup>the shelter of the *rasa* of great bliss is a great abode, a great dwelling. Underneath this, in this Seat of perfection, made from Satī’s hair, <sup>18</sup>attended by swarms of bees, intoxicated by the fragrance of sprouts and flowers, is a place of seven coverings, perpetually searched for by the Śruti. <sup>19</sup>In this place is a pure golden Seat decorated with jewelled pavillions, and in the middle of that is a shining Seat of Union[^a-5] inlaid with lovely gems, <sup>20</sup>fashioned as an octagon and enchantingly lit with various lamps. <sup>21</sup>Upon that, placed on a royal throne of gold and rubies is a single red lotus of eight petals made of joy. This is Govinda’s dear abode. What can I say about its greatness?
<sup>22</sup>‘There sits blessed Govinda, attended by the cowherdesses, in the form of a boy of divine Vraja, beloved of the dear cowherdesses, <sup>23</sup>with power suitable for the king of Vraja, the only beloved of the children of Vraja, an adolescent just on the cusp of youth, whose body is decked in beautiful garments, <sup>24</sup>the light and abode of compact bliss, shining dark as ground collyrium, beginningless, original, the lord of life, cowherd Nanda’s dear son, <sup>25</sup>searched for by the Śruti, birthless, eternal, the enchanter of the minds of the cowherdesses, the highest abode, the supreme form, the two-armed lord of the cowherd maidens, <sup>26</sup>the master of Vṛndāvana and the only cause for liberation from the qualities – on him should one meditate: bright as crystals[^b-5] on a string, an enchanting abode of darkness, <sup>27</sup>soft as a row of [p. 271] newly formed rainclouds, sweet and lovely, beautiful as a blossoming blue lotus, pleasing to touch, the shelter of pleasure, <sup>28</sup>black as ground collyrium, smooth and enchantingly dark, whose hair is smooth, dark, wavy and entirely fragrant and <sup>29</sup>has an enchanting oblique lock of hair above and to the right, shines with various gems, decked with shining peacock feathers, <sup>30</sup>beautifully decorated with a bunch of sweet Mandāra flowers, sometimes sweetly crowned with a row of peacock-feathers, <sup>31</sup>at other times decorated with a diadem of rubies and various ornaments; whose face with dangling curls of hairs shines like millions of moons, <sup>32</sup>with a *tilaka* of musk honoured with glittering sweet *gorocana;*[^c-5] whose petal-like eyes are very soft and wide like blue lotuses, <sup>33</sup>completely smiling and glancing to the sides with raised, arched eyebrows; the delightful appearance of whose nose is very beautiful, raised and lovely; <sup>34</sup>the elephant pearl at the tip of whose nose bewilders the three worlds; whose enchanting lips are red like vermillion; <sup>35</sup>whose earrings are shaped like golden *makaras* shining with various gems and take on his splendour and his fragrance, shining like mirrors; <sup>36</sup>who is decorated with the best flowers, such as ear-lotuses and good Mandāras; whose enchanting, beautifully bent neck astonishes the three worlds; <sup>37</sup>who is decorated with a quivering, sweet, reddish conch of a neck; whose chest carries the Śrīvatsa mark, the Kaustubha jewel and a beautifully glittering pearl necklace; <sup>38</sup>who is most enchantingly ornamented with Kadamba and sweet Mandāra flowers, divine jewels and rubies inlaid in pure gold; <sup>39</sup>who has golden bracelets on his arms and bells at his hips; whose beautiful, blessed feet wear sweet anklets; <sup>40</sup>who is decorated with unguents of camphor, *aguru*-pulp,[^d-5] musk and glittering sandalwood pulp mixed with *gorocana*; <sup>41</sup>who is embellished with a smooth yellow *dhoṭi* with a swinging, charming edge; whose deep lotuslike navel is garlanded with creepers of lines of soft hairs; <sup>42</sup>whose knees are beautifully shaped; whose feet are like lotuses; whose palms and footbottoms are marked with a flag, thunderbolt, hook and a lotus; <sup>43</sup>the rays from whose moonlike toenails are the cause of the complete Brahman – Sanaka and other kings of Yoga meditate on him in their hearts: <sup>44</sup>who is bent in three places, made of the nectar of the beauty of all kinds of loveliness, whose bent neck defeats the beauty of countless millions of cupids; <sup>45</sup>whose trembling, golden earrings carry the fragrance of his beautiful body; <sup>46</sup>whose smile and sidelong glances bewilder millions of cupids, and who by the sweet music of the flute by his curved lips bewilders the three worlds and plunges them into an ocean of love!’

*Devī said:* <sup>47</sup>‘God of gods, great god, deliverer from the ocean of birth and death! Explain in detail all of this most confidential <sup>48</sup>meditation on Viṣṇu of unlimited strength! Śaṅkara, great god, master, please explain the practice [p. 272] of the Clan behaviour. <sup>49</sup>Ocean of compassion, greatest master! Please explain to me whatever wise Hari did on account of the Clan behaviour.’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>50</sup>‘Listen, audacious woman, and I will tell you Vāsudeva’s story; listen, my dear, I will tell all its parts and details! <sup>51</sup>Without you, greatest mistress, the world would be like a dead corpse. In the same way, greatest mistress, fair-complexioned one, all of this exists only on account of Kṛṣṇa’s Clan behaviour.’

## Fifteenth Paṭala

*Īśvara said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Listen carefully, great mistress, to the truth of the meditation! Goddess, there can be no meditation without a body. <sup>2</sup>The body is made of Prakṛti, but it is the only cause of attaining the complete Brahman. That which is called *Vṛndāvana* has come from your hair.<sup>ǁ</sup> <sup>3</sup>The *Mandāra* tree, greatest mistress, is the enchanting desire tree, and the Surabhi cow of Prakṛti is the desire tree itself, and <sup>4</sup>its *branches and twigs* are the Mātṛkās, the syllables; know its swarms of intoxicated *bees* to be Prakṛti, beautiful one. <sup>5</sup>Handsome woman, the *Seat of perfection*, always full of all powers, of seven coverings, is the highest Prakṛti herself. <sup>6</sup>Great mistress! The faultlessness of the *Śruti*, the *golden seat,*[^x-5] the *jewelled pavilion,* the *shining Seat of Union*, <sup>7</sup>that which is called an *octagon*, having the eternal form of a *yoni*, the peerless *royal throne* made of rubies, goddess, <sup>8</sup>the *eight petals* that are the eight heroines, the place[^ba-5] that is *dear to Govinda*, the endless, wonderful *joy*, <sup>9</sup>the *dear love* that is always a form of Śakti, the *cowherdesses*, that is, all of the Gopī maidens, who always attend to Kṛṣṇa’s needs – <sup>10</sup>great mistress, a cowherdess is a part of a form of Śakti – his youth and lovely form: all of this is said to be Prakṛti.
<sup>11</sup>‘The *infancy, boyhood* and *adolescence –* all is said to consist of Prakṛti. You whose eyes are long like fishes![^bb-5] Greatest mistress, that *highest light* that was said to be *compact bliss,* <sup>12</sup>that, my dear, greatest mistress, Śakti herself became. That which was called *shining dark as dark collyrium*, <sup>13</sup>is Mahākālī Mahāmāyā herself, the embodiment of that colour. Know *begin-ningless* to refer to Prakṛti and *original* to be Prakṛti herself.
<sup>14</sup>‘Mistress of the gods! Kṛṣṇa is always dear to *cowherd Nanda*, for he who is from one’s self (*ātman*) born (*jā*) is known as a *son* (*ātmaja*). <sup>15</sup>Fair-complexioned one, he is said to be Nanda’s cherished son. [p. 273] ‘All of this *enchantment*, handsome woman, is a form of Śakti. <sup>16</sup>*Searched for by the Śruti*, *birthless, eternal,* the *mind* and *the cowherdesses*, greatest mistress – Śakti herself became that, my dear. <sup>17</sup>*The newly formed raincloud* is nothing but Kālikā’s body. The *crystals on a string* are a form of the highest Prakṛti, <sup>18</sup>and that, Pārvati, which was said to be *beautiful as a blossoming blue lotus* should be understood to a portion of the beauty of the highest, supreme Prakṛti.
<sup>19</sup>‘Greatest mistress, handsome woman, that which was said to be *black as ground collyrium* is always a part of Mohinī, a form of Śakti, <sup>20</sup>and Mohinī, sweetly smiling one, is a part of Prakṛti Māyā. That which was called *smooth* and so on *hair* <sup>21</sup>is a part of she who is the form of fragrance, greatest mistress. Great mistress, fair-complexioned one! That which was called an *oblique lock of hair*, <sup>22</sup>that lock is Prakṛti Māyā, perpetually enchanting the world, possessing the Kuṇḍalī Śakti[^bc-5] and the Yoni Mudrā.[^bd-5] <sup>23</sup>That which was called a *garland of Mālatī flowers* <sup>†ǁ</sup> is always a part of Mālatī,[^bx-5] while the *ribbon in the hair* should be understood as Kuṇḍalī.[^ca-5] <sup>24</sup>The *peacock* *feather* is the Yoni Mudrā, handsome woman, and the *diadem*, greatest mistress, is directly a form of Śakti. <sup>25</sup>Great mistress, the *face with dangling curls of hair, like millions of moons* is directly Śakti, the supreme part of the moon. <sup>26</sup>The moon, beautiful one, has sixteen parts; therefore, the moon is a form of Śakti.
<sup>27</sup>‘Dear one, great mistress, the *tilaka of musk*, the *tilaka of rocana,* is the blazing Śakti, the highest mistress Prakṛti. <sup>28</sup>Highest mistress, that which was called *the very soft and wide petal-like eyes like blue lotuses*, that is that what has been described before as having two parts. <sup>29</sup>The *raised eyebrows* are that which has been described before, with its parts, and so is the *very beautiful and raised nose,*[^cb-5] <sup>30</sup>for the supreme cause of Brahman should be known to always possess two parts.[^cc-5]
‘What use is there for more words, my dear goddess? Everything is made of Śakti.
<sup>31</sup>‘Greatest mistress, my dear, the supreme Śakti herself has become this body, great mistress, this body of Kṛṣṇa beyond the qualities, that has been described. <sup>32</sup>Great mistress, beyond the letters, it is the highest goddess who is the cause. <sup>33</sup>Beautiful one, daughter of the mountain, when Viṣṇu is without a body, then only is he the eternal, unalterable Brahman. <sup>34</sup>When Viṣṇu [p. 274] possesses a body he becomes Śabdabrahman, and that Śabdabrahman, higher than the highest, is the cause of everything. <sup>35</sup>Mistress of the gods! Both in the case of Śabdabrahman and in the highest Brahman,[^cd-5] the cause is always the supreme goddess whose form is Prakṛti.
