<sup>36</sup>‘This body of Prakṛti is a form of the abundance of the highest bliss. Therefore, goddess, great mistress, lotus-eyed Viṣṇu is always beyond the qualities; he is never entitled to independence.’

## Sixteenth Paṭala

*Devī said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Lord of the god of gods! Tell me about Kṛṣṇa, the highest cause, about Govinda, higher than the highest, the eternal master of Vṛndāvana, the only cause of that which is beyond the *guṇas*, <sup>2</sup>and about his wonderful greatness, full of sovereignty and excessive beauty! Master, I wish to hear.’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>3</sup>‘What can one say about his greatness, when even the end of the greatness of the rays from his moonlike toenails is not known? Listen, goddess! <sup>4</sup>Within countless of millions of universes, within countless festivals of the three *guṇas*, Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Maheśvara are his fragments, millions upon millions times divided. <sup>5</sup>Through his power they engage in their tasks of creation, maintenance and so on. The moon is a millionth of a millionth part of the splendour of his shining body. <sup>6</sup>‘With the beams of his dark body, the nectar of the *rasa* of supreme bliss, the various forms of the highest self, he is the only cause of that beyond the *guṇas*. <sup>7</sup>The individual selves are a millionth of a millionth part of one of its rays; they consist of beams of it. <sup>8</sup>They say that the effulgence from the moongem of his blessed face and his lotus feet is the cause of the complete Brahman, difficult to attain by the Veda.
‘She who bewilders the world is unlimited millions of times divided from his fragrance, <sup>9</sup>born from the various perfumes of sandalwood, flowers and so on that have touched him. Dear to her is the *dūtī* Padminī, Rādhikā, beloved of Kṛṣṇa, and her parts, millions and millions times divided from her, are Lalitā and the other beautiful women.’

*Devī said:* <sup>10</sup>‘God of gods, great god, you who carry the spear and the trident, master! Please explain at length this secret that you just told me!’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>11</sup>‘The Mātṛkā that is goddess Kalāvatī is Mahāmāyā, the best of all, hanging around the neck of Tripurā, <sup>12</sup>the garland which hangs around Tripurā’s neck and increases fortune.
‘Padminī, Hastinī, Citriṇī and Kāminī, the supreme: <sup>13</sup>Padminī is supremely wonderful, full of beauty and loveliness; Padminī herself, great mistress, [p. 275] reveals Brahman. <sup>14</sup>Padminī, greatest mistress, is the supreme part of Brahman; from this highest Padminī come millions and millions of universes, <sup>15</sup>and in every universe, my dear, greatest mistress, Rudra, Viṣṇu and grandfather Brahmā stay forever, creating, maintaining, destroying and so on.
<sup>16</sup>‘The splendour of her shining body is a form of the supreme Prakṛti; one millionth of a millionth part of her is the moon, the supreme Prakṛti. <sup>17</sup>Kṛṣṇa’s dark body is directly Kālī, the world itself. With the beams of his body, the nectar of the *rasa* of supreme bliss, <sup>18</sup>the various forms of the highest self, he is the only cause of that beyond the *guṇas*. Greatest mistress, a ray of Brahman is the supreme Prakṛti, <sup>19</sup>and the living beings are a millionth of a millionth part of one of its rays; they consist of beams of it. They say that it is the cause of the complete Brahman, difficult to attain by the Veda.<sup>‡ǁ</sup>
<sup>20</sup>‘She who bewilders the world is unlimited millions of times divided from his fragrance. Great mistress, that fragrance which is said to exist in Kṛṣṇa’s body; that part, known as fragrance, is directly a form of Prakṛti.’

*Pārvatī said:* <sup>21</sup>‘Greatest master, if they say that the complete Brahman is difficult to attain by the Veda,[^cx-5] then how can Kṛṣṇa be *complete* and *higher than the highest*?[^da-5] <sup>22</sup>If it is not attained by the Veda, great god, carrier of the trident, there will never be any difference between the Veda and the supreme Brahman.[^db-5] Veda is the supreme Brahman; Brahman again assumes the Veda as its very form. Unity between Veda and Brahman is called the complete Brahman. <sup>24</sup>An inactive and immovable god is this eternal complete Brahman, but the Veda is also Prakṛti Māyā, the supreme cause of Brahman. <sup>25</sup>The unity of Veda and Brahman is the wonderful, fourth abode. Greatest master, how then can this ancient one be said to be attainable by the Veda? This doubt is a thorn in my heart; please remove it, lord!’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>26</sup>‘The imperishable Brahman without qualities is called the supreme Brahman, but when Brahman takes on qualities, it is called Śabdabrahman. <sup>27</sup>When the unqualified gives rise to the qualities of Prakṛti Māyā, then it is qualified Brahman; otherwise it is motionless. <sup>28</sup>Great mistress, where and when could the motionless move, and what would its movement serve, my dear?
<sup>29</sup>‘When the unqualified Brahman becomes qualified, it can always be attained by the Veda, but the Brahman that is always motionless cannot be attained by the Veda. <sup>30</sup>In this world we speak of the two Brahmans: Śabdabrahman and the supreme Brahman, but without the Śabdabrahman, goddess, the [p. 276] supreme is like a dead corpse. <sup>31</sup>Therefore, great mistress, Śabdabrahman is accompanied by the Mātṛkā letters – the Mātṛkā is most worshippable and Kṛṣṇa’s supreme mother.’

## Seventeenth Paṭala

*Īśvara said:* <sup>1</sup>‘From the touch of the dust of Padminī’s feet millions of universal eggs are born. Padminī is Tripurā’s *dūtī*, always attending to Kṛṣṇa’s needs.’

*Pārvatī said:* <sup>2</sup>‘Master, lord, lord of gods, highest master! Please explain all about Govinda’s seven coverings and about his associates.’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>3</sup>‘My dear, Govinda sits together with Rādhā on a jewelled royal throne, beautiful as already explained, wearing divine garlands. <sup>4</sup>His body is bent in three places, most charming, the star of the cowherdesses’ eyes. Outside of this but within the Seat of Union, surrounding the golden throne, <sup>5</sup>are the principal beloveds of Kṛṣṇa, decorated with ornaments and garments on all of their limbs: Lalitā and the other eight Prakṛtis. Rādhikā is Padminī herself.
<sup>6</sup>’In the West is goddess Lalitā, and Śyāmā is in the Northwest. In the North is blessed Dhanyā, in the Northeast is Haripriyā, <sup>7</sup>Viśākhā is in the East and Śaibyā then is in the Southeast. Padmā is in the South and Bhadrā is in the Southwest. So are they situated, one after the other.[^dc-5]
<sup>8</sup>‘Greatest mistress, these are Padminī’s eight heroines. Now listen, sweet one, to the practice of the Clan behaviour![^dd-5]
<sup>9</sup>‘In front of the edge of the Seat of Union is sweet and dear Candrāvalī. These eight are the principal Prakṛtis, and Śaibyā then is in the Southeast. <sup>10</sup>Padminī, Tripurā’s *dūtī* is blessed Rādhā who bewilders Kṛṣṇa. Candrāvalī, Candrarekhā, Citrā, Madanasundarī, <sup>11</sup>Priyā, blessed Madhumatī, Śaśirekhā and Haripriyā are situated one after the other clockwise in all the main and intermediate directions.<sup>§ǁ</sup>
<sup>12</sup>‘These sixteen main Prakṛtis are Kṛṣṇa’s principal beloveds, but Rādhā, the mistress of Vṛndāvana, who gives fearlessness to Kṛṣṇa, <sup>13</sup>is because of her loveliness and the sweetness of her perfect qualities most beloved of all. She is enchanting, she dresses beautifully and is glorious in her adolescence. <sup>14</sup>Coloured by various jewels and shining in their silken clothes, greatest mistress, these sixteen are the embodiments of the vowels.<sup>\#\*</sup> <sup>15</sup>This [p. 277] group of sixteen that I have described, goddess, is Mahāmāyā, the world itself.
‘Outside of them, in the middle of the splendid house that surrounds the Seat of Union, <sup>16</sup>facing and intent on them are thousands of fortunate cowherd girls, resembling masses of pure gold, very bright and having beautiful eyes, <sup>17</sup>adolescent, lovely as millions of cupids with their divine ornaments and decorations, with elephant-pearls at the nosetip, <sup>18</sup>with various ornaments in their hair and sweet, dangling earrings, perceiving his form within their hearts, longing to embrace him, <sup>19</sup>plunged into the *rasa* of embracing dark Kṛṣṇa, their minds tremblingly engrossed on Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet, worshipped with the lotuses of their eyes, motionless as painted pictures. <sup>20</sup>Their appearance bewilders Kṛṣṇa, their correct behaviour is devoted to Kṛṣṇa, their voices are sweet in different ways and in different ways they bewilder the three worlds. <sup>21</sup>Agitated by love, they sing about Kṛṣṇa’s confidential secrets; they are expert in sly tricks, they are dressed in divine clothing, <sup>22</sup>they are lovely and their gracefulness is wonderful, their sidelong glances are enchanting, they are exclusively attached to Govinda, longing for the touch of his limbs; <sup>23</sup>they are lovely, charming, excited, devoted to meditating on Kṛṣṇa, decorated with various clothes, shy, and their faces are sweet.
<sup>24</sup>‘Facing them are thousands of fortunate cowherd girls. Great mistress, these are the Veda girls,[^a-6] and there are thousands of billions of them. <sup>25</sup>All the Celestial girls are splendid in their divine forms and Rasa, and behind them, the Sage girls, sweet and enchanting. Their minds are plunged into Rādhā, and they cast smiling, sidelong glances.[^b-6]
‘Further outside the temple, surrounding these dear associates, <sup>27</sup>are those young boys who are of a similar age and dress, similar strength and manliness, similar form and similar qualities and activities, <sup>28</sup>who are devoted to similar calls, songs and fluteplay, who sit on golden seats and are decorated with golden ornaments. <sup>29</sup>Together with billions and billions of cowherds, such as Stokakṛṣṇa and Subhadra, noisy, youthfully clad, holding horns, canes, lutes and flutes, <sup>30</sup>meditating on all of his qualities, with bodies non-different from his form and intent on protecting Kṛṣṇa’s life, they sing, agitated by *rasa*.[^c-6]
<sup>31</sup>’Behind them, he is further attended by herds of Surabhī cows with their calves, agitated by *rasa*, watching his form motionless as painted pictures, always shedding showers of tears of bliss, <sup>32</sup>the hair on whose whole body [p. 278] is standing on end, amazed as masters of Yoga – by thousands of thousands of herds of cows with flowing milk.[^d-6]
<sup>33</sup>‘Behind them, goddess, within an enclosure shining like millions of suns, in a great garden in all four directions, pervaded by all kinds of fragrances, <sup>34</sup>there is in the West facing them a dwelling under a blessed Pārijata tree. Under that tree is a golden platform decorated with a golden temple, and <sup>35</sup>in the middle of that, on a splendid throne of jewels, rubies and pearls, sits Vāsudeva, the preceptor of the world, the highest bliss, <sup>36</sup>whose form of consciousness is beyond the three qualities, who is the cause of all causes, who is densely dark as a sapphire, whose hair is dark and curved, <sup>37</sup>whose broad eyes resemble lotus petals and who wears *makara*-shaped earrings. He is four-armed, a great abode, an everlasting form of light, <sup>38</sup>without beginning or end, eternal and the master of *pradhāna* and *puruṣa*. He carries the conch, disk, club and lotus, he wears a garland of forest-flowers, <sup>39</sup>he is dressed in yellow, he is very charming, decorated with divine ornaments and divine unguents and enchanting with his glittering, variegated bracelets. <sup>40</sup>Vāsudeva is further surrounded by his eight queens: Rukminī, Satyabhāmā, Nāgnajitā, Lakṣmaṇā, Mitrāvindā, Sunandā, dear Jāmbavatī and Suśīlā, <sup>15</sup>and Uddhava and his other associates, eagerly devoted to him.
<sup>42</sup>‘In the North is a divine garden overspread with yellow sandalwood trees. Under them is a golden platform adorned with a jewelled pavillion. <sup>43</sup>In the middle of that, on a shining, divine royal throne of rubies sits Balarāma together with Revatī, <sup>44</sup>the lord’s dear Ananta, non-different from him in terms of form and qualities, shining white as clear crystal, with eyes resembling red lotuses, <sup>45</sup>dressed in blue clothing, anointed with divine sandalwood pulp, whose beautiful cheeks are touched by his earrings, who is decorated with divine ornaments and garlands, <sup>46</sup>always intoxicated by wine, his eyes always rolling in his head,<sup>ǁ</sup> his beauty enchanting the world, who enjoys the juice of streams of nectar, <sup>47</sup>his eyes like white lotus petals, richly decorated with divine ornaments and jewelry and anointed with divine garlands. <sup>48</sup>His beautiful, extraordinary body completely bewilders the world.
‘In the Eastern garden is a great forest of divine trees, <sup>49</sup>and under them is a great platform decorated with a golden pavillion. In its middle, on a splendid, divine throne, sits <sup>50</sup>together with Ūṣā blessed Aniruddha, the lord of the world: sweet bliss, densely dark, with very sweet, black hair. <sup>51</sup>His sweet, restless eyes are gentle as blue lotuses, he has nice eyebrows that are raised and crooked, nice cheeks, a nice nose, <sup>52</sup>a nice neck, a beautiful chest, a nice voice and is completely enchanting! He has a diadem and earrings and is decorated with ornaments around his neck and elsewhere; <sup>53</sup>he is [p. 279] sweet with his lovely bracelets and his form is wonderful; where he is, he loves song and is worshipped by his dear servitors; <sup>54</sup>he is the complete Brahman, eternal bliss, and his form is made of pure *sattva*.[^x-6]
‘Above him and in the sky is Viṣṇu, lord of all lords, <sup>55</sup>eternal, with a form of original consciousness, the bliss of consciousness, the highest master, beyond the three qualities, imperceptible, imperishable, perpetual and undecaying, <sup>56</sup>his smile a mass of sweetness, his dark body beautiful, his hair dark, wavy and smooth, his dress and hair beautiful, <sup>57</sup>his very wide eyes gentle like lotus petals – shining with diadem and earrings, he enchants the three worlds. <sup>58</sup>He has four hands, decorated with the weapons of the disk, the lotus, the club and the conch. He wears armrings and bracelets of different types, and his hips are decorated with bells; <sup>59</sup>he carries the Śrīvatsa and Kaustubha ornaments and a splendid garland of forest flowers, his chest is illumined by an exalted necklace of lovely pearls, <sup>60</sup>he wears golden garments, he is carried by the blessed son of Vinatā,[^ba-6] he is flanked on both sides by Lakṣmī and Sarasvatī, <sup>61</sup>his is the majesty and joy of the complete Brahman, he is the receptacle of the *rasa* of complete bliss, he is praised by great seers and others and surrounded by his dear associates, <sup>62</sup>he is the Lord of all causes and effects – let one remember the master of masters of Yoga.
‘Below him and in Pātāla, together with the Power that Supports[^bb-6] <sup>63</sup>and in the middle of a jewelled pavillion, on a splendid, jewelled royal throne sits blessed Ananta, devoted to meditation on his form. <sup>64</sup>Behind him and surrounded in all four directions by lofty and enchanting walls made of crystal, <sup>65</sup>in a garden, the fragrance of whose flowers bewilders the three worlds, he is surrounded by gods and demons, served by Siddhas and Cāraṇas, <sup>66</sup>with divine limbs, pleasant and beautiful, their carriers properly decorated, praying for the boons they desire, longing to worship his feet.
<sup>67</sup>‘To their right, sages with selves of pure *sattva*, whose law and practice is devotion to him and who are eagerly attending to his devotion, desire[^bc-6] him.
<sup>68</sup>‘To their back, Sanaka and other noble and great yogis who take delight in themselves, whose forms consist of consciousness, who are devoted to glimpsing his form, <sup>69</sup>who have brought the meditation on him into their hearts and who have fixed their eyes on the tips of their noses, engage [p. 280] themselves in unmotivated devotion with their hearts, thoughts, bodies and words.
<sup>70</sup>‘Perfected Gandharvas who have attained him,[^bd-6] together with Vidyādharas and Kinnaras, groups of lusty Apsaras, devoted to song and dance, <sup>71</sup>wishing to worship his feet, desire him with joyful minds.
‘To their front and in the air, on pleasant seats, are all the Vaiṣṇavas: <sup>72</sup>Prahlāda, Nārada, the Kumāras, Śuka, Uddhava and others, whose brilliant emotions include the hairs on their bodies standing on end and internally longing for his vision, <sup>73</sup>their hairs on whose whole bodies are standing on end, who are full of brilliant love, who are endowed with secret love, with their selves filled with the joy of complete love, mentally recite the mantra of two letters,<sup>‡ǁ</sup> <sup>74</sup>called the crest jewel of mantras and which is the only cause of all mantras. The Kṛṣṇa mantra is the life of the mantras of all gods, <sup>75</sup>and the Kṛṣṇa mantra is the cause of all mantras of blessed Kṛṣṇa.[^cx-6] Of all Kṛṣṇa mantras, that of the adolescent is most efficacious, <sup>76</sup>and of all mantras of the adolescent, the crest jewel mantra is the origin. <sup>77</sup>At his lotus feet, they desire the unflinching practice of loving devotion.
‘Beyond this are lofty and most enchanting walls made of crystal <sup>78</sup>in all the four directions, splendidly shining with red and white flowers. A white, four-armed Viṣṇu, <sup>79</sup>decorated with a conch, disk, club, lotus, diadem and other ornaments is gatekeeper in the West. A red, four-armed Viṣṇu, wearing the weapons of the conch, disk and club, <sup>80</sup>splendid with a diadem, earrings and other ornaments, is gatekeeper in the North. A golden, four-armed Viṣṇu, wearing the weapons of the conch, disk and club, <sup>81</sup>decorated with a diadem, earrings and other ornaments and a garland of forest flowers is gatekeeper in the East. He is known as Golden Viṣṇu. <sup>82</sup>A black, four-armed Viṣṇu, decorated with conch, disk and so on is gatekeeper in the South. Let one meditate on this blessed Viṣṇu Hari![^ca-6]
<sup>83</sup>‘This, greatest mistress, is the supreme sevenfold covering. Padminī, the highest Rādhā, has this sevenfold covering. My dear – this covering is the Seven Śaktis[^cb-6] themselves.’

[p. 281]

## Eighteenth Paṭala

*Devī said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Great god, you who have the bull for your emblem, one more time I am asking you: Vāsudeva Viṣṇu is one and mistress Prakṛti is one. How then, greatest master, greatest lord, does variety appear?’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>2</sup>‘Listen, goddess, great mistress, and I will tell you a secret most confidential, how the one Viṣṇu became various! <sup>3</sup>Since the greatest mistress Prakṛti pervades the universe, mistress of the gods, she who is the world itself pervaded everything by becoming woman and man. <sup>4</sup>By the forms of woman and man she pervaded everything and blossomed. Vāsudeva, Mahāviṣṇu is the highest lord, beyond the qualities. <sup>5</sup>Whatever form Vāsudeva is situated in, that is true, lotus-eyed one, for that which is called the form of Kṛṣṇa is the cause of perfection of *vidyā*.
<sup>6</sup>’Sweetly smiling one! Know this Rādhā to be Tripurā’s Padminī. All other heroines are always the eight heroines;[^cc-6] <sup>7</sup>great mistress, it is the Group of Eight[^cd-6] that is called the eight heroines.
‘By the grace of Tripurā, Vāsudeva Mahāviṣṇu, <sup>8</sup>took on many bodies and performed various activities. Beautiful one, great mistress, having taken on the form of Kṛṣṇa, together with Padminī, <sup>9</sup>he recited the *vidyā* of Mahākālī, the mistress of the gods. In this way, my dear, Hari took perpetual shelter of Vṛndāvana.
<sup>10</sup>‘Lotus-eyed one! Vāsudeva, Hari himself, appeared as Kṛṣṇa. When Mahāviṣṇu appeared in Mathurā, beautiful one, <sup>11</sup>Viṣṇu with four arms, Hari himself, appeared. At that moment, greatest mistress, the two-armed form of intelligent Hari appeared;<sup>‡ǁ</sup> <sup>12</sup>Kṛṣṇa, perpetually unchanging, made himself two-armed to hide himself. Having become like a human being, with a human body of Māyā, <sup>13</sup>great mistress, Kṛṣṇa did[^cx-7] in the middle part of his life that which has been explained with Padminī, in each opening, both in the top and in the lower part, <sup>14</sup>that which was secretly explained, handsome woman, to attain perfection of his mantra.[^da-6] When Kṛṣṇa, living in Dvārakā, gave up his body, <sup>15</sup>Kṛṣṇa’s light again entered into Vāsudeva Mahāviṣṇu. Therefore, great mistress, my dear, without Vāsudeva, <sup>16</sup>one can never attain Brahman in any other gods.
‘Goddess! Though always unchanging, Vāsudeva partakes of variety. <sup>17</sup>Beautiful one, whichever form Vāsudeva sees, into that form he indeed goes. Thus Hari partakes of variety. <sup>18</sup>The infallible one quickly assumes separated bodies,[^db-6] after he by the grace of Tripurā has taken on a secret body. [p. 282] ‘Great mistress, handsome woman! All of those who were mentioned, *Sanaka* and so on; all of those various *Viṣṇus* who were mentioned and the others, <sup>20</sup>all of them are knowledgeable in the Clan scriptures and devoted to Creeper practice.[^dc-6] All of the *heroines* mentioned are illuminators of the Clan *vidyā*; <sup>21</sup>handsome woman, all of those *golden, black, red, white* and *yellow* forms mentioned are devoted to the Clan scriptures. <sup>22</sup>Fair-complexioned one, these *black, golden* and so on forms are partial forms of Vāsudeva, <sup>23</sup>but Kṛṣṇa is Vāsudeva himself because of his worshipping Tripurā’s feet.
‘The *Revatī* who was mentioned, the others, *Rukminī* and the other eight who were mentioned; <sup>24</sup>he who is called *Aniruddha* and his *Ūṣā*; *Lord Balarāma*, *the Power that Supports* itself – whatever was mentioned, great mistress, and whatever else there is, all of that, greatest mistress, are the Mātṛkās, bewilderers of the world.
<sup>26</sup>‘This world, greatest mistress, is always made up of letters, for without letters, great mistress, this whole world would be meaningless. <sup>27</sup>Vāsudeva Mahāviṣṇu is always without qualities, my dear, but he engages with the various *vidyās*, the very forms of the complete Brahman. <sup>28</sup>Viṣṇu is always without qualities, but the supreme Prakṛti has qualities; therefore, the qualified Viṣṇu has come together with Prakṛti. <sup>29</sup>Without Prakṛti, eminent Viṣṇu is always motionless. Vāsudeva Mahāviṣṇu carries a conch, disk and a club, <sup>30</sup>yellow clothes, a garland and bells at his round hips. All of this decoration, goddess, and this form, are always Prakṛti. <sup>31</sup>Mahāviṣṇu has no senses and Kṛṣṇa is his part. Mahāviṣṇu is the complete Brahman and Kṛṣṇa is his part.’

*Devī said:* <sup>32</sup>‘The eternal master of Vṛndāvana is the only cause of that beyond the qualities! Lord, best of ascetics, why do you speak like this?’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>33</sup>‘Listen, audacious, beautiful woman, and I will answer your doubt. He who is called the master of Vṛndāvana is a part of Viṣṇu. <sup>34</sup>His body, great mistress, handsome one, is the goddess, original Prakṛtī; his self is Mahāviṣṇu and his mind is Rudra.
<sup>35</sup>’This truth, beautiful one, is the description of the hidden Brahman; without this truth, there can be no truth at all. <sup>36</sup>That which is born from this, goddess, should be known as the same as Brahman.
‘Greatest mistress, my dear, the wonder of Kṛṣṇa that you have seen, <sup>37</sup>all of that is Kṛṣṇa’s by his worship of Tripurā’s feet. This body of Kṛṣṇa, dear [p. 283] one, is a form of Kālī herself, <sup>38</sup>but Padminī, greatest mistress, is the supreme part of Padminī. Only by the coming together of these two can Kṛṣṇa be said to be complete.
<sup>39</sup>‘Handsome one! Previously, at the ocean of milk, Vāsudeva accompanied by Lakṣmī suddenly gave Brahmā two strands of hair, a white and a black one. <sup>40</sup>This was a sign for his birth, my dear, beautiful one, in the Seat of the hair, in Vraja, in Madhuvana.[^dd-6]
<sup>41</sup>‘Therefore, great mistress, Pārvati, a part of Vāsudeva appeared, but Kṛṣṇa is himself he who has *bhaga*,<sup>§ǁ#</sup> <sup>42</sup>for *bhaga* is the greatness of desire; Hari is always furnished with *bhaga*. Without *bhaga*, handsome woman, Brahman is like a dead corpse.
<sup>43</sup>‘All of this imposture[^dx-5] is made up of your hair, for one is not able to describe your hair, great mistress. <sup>44</sup>Mistress of the gods! The imposture of your hair is always in Brahman; by the fragrance of your hair, the immovable starts moving.
<sup>45</sup>’Mistress of the gods! This Bhāgavata Tantra is known as Rādhā Tantra; it is the secret of Vāsudeva, most confidential. <sup>46</sup>Mistress of the gods, Vāsudeva Mahāviṣṇu, the prosperous, is Prakṛti herself, and Vāsudeva is Kṛṣṇa himself, none other. Handsome woman, Kṛṣṇa is therefore a part of Vāsudeva and of Prakṛti.’

## Nineteenth Paṭala

*Īśvara said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Goddess, greatest mistress, my dear! The black and other coloured forms are parts of Vāsudeva, as are the master of Vṛndāvana and the golden, <sup>2</sup>white, red and blessed Viṣṇus, sweetly smiling one. <sup>3</sup>Vāsudeva’s conch is said to be the *white Viṣṇu*, Vāsudeva’s disk is known as the *golden Viṣṇu* and <sup>4</sup>the lotus, greatest mistress, is known as the *red Viṣṇu*. Greatest mistress! The club of Viṣṇu of unlimited splendour <sup>5</sup>is *blessed Viṣṇu*, enchanter of the world, but Kṛṣṇa, dear to Padminī, is always a two-armed Viṣṇu.
<sup>6</sup>‘Vāsudeva Mahāviṣṇu has two Śaktis; Hari is always accompanied by Lakṣmī and Sarasvatī. <sup>7</sup>Therefore, handsome woman, Vāsudeva is the complete Brahman; great mistress, Vāsudeva is mistress Prakṛti herself. <sup>8</sup>Know [p. 284] the eldest Prakṛti to be the pervasive Vaiṣṇavī; the eldest Māyā of Prakṛti is Vāsudeva, Hari himself.’

*Devī said:* <sup>9</sup>‘God of gods, great god, you who carry the spear and the trident! Great god, you indicated that Rādhā remained in a lotus grove <sup>10</sup>and that it was the Candrāvalī Rādhā that lived in Vṛkabhānu’s house. Explain all of this in detail, greatest master, Lord; how could Rādhā then come together with Kṛṣṇa? Lord, ocean of compassion, destroy, destroy this doubt!’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>11</sup>‘This Bhāgavata Tantra, this enchanting Rādhā Tantra, is extremely beautiful, pure, spotless and the highest abode. <sup>13</sup>When practitioners bearing divine bodies hear it, greatest mistress, and hide it in their hearts, they wish to hear nothing else. <sup>14</sup>This Tantra, great mistress, is pleasing to hear, it increases happiness, it is the highest secret, the essence of essences, higher than the highest!
<sup>15</sup>‘This Padminī Tantra is also known as the Bhāgavata. In whichever scripture or Tantra one finds the Gāyatrī,[^a-7] my dear, <sup>16</sup>a description of the five Viṣṇus[^b-7] and the qualities of Padminī, that indeed is known as the Bhāgavata. <sup>17</sup>This Bhāgavata Tantra includes the Gāyatrī.[^c-7]
‘Whatever Tantra in which one finds a description of the five Viṣṇus, the qualities of Padminī <sup>18</sup>and the complete Gāyatrī of Padminī, beautiful one, is known as the Bhāgavata. <sup>19</sup>That which is without cannot be called the Bhāgavata; in whichever Purāṇa or Tantra, beautiful one, <sup>20</sup>if the complete Gāyatrī is lacking, or the qualities of Prakṛti. Wherever one finds a description of five Viṣṇus, <sup>21</sup>that should be understood as the Bhāgavata; anything else is nothing but an imposture.
‘Handsome woman, Vāsudeva Mahāviṣṇu appeared in Mathurā; <sup>22</sup>Mahāviṣṇu appeared together with Prakṛti; Mahāviṣṇu appeared because of having worshipped the feet of Tripurā. The highest mistress Mahāmāyā appeared before him. <sup>23</sup>Hari himself appeared during the dark fortnight of the month of Bhadra; Padminī, during the bright fortnight of the month of Caitra.
<sup>24</sup>‘Great mistress, dear goddess, once Padminī, fragrant as a lotus, had appeared, she created Candrāvalī just like herself, <sup>25</sup>furnished with a lovely form for the house of Vṛkabhānu. This supreme part of Padminī then extended her enchanting Māyā, <sup>26</sup>goddess, in a thicket by the Kālindī, splendid, surrounded by various lotuses; white, red, yellow and blue, <sup>27</sup>and by other, various flowers, fragrant and of different colours; beautiful, filled with geese, ducks and white birds; <sup>28</sup>surrounded by hosts of Gandharvas and gods, lotus-eyed one; filled with the sound of drums, conches and lutes, <sup>29</sup>on a jewelled bed, [p. 285] inlaid with all kinds of jewels, in the middle of all of this. This bed, my dear, awards *dharma*, *k āma* and *artha*; it gives *mokṣa*!
<sup>30</sup>‘In its middle, greatest mistress, sitting on a jewelled royal throne, accompanied by the fifty Mātṛkās and the four Vedas, <sup>31</sup>surrounded by Nārada and other great sages, greatest goddess, on a jewelled royal throne in the middle of all of this, <sup>32</sup>greatest mistress, sits the eternal Kātyāyanī Śivā – and to the left of Kātyāyanī and on a lion, Padminī. <sup>33</sup>To serve Kātyāyanī this loving woman sits there, great mistress, until coming together with Kṛṣṇa.
<sup>34</sup>‘After one has properly worshipped an earthen Liṅga of Parameśvara, one should worship with various flowers and enchanting ingredients. <sup>35</sup>Having worshipped properly, one should devotedly recite the supreme mantra. My dear, one should always recite Kātyāyanī’s great mantra. Listen carefully to this mantra, greatest mistress!
<sup>36</sup>‘“*Oṃ Hrīṃ*. Kātyāyanī, Mahāmāyā, great Yoginī, supreme goddess! Make Kṛṣṇa, the son of cowherd Nanda, my husband! I bow to you.[^d-7] *Hrīṃ Oṃ*.”
<sup>37</sup>‘Goddess! Padminī of the lotus garland incessantly recited this Bhāgavatī *vidyā* addressed to Kātyāyanī. <sup>38</sup>She recited this Mahāvidyā until coming together with Kṛṣṇa. After some days, goddess, she who is the world itself appeared; Mahāmāyā, the world itself, the slayer of Mahiṣa herself.<sup>ǁ</sup>

*ātyāyanī said:* <sup>39</sup>“Who are you, my dear, with eyes like lotus petals? Why are you alone? Why have you come here? Tell me everything properly, my dear.”

*‘Padminī replied:* <sup>40</sup>“Kātyāyanī, Mahāmāyā, I bow to you, beloved of Śiva! You are Kṛṣṇa’s mother! I bow to you again and again. <sup>41</sup>Mistress of gods, who is my father? Whose am I, fair-complexioned one? Tripurā is the mother of the world; I am her[^x-7] maid. <sup>42</sup>My name, great mistress, greatest goddess, is Padminī. When will I see Vāsudeva, beautiful one?”

*ātyāyanī replied:* <sup>43</sup>“Do not fear, daughter; you will soon attain Kṛṣṇa. Sweetly smiling one, mistress of gods! At full moon during the waning fortnight in winter <sup>44</sup>you will meet with Vāsudeva. My dear, without your company, Vāsudeva will not be successful, <sup>45</sup>for your company, beautiful one, is liberation, the highest abode. [p. 286] ‘“During the eighth day of the bright fortnight of Bhadra, under the asterism of Rohiṇī, <sup>46</sup>Mahāviṣṇu appeared; I am telling you nothing wrong.” So saying, Mahāmāyā disappeared.
<sup>47</sup>‘Sweetly smiling one, lotus-eyed Padminī became ecstatic to hear this and sat down on Kātyāyanī’s throne. There Padminī Rādhā remained until meeting Kṛṣṇa, growing up together with the other cowherd girls in their respective houses; they were all by their thousands Celestial girls, greatest mistress. Kṛṣṇa appeared after Padminī in Vraja, my dear, <sup>50</sup>but lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa, beautiful, lotus-eyed one, the son of Devakī, grew from day to day in the house of Nanda and enacted his infancy, boyhood and adolescence.’

## Twentieth Paṭala

*Īśvara said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Handsome woman, I will tell you a most confidential secret, beautiful and most enchanting. Listen carefully! <sup>2</sup>Great mistress, my dear, listen to Kṛṣṇa’s relatives.[^ba-7] His brothers, his friends of the same age, his servants and his beloveds <sup>3</sup>are all according to rank respected by him, the crown prince of blessed Vraja.
‘Raining (*parjanya*) auspicious nectar, the cowherd called Parjanya <sup>4</sup>is the eldest of the cowherd people of Vraja. He is Kṛṣṇa’s paternal grandfather. Kṛṣṇa’s most respected paternal grandmother is known as Varīyasī. <sup>5</sup>His very powerful maternal grandfather is known as Sumukha. In Vraja, his maternal grandmother is known by the name Pāṭalā.
<sup>6</sup>‘His father, giving bliss (*ānanda*) to Vraja, is Nanda, worshipped by the world. His mother, giving (*dātrī*) fame (*yaśas*) to the cowherds, is Yaśodā, full of happiness. <sup>7</sup>Rohiṇī, who always increases (*ārohiṇī*) everyone’s delight, is his elder mother.[^bb-7] His paternal uncles Upananda and Abhinanda are the elder brothers of his father <sup>8</sup>while Sananda and Nandana are younger brothers. Nandinī is his paternal aunt and her husband is Nīla. <sup>9</sup>The wives of his paternal uncles are Tuṅgī, Pivarī, Kuvalā and Attanā.
‘His maternal uncles include Yaśodhara, Yaśodaiva and Sudeva. <sup>10</sup>His mother’s sister is Yaśasvinī and Nanda is her husband.[^bc-7]
‘Tuṇḍu, Kuṭera, Puraṭa and others are like his paternal grandfathers, <sup>11</sup>while Goṇḍa, Kadordu, Kāruṇḍa and so on are like maternal grandfathers to him. Śilābherī, Śikhā and other old ladies are like his paternal grandmothers. <sup>12</sup>Tāruṇḍā, Jaṭilā, Bhelā, Karālā, Karavālikā, Ghargharā, Mukharā, Ghorā and Ghaṇṭā are like maternal grandmothers to him. <sup>13</sup>Piṅgala, Kapila, Piṅga, Māṭhara, Pīṭha, Paṭṭiśa, Śaṅkara, Saṅgara, Bhṛṅga, Biṅga and others are like [p. 287] fathers to him. <sup>14</sup>Taraṅgākṣī, Taraṇikā, Śubhadā, Mālikā, Aṅgadā, Vatsalā, Kuśalā, Tālī, Medurā and so on are like mothers to him.
<sup>15</sup>‘Ambikā and Kilimbā are wet nurses, giving him their breasts.
‘Vedagarbha, Mahāyajvā and Bhagu are their family priests, <sup>16</sup>and Sulatā, Gomatī, Yāmī, Caṇḍikā and others are the wives of the Brahmins.
‘Foremost among his friends of the same age and his elder brother is Pralamba.[^bd-7]
<sup>17</sup>‘Samudra, Kuṇḍala, Daṇḍin and Maṇḍala are his paternal cousins. Sunanda, Nandira, Ānandin, Mandira and others accompany him on his walks.
<sup>18</sup>’Moonlike Kṛṣṇa’s companions are divided into four types: allies, friends, dear friends and dear playful friends.
<sup>19</sup>‘Allies are Maṇḍalībhadra, Bhadravardhana, Gobhaṭa, Yakṣendra, Bhaṭa, Bhandrāṅga, Vīrabhadra, Mahāguṇa, <sup>20</sup>Kulavīra, Mahābhīma, Divyaśakti, Suraprabha, Raṇasthira and others who are older than him and protect him. <sup>21</sup>Because his parents were terrified of the wicked Kaṃsa, they appointed them to protect their two sons, dearer to them than millions of their own lives. <sup>22</sup>Here the leader is Vijayākṣa, the son of Ambikā, whom Ambikā the wet nurse received by her penance and constant worship of Pārvatī.
<sup>23</sup>‘Viśāla, Vṛṣabha, Ājambi, Devaprastha, Varūthapa, Mandāra, Kusumāpīḍa, Manibandha, Karandhama, <sup>24</sup>Mandāra, Candana, Kunda, Kalinda, Kulaka and others are younger than him and serve him. They are his friends and defeat his enemies.
<sup>25</sup>‘Now the dear friends: Dāma, Sudāma, Vasudāmaka, Kiṅkinin, Stokakṛṣṇa, Bhadrasena, Viśālin, <sup>26</sup>Puṇḍarīka, Viṭāṅkākṣa, Kalaviṅka, Priyaṃkara, Śrīdāma and others are of the same age. Among them,
<sup>27</sup>Bhadrasena is the general of the armies of his friends and always delights Śrīdāma. In accordance with his name, Stokakṛṣṇa (Little Kṛṣṇa) is always under the guidance of Kṛṣṇa. <sup>28</sup>These dear friends delight Keśava with different kinds of adventures and sports, such as boxing and stick fights.
<sup>29</sup>‘Subāla, Arjuna, Gandharva, Vasanta, Ujjvala, Kokila, Sanandana, Vidagdha and others are known as the dear playful friends. <sup>30</sup>There are no secrets of his that these ones do not know about. Among these, blessed Sanandana is always beautified by the bliss of his affectionate friendship. <sup>31</sup>Ujjvala is greatly luminous, like an embodiment of quicksilver. Even the crown jewel of play becomes captivated by his playing.
<sup>32</sup>‘Madhumaṅgala, Puṣpāṅka, Hāsāṅka and others are jesters. [p. 288] ‘Kaḍāra, Bhāratībandha, Gandhaveda and others are <sup>33</sup>his servants of different types, devoted to the pleasure of his service. Retainers<sup>†ǁ</sup> are Bhaṇḍura, Bhṛṅgāra, Sandhika, Grahila, <sup>34</sup>Raktaka, Patraka, Patrī, Madhukaṇṭha, Madhuvrata, Śyālika, Tālika, Mālin, Māna, Mālādhara and others. <sup>35</sup>They carry his horn, his different kinds of flutes, his stick, rope and other things; among them, they are skillful and they present him with minerals.[^bx-6] <sup>36</sup>Younger, and staying close to him, budding in the art of describing the arts of play, are Pallava, Maṅgala, Phulla, Komala, Kapila, <sup>37</sup>Suvilākṣa, Viśāla, Rasāla, Rasaśālin, Jambula and others, experts in preparing Betel. <sup>38</sup>Payodha, Vārida and others bring water for his purification. Sāraṅga, Kuvala and so on are experts in decorating his clothes. <sup>39</sup>Premakanda, Mahāgandha, Sairindhri, Madhukandala, Makaranda and others tastefully dress him. <sup>40</sup>Sumana, Kusumollāsa, Puṣpahāsa, Hara and so on decorate him with flower ornaments, garlands, perfumes and unguents. <sup>41</sup>Suraṅga, Bhadrāṅga, Karpūra, Kusuma and others are barbers, expert in arranging his hair, combing him and offering him a mirror. <sup>42</sup>Svaccha, Suśītala, Guṇa and others are in charge of the store-rooms. Vimala, Kamala and so on are in charge of his plate and seat.
<sup>43</sup>‘Dhaniṣṭhā, Candanakalā, Guṇā, Mālā, Ratiprabhā, Bhavānī, Induprabhā, Śobhā, Rambhā and others are maids, <sup>44</sup>expert at cleaning the house and skilled in all kinds of work. Kuraṅgī, Bhṛṅgārī, Sulambā, Lambikā and so on are female servants.
<sup>45</sup>‘Catura, Cāraṇa, Dhīmat, Peśala and others are his best spies. Wearing various disguises, they always move among the cowherds and cowherdesses.
<sup>46</sup>‘Viśārada, Tuṅga, Vāvadūka, Manorama, Nītisāra and so on are his messengers both when it comes to play and quarrels, also among Rāma’s people.[^ca-7]
<sup>47</sup>Vṛndā, Vṛndārikā, Menā, Subālā and others are female messengers, experts at decorating groves and similar places; foremost of them is Vṛndā. <sup>48</sup>Another prominent female messenger is called Vīrā; she is famous and worshippable, my dear.
‘Śobhana, Dīpana and others are known as being in charge of the lanterns. <sup>49</sup>Vicitrarāva, Madhurarāva and so on are his bards. Candrahāsa, Induhāsa, Candramukha and others are dancers. <sup>50</sup>Sudhākara, Sudhādāna, Sāraṅga and so on are drummers. They are skillful, oceans of good qualities and play the great lute.[^cb-7] <sup>51</sup>Kalākaṇṭha, Sudhākaṇṭha, Śūlakaṇṭha, Bhārata, Sārada, [p. 289] Vidyāvilāsa, Sarasa and others <sup>52</sup>know about all kinds of literary compositions, are conversant with *rasa*, and recite poetry. The tailor called Rauhika makes jackets and other clothes. <sup>53</sup>Sumukha, Durlabha, Rañjana and others are washermen. Puṇya, Puñja, Bhājyavāsin and others play the Ḍiṇḍima-drum.[^cc-7] <sup>54</sup>The goldsmiths Raṅgana and Ṭaṅkana fashion ornaments. The potters Pavana and Karmaṭha make bowls and cups. <sup>55</sup>The carpenter called Vardhamāna makes his bed and cart. Sucitra and Vicitra both make wonderful artwork. <sup>56</sup>Kuṇḍa, Kaṇḍola, Karaṇḍa, Kaṭula and others are craftsmen, in charge of ropes, churning sticks, axes, boxes and carrying swings.
<sup>57</sup>‘Dhūmalā, Piṅgalā, Gaṅgā, Piśāṅgī, Maṇikastanī, Haṃsī, Vaṃśī, Priyā and others are excellent cows, very dear to him. <sup>58</sup>The two bulls Padmagandha and Piśaṅgākṣa are most dear to him. His deer is called Suraṅga and his monkey is named Dadhilobha. <sup>59</sup>His two dogs are Vyāghra and Bhramaraka; Kalasvana is his royal goose; his peacock is called Tāṇḍavika and Dakṣa and Vicakṣaṇa are his parrots.
<sup>60</sup>‘Vṛndāvana is a great garden, more splendid than the most splendid. Blessed Govardhana is appropriately called the mountain of sports. <sup>61</sup>There is a cave called Kandalī, named Nīlakaṇṭhapika.[^cd-7] At the Mānasa Gaṅgā, there is a famous bathing place called Pavaṅga, <sup>62</sup>and there one finds the boat Suvilāsatarā.
‘The hill called Nandīśvara is his splendidly shining palace. <sup>63</sup>There is an assembly hall on an enchanting, large rock. Since he lives there in the highest happiness (*āmoda*), it is called Āmodavardhana.
<sup>64</sup>‘The lake called Pāvana has on its banks plenty of sporting groves. The groves are called Kāma, Mahābhīma, Mandāra and Maṇikuṭṭima.<sup>‡ǁ</sup> <sup>65</sup>Bhāṇḍīra is his best banyan tree, and his Kadamba-tree is Kadambarāṭ. The sandbank where he plays is called Anaṅgaraṅgabhū. <sup>66</sup>The greatest place of pilgrimage by the Yamunā is called Khelatīrtha; there he eternally plays with his most beloved.
<sup>67</sup>‘His mirror is Śaradindu, his fan is Madhumāruta, his play lotus is Sadāsmera and his ball is Citrakāraka. <sup>68</sup>His bow, decorated with gold, is called Vilāsakarmika. The bowstring, tied to jewels at both ends, is Śiñjinī. <sup>69</sup>His little knife, with a handle studded with divine jewels, is called Tuṣṭidā. His horn is Mandraghoṣa and his flute is Bhuvanamohinī. <sup>70</sup>By this hook, he catches the fish of Rādhā’s mind; therefore, it is also known as Mahānandā [p. 290] (Great bliss). His smaller flute has six holes and is known as Madanarāṭ. <sup>71</sup>His Muralī-flute is called Saralā, a peacock that silences the sweetest tones. His favourite Rāgas are Gauḍī and Gurjarī.
<sup>72</sup>‘The mantra that he recites to achieve his goal is the wonderful name of his beloved.
‘His stick is called Maṇḍana and his lute is called Taraṅginī. <sup>73</sup>His two ropes are called Paśuvaśīkara and his milking pot is Amṛtadohanī.
‘On his arms his mother has tied great amulets inlaid with the nine gems. <sup>74</sup>His armlets are called Raṅgada; his bangles, Cikkaṇa; his signet ring, Ratnamukhī; and his yellow clothes, Nigamaśobhana. <sup>75</sup>His waist belt is Ruṇajhaṅkārā; his ankle bells, Haṃsagañjana. Their sweet tinkling captivates the deer of the deer-eyed girls’ minds. <sup>76</sup>His necklace is called Tārāvalī; his pearl necklace, Taḍitprabhā; and the golden medallion on his chest, with a picture of Rādhā secreted within, Hṛdayamodana. <sup>77</sup>His jewel is called Kaustubha. When he entered the snake’s lake, the wives of Kāliya offered it to him by their own hands. <sup>78</sup>His earrings, shaped as *makaras*, are known as Ratirāgādhidaivata. His diadem is called Ratnapāra and the crest, Cāmaraḍāmarā.
<sup>79</sup>‘Blessed Hari’s crown is known by the name Navaratnaviḍamba. His necklace of Guñja-seeds[^dx-6] is Rāgavallī; his *tilaka*, Dṛṣṭimohana. <sup>80</sup>His garland, reaching down to his feet and made of flowers and leaves, is Vanamālā, but one made of flowers of five different colours is called Vaijayantī.
<sup>81</sup>‘The fortunate night of the eighth day of the dark fortnight of Bhadra is ornamented by his birth.
‘Bhṛṅgī, Sunvī and Mataṅgī are daughters of the Pulinda people.[^da-7] <sup>82</sup>Some are Kṛṣṇa’s followers; others are part of his family. The Brahmin ladies led by Gārgī; Bhṛṅgārikā and other female servants; <sup>83</sup>Subāla, Ujjvala, Gandharva, Madhu, Piṅgala and Raktaka; Vijayā and others; Rasālā and others; Payodā and others; the jesters and more; <sup>84</sup>Svargī, Piśāṅgī, Kalakandalā, Mañjulā, Bindulā, Sandhā, Mṛdulā and others – all of these are at all times virtuous.[^db-7]
<sup>85</sup>‘Sunadā, Yamunā, Bahulā and others are cows that give birth to calves every year. The calf Tuṅgī is full and well-nourished. The monkey is called Kakkhaṭī. <sup>86</sup>The female doe is called Raṅginī, and the Cakora-bird, Cārucandrikā. The peahen in called Sundarī, and the two female parrots [p. 291] Sūkṣmadhī and Śubhā. <sup>87</sup>Day and night they sing the playful pastimes of their masters with a wonderful voice that astonishes the girlfriends. In their own grove, even the gods wander about for play.’

## Twenty-first Paṭala

*Īśvara said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Listen goddess, yoginī, to the supreme truth of Vāsudeva, unlimitedly sweet, peaceful and supreme among the supreme of all knowledge!
<sup>2</sup>‘Confusion, laziness, error, harshness, dependence, absorption in lust, fickleness, arrogance, jealousy, violence, depression, fatigue, <sup>3</sup>falsehood, anger, neediness, mental derangement, inequity and disregarding others – these are the eighteen faults, my dear. <sup>4</sup>The Lord’s body is bereft of these eighteen faults; it has all majesties, it is true and its nature is one of consciousness and bliss.
<sup>5</sup>‘His body is not one of Prakṛti; it is not born of meat and fat. Through yoga, great mistress, he is the self of all and his body is eternal. <sup>6</sup>Pārvatī, one who sees or touches someone who thinks that Vāsudeva’s body is material attains the result of killing a Brahmin.’

*Īśvara said:* <sup>7</sup>‘Pārvatī, one should understand the marks of the enchanting body of Vāsudeva to be that it is threefold broad, threefold deep, threefold slender, fivefold long, <sup>8</sup>fivefold fine, sixfold raised and sevenfold reddish.
‘Hari’s forehead, cheek, neck and chest – <sup>9</sup>one should know the threefold broad, threefold deep and threefold slender of Hari. The threefold slender are the nails, the hair and the lips. <sup>10</sup>The sages know that the threefold deep are the navel, the hand and the eye. Pārvatī! One should understand the arms and legs, hands, eyes and cheeks[^dc-7] <sup>11</sup>to be the fivefold long of Vāsudeva. Loving one, the neck, the waist, the shanks, the hair and the teeth – <sup>12</sup>these should be understood as the fivefold fine of Vāsudeva. The feet, the hands, the navel, the mouth, the nostrils, <sup>13</sup>the eyes and the ears are Hari’s sevenfold red. The nose, the neck, the shoulders, the chest, the head and the loins <sup>14</sup>are raised in Vāsudeva. These are the thirty-two marks on his body.[^dd-7]
‘His form, greatest mistress, has these marks, <sup>15</sup>and all of it, handsome woman, is goddess Prakṛti herself. Vāsudeva Mahāviṣṇu is like a shining Kālikā. <sup>16</sup>Sweetly smiling one! Having taken shelter of this body, with all [p. 292] of its marks, Viṣṇu became qualified, even though he is unqualified, <sup>17</sup>always in order to perform tasks, for otherwise Viṣṇu is always inactive. Vāsudeva’s body is Kālikā herself, nothing else.
<sup>18</sup>‘My dear, in Mathurā, in Vraja, Kṛṣṇa is a part of Vāsudeva, and the secret of Vṛndāvana, my dear, is Mahāmāyā herself, <sup>19</sup>for without Śakti, great mistress, Brahman is like a dead corpse.
‘By coming together with Padminī and by the grace of Mahāmāyā, <sup>20</sup>the glow from Kṛṣṇa’s toenails equals the effulgence of millions of Brahmans. Great mistress, loving one, what is impossible for Vāsudeva? <sup>21</sup>In each and every one of his toenails can be found the effulgence of millions of Brah-mans. He has everything − by worshipping the feet of Tripurā.’

*Devī said:* <sup>22</sup>‘God of gods, great god, deliverer from the ocean of birth and death! Lord! Please tell me the supreme truth of Padminī! Greatest master, please tell me the truth of Padminī.’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>23</sup>‘Sweetly smiling one, Padminī Rādhikā is Tripurā’s *dūtī*. Sweetly smiling one, once she attained Kṛṣṇa’s company, <sup>24</sup>she engaged herself in the Clan behaviour, goddess, in the rare Clan behaviour both day and night: all of that supreme and wonderful Clan behaviour, handsome woman, <sup>25</sup>that has been explained in the mantras and Tantras. My dear, many times Padminī discarded her own form as a heroine into the lotus garland, <sup>26</sup>and then Padminī, the most wonderful Rādhikā, the enchanter of Kṛṣṇa, created millions of new ones.
<sup>27</sup>Kṛṣṇa also wanted to make himself many for the Clan practice, so the lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa took on many bodies. <sup>28</sup>Having taken on expansions of his body, he who has eyes like lotus petals enjoyed himself with all the cows, cowherd boys and cowherd girls that Padminī had created, one after the other. <sup>29</sup>During the first month of winter and until the end of winter,<sup>§ǁ</sup> daughter of the mountain, when winter had begun, he performed the Clan behaviour; he performed all of the Clan behaviour as it has been described before in the Tantras.
<sup>30</sup>‘A most wonderful heroine has eight Seats.[^dx-7] Goddess, by the worship of a heroine, Kālikā is always worshipped.
<sup>31</sup>‘By doing seven hundred recitations at seven Seats, Hari became the master of perfections. For when winter had begun, he performed the Clan behaviour. <sup>32</sup>Fair-complexioned one, he placed himself on the left side of Padminī, [p. 293] faced Kāmākhyā, did the wonderful Pervasive Installation,[^a-8] <sup>33</sup>and then worshipped the goddess of the Seat in the parts of Padminī’s body. Sweetly smiling one, all that is described in all of the Tantras, <sup>34</sup>all of that very rare and most confidential secret, greatest mistress, did lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa do. <sup>35</sup>All of this practice, handsome woman, is very confidential, and this divinity and this secret is all established in you. <sup>36</sup>Therefore, sweetly smiling one, mistress of the gods, when one has worshipped properly with fragrances and enchanting articles in all heroines, <sup>37</sup>then one should also properly worship one’s cherished goddess, great Kālī.
‘After properly worshipping the goddess in the parts of Padminī’s body, <sup>38</sup>he did one hundred thousand recitations and then entered Uḍḍiyānā. That Seat is known as Yoganidrā. Hari worshipped and recited; <sup>39</sup>after worshipping his own cherished goddess he composedly did one hundred thousand recitations. Uḍḍīyanā is the thighs; Kāmākhyā is the genital area. <sup>40</sup>He then entered Kāmarūpa where Kātyāyanī Śivā dwells; Kāmarūpa, great mistress, is said to be the head of Brahmā. <sup>41</sup>Great mistress, there Hari properly did one hundred thousand recitations. Then Kṛṣṇa went to Jālandhara and worshipped the goddess. <sup>42</sup>Great mistress, Jālandhara is said to be the breasts. There also lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa did one hundred thousand recitations. <sup>43</sup>Then he went to Pūrṇagiri and quickly worshipped Caṇḍī. Fair-complexioned one! There also he did one hundred thousand recitations, as at the head. <sup>44</sup>Having worshipped the original goddess in these parts of Padminī’s body, greatest mistress, he did one hundred thousand most rare recitations <sup>45</sup>at a seat within Kāmarūpa, at the enchanting Moon circle,[^b-8] and worshipped goddess Mahāmāyā as Dikkarivāsinī.[^c-8]
<sup>46</sup>‘Great mistress, after Kṛṣṇa had composedly recited at each Seat, after he had done seven hundred thousand recitations at the seven Seats, Hari became a master of perfections. <sup>47</sup>In this very way unchanging Hari became perfected.
‘In winter, in the proper time, he performed the Clan behaviour, <sup>48</sup>in a blossom-covered cottage in the great forest of Vṛndāvana, in a grove by the Yamunā, beautified by Aśoka trees and fresh blossoms, <sup>49</sup>overspread with geese and ducks, resounding with the cries of gallinules, filled with peacocks and koils and all kinds of other birds; <sup>50</sup>in the circle of Vraja, beautified by [p. 294] a thousand autumnal moons, in the land of Vraja, bereft of the Six Waves,[^d-8] my dear, handsome woman.
<sup>51</sup>‘The land of Vraja, great mistress, my dear, is always the land of Śyāma, where goddess Mahāmāyā, great Kālī always stays, <sup>52</sup>and where, great mistress, the Tamāla tree is Kālī herself. In the circle of Vraja, greatest mistress, the Kadamba tree is Tripurā,<sup>ǁ</sup> <sup>53</sup>for both the Tamāla and Kadamba are as wish-fulfilling trees.
‘The circle of Vraja is made from the mass of your hair, and, <sup>54</sup>great mistress, he whose eyes are like lotus petals stays in Vraja. Goddess, when he did this most arduous ritual, Kālī appeared. <sup>55</sup>My dear, after Kṛṣṇa had attained perfection is his mantra, she appeared.
‘“Chose a boon, my son, whatever is on your mind!”

*‘Kṛṣṇa replied:* <sup>56</sup>“If you, great mistress, are the highest goddess after me, I bow to the mother of the world; I bow at your feet. <sup>57</sup>Mistress of the gods, sweetly smiling one, nothing is impossible for me.”
‘This manifest Mahāmāyā, the highest goddess turned to him.
<sup>58</sup>‘“In the age of Kali, you will be famous in the land of India. Singing about your qualities will become popular; it is in no other way.”
‘Saying this, Mahāmāyā disappeared.’

## Twenty-second Paṭala

*Īśvara said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Beautiful one! Listen to the supreme truth of Rādhikā, which Kālī Mahāmāyā then told Padminī.
<sup>2</sup>‘“Listen now Padminī to the tonic of my words! My dear, most excellent one, you are a *dūtī*, always attending to Kṛṣṇa’s needs. <sup>3</sup>Rādhā, my *dūtī*, now quickly become a resident of Vraja, for you are the Śakti between the names of Kṛṣṇa and Govinda.”
‘Greatest mistress, listen carefully to this mantra!

*‘ Oṃ Kṛṣṇa Rādhe Govinda Oṃ*
<sup>4</sup>‘Mistress of the gods, lotus-eyed one, what I have told you is the mantra of nine syllables. KṚṢṆA or GOVINDA, greatest mistress, handsome woman, <sup>5</sup>all consists of Prakṛti; it is never in any other way. Mistress of the gods, this is a casket concealing how the cowherdess is everything for Vāsudeva. <sup>6</sup>Incessantly Kṛṣṇa meditated on the supreme syllables RĀDHĀ Rādhā. [p. 295] ‘In this way, Kṛṣṇa, the abode of the quality of *sattva*, <sup>7</sup>performed the most arduous Clan behaviour, sweetly smiling one. By coming together with Padminī, Kṛṣṇa became one with Brahman. <sup>8</sup>She who is Padminī Rādhā is herself a form of Brahman.
‘Once one has honoured a Mahāvidyā, let one always remember Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa; then, goddess, <sup>9</sup>this *vidyā* will quickly and surely award perfection. Goddess, mistress of the gods, all of those who remember Kṛṣṇa and Rādhikā without a Mahāvidyā <sup>10</sup>will kill Brahmins with their every step. Having honoured Kālikā’s highest *vidyā*, handsome woman, <sup>11</sup>one should make sure to conceal it with utmost care, great mistress. Having diligently worshipped a Mahāvidyā, great mistress, <sup>12</sup>one should proclaim the remembrance of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.
‘Greatest mistress, one can proclaim Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa both day and night, <sup>13</sup>and Vāsudeva or Govinda can be remembered in any way. Rāma and Lord Kṛṣṇa can be remembered in any way, <sup>14</sup>but, great mistress, a Mahāvidyā can never be revealed. This truth, great mistress, is enchanting and very confidential.
<sup>15</sup>‘My dear, killing Kālīya, breaking the two Arjuna trees, then killing Śakaṭa and Tṛnāvarta, <sup>16</sup>the Baka demon and Keśi, lifting the mountain, the terrible forest fire, and whatever else, sweetly smiling one, <sup>17</sup>that Kṛṣṇa did, greatest mistress, handsome woman, all of that, greatest mistress, was due to Kālikā’s grace. <sup>18</sup>The Rāsa festival, great mistress, my dear, all of the known and unknown things that he did in his youth, handsome woman, is a form of Mahāmāyā. Without Śakti, great mistress, my dear, nothing can be known.’

*Devī said:* <sup>19</sup>‘Lord, previously you indicated something about Rādhā and Candrāvalī. Master, lord of the world, expand on all of this!’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>20</sup>‘Rādhā, the enchanter of Kṛṣṇa, is Padminī, Tripurā’s *dūtī*. From her body have come both Rādhā and Candrāvalī. <sup>21</sup>The daughter of Vṛkabhānu is directly she who has the fragrance of a blooming lotus; she looks just like Padminī, she is endowed with beauty and sweetness, <sup>22</sup>she is beautifully dressed, most wonderful, fortunate and capricious. Padminī of the lotus garland always stays on the left side of Kṛṣṇa.
<sup>23</sup>‘Listen, mistress of the gods, sweetly smiling one, to other supremely beautiful Śaktis! Candraprabhā, Candravatī, Candrakānti, <sup>24</sup>Candrā, Candrakalā, Candralekhā, Candrāṅkitā, Rohiṇī, Dhaniṣṭhikā, <sup>25</sup>Viśākhā, Mādhavī, Mālatī, Gopālī, Ratnarekhā, Pārākhyā, <sup>26</sup>Subhadrā, Bhadrarekhā, Sumukhā, Surati, Capalā, Kuñjarī, Kalāvatī, Kalā, <sup>27</sup>Kalahaṃsī and Kalāpi.[^x-8] They are always of the same age as she. They are all of the same age; their bodies are eternally young, <sup>28</sup>they are decorated with all kinds of ornaments, [p. 296] they carry rosaries and they incessantly recite the Kālī *vidyā* that awards Brahman. <sup>29</sup>There are billions of other most excellent women, and all of these select women are Śakti herself, sweetly smiling one. <sup>30</sup>The forest dwellers cannot understand their thoughts and deeds. They are all bewildered by Māyā; the Vrajadwellers cannot understand.
<sup>31</sup>‘The garland around the neck of Padminī is Prakṛti Padminī. Greatest mistress, sweetly smiling one, <sup>32</sup>all of them always come from this garland and are dissolved into it in the middle of the night, all of them, fair-complexioned one, Candrā and the others of eyes like lotus petals. <sup>33</sup>The garland around the neck of Padminī is an enchanting lotus garland. Greatest mistress, one cannot describe the virtues of this garland! <sup>34</sup>I speak as much as I know, by your power, beautiful one, great mistress, as much as I have knowledge connected with Yoga. <sup>35</sup>Whatever I say, deer-eyed woman, is through worship of Tripurā’s feet. Great mistress! What is impossible by the grace of Tripurā?’

## Twenty-third Paṭala

*Īśvara said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Pārvatī, impudent woman! Listen to this most confidential secret. Together with the cowherds, Kṛṣṇa engaged himself day after day in <sup>2</sup>the most meritorious Clan behaviour that awards perfection in Mantra. Together with Bhairava,[^ba-8] great mistress, Pārvatī, the blessed, imperishable Hari did so in <sup>3</sup>the middle of the nights together with the women.
‘Greatest mistress, mistress of the gods, handsome one! Once Hari, the enchanter of the universe, <sup>4</sup>entered into a boat on the Yamunā. Whatever lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa did on the royal road, difficult to transverse, full of throngs of people, in the middle of the path, <sup>5</sup>swarming with elephants, horses, chariots and pedestrians – <sup>6</sup>I will tell you, handsome woman. This is the enchanting Section of the Boat.[^bb-8]
‘Invisible to all the people, a form of Mahāmāyā, <sup>7</sup>made of all kinds of gems, pure, a form of Prakṛti herself, surrounded by geese and ducks, frequented by bees, <sup>8</sup>filled with pure chowries, resounding with the tinkling of ankle bells, perfumed with various scents, delightful, greatest mistress, <sup>9</sup>awarding *dharma*, *artha* and *kāma*, my dear, providing *mokṣa*, assuming various forms, my dear, surrounded by divine women, <sup>10</sup>at every moment assuming different forms, sometimes white, sometimes red, <sup>11</sup>sometimes yellow, sometimes black, sometimes green and sometimes many-colored [p. 297] – <sup>12</sup>this boat of many forms is Kālī herself, my dear. Such a boat appeared by itself, my dear, on the Yamunā, great mistress, in Mathurā, in the pasture lands.

*Īśvara continued:* <sup>13</sup>‘When this great boat had appeared, she saw a dream in the night. Together with Padminī, Kṛṣṇa also saw a dream in the night. <sup>14</sup>Greatest mistress, my dear, Mahāmāyā appeared in the night and said something to Kṛṣṇa and to Rādhikā.’

*‘Kālikā said:* <sup>15</sup>“Listen, child, mighty-armed, lotus-eyed one! You have attained perfection. My child, I am Kālī in the form of a boat; it is in no other way. <sup>16</sup>Son, I will remain in the middle stream of the Yamunā for three days. My son, enjoy yourself with Rādhā and do recitations, <sup>17</sup>and I will then award you, my child, wonderful happiness.”
‘Having said this, Mahāmāyā Kālī, the mistress of Vṛndāvana, disappeared at the time of his coming together with Padminī.
<sup>18</sup>‘Then these choice women returned to their houses at the end of the night. <sup>19</sup>Then mighty-armed Kṛṣṇa took on another body. Having created another for the house of the cowherd Nanda, Hari left. <sup>20</sup>Goddess! Immediately Kṛṣṇa, of eyes like lotus petals, went to the great boat that was a form of Kālī and that had come to the edge of the royal road. <sup>21</sup>Having quickly come there, he of eyes like lotus petals bowed down to the great boat together with Sudāman and the others.
<sup>22</sup>‘Greatest mistress! Hari climbed on and then recited his cherished *vidyā*; having recited it, towards the end of the night, Hari then played his flute. <sup>23</sup>My dear, this flute that bewilders the world is Mahākālī herself. Mistress of the gods, with one syllable, he played a sweet tune. <sup>24</sup>That one syllable is the Kūrca seed,[^bc-8] enchanting the minds of women. Playing the flute, my dear, Kṛṣṇa recited his cherished *vidyā*.
<sup>25</sup>‘At this moment, goddess, my dear, the sun arose. Together with all his associates, Kṛṣṇa Hari performed his morning rituals, <sup>26</sup>recited his cherished *vidyā*, composed of the complete Brahman, re-entered the boat and enjoyed himself with the boat.
<sup>27</sup>‘Playing different flutes and then also his horn, Kṛṣṇa Hari, of eyes like lotus petals, bowed to Kātyāyanī, <sup>28</sup>handsome woman, and then enjoyed many kinds of games with the boat.
‘Goddess! At that time, together with her friends, Padminī Rādhā, enchantress of the universe, <sup>29</sup>having the colour of asparagus flowers, casting various sidelong glances and smiling, beautiful one, <sup>30</sup>filled a jewelled pot with nectar. Goddess, fair-complexioned one! Under the pretext of selling cow products, <sup>31</sup>she took hold of Candrāvalī and quickly collected some cow [p. 298] products. Goddess, from the house of Vṛkabhānu came together with Padminī <sup>32</sup>others by the thousands, goddess, surrounding Rādhikā at all times, decorated with erotic clothing and with trembling, restless eyes. <sup>33</sup>Greatest mistress, the fragrance from the mouths of these cowherdesses enchanted all of the gods, Gandharvas and Kinnaras.
<sup>34</sup>‘Sweet one! Now listen to the most confidential secret of what she said to Kṛṣṇa after she had reached the boat!’

## Twenty-fourth Paṭala

*Pārvatī said:* <sup>1</sup>‘This is a great secret, the supreme Clan practice! Please tell it to me, greatest master, ocean of compassion!’

*Īśvara said:* <sup>2</sup>‘Listen, dear Pārvatī, and I will tell you the supreme truth of Padminī, most confidential, greatly meritorious and never to be revealed. <sup>3</sup>All of this, great mistress, is your unfathomable play – your play is difficult to approach, but it always nourishes Kṛṣṇa.
<sup>4</sup>‘Rādhikā Padminī is Lord Kṛṣṇa’s *vāgbhavā.*[^bd-8] Kṛṣṇa with eyes like lotus petals has come from a part of Vāsudeva. <sup>5</sup>My dear, Padminī is always the *vāgbhavā* of this Kṛṣṇa.
‘Padminī, fragrant as a lotus, quickly came there. <sup>6</sup>After the lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa has recited his cherished *vidyā* there on the path, he recited the one-syllable Kūrca *vidyā* that enchants Kṛṣṇa. <sup>7</sup>All the inhabitants of Vraja incessantly recited the Kūrca. By the grace of Kātyāyanī, all these inhabitants of Vraja <sup>8</sup>constantly recited the Kūrca that awards the four goals of life.
‘Great mistress, when Padminī had gone there along the path – <sup>9</sup>the royal road, great mistress, decorated with various gems, shaded by Kadamba trees, beautified with Tamāla groves, <sup>10</sup>filled with various birds, adorned with various ornaments – by this royal road by the Kālindī Padminī, fragrant as a lotus, <sup>11</sup>great mistress, saw there the boat adorned with gems. In her mind, she bowed to this boat called Brahmapravāhinī <sup>12</sup>and then, lotus-eyed one, incessantly recited the great Kūrca seed. Goddess Pārvatī! At this moment, the mother of the world, the world itself, extended her bewildering Prākṛta Māyā.’

<sup>13</sup>*‘Padminī said:* “Hey Kṛṣṇa, listen carefully to my words! Mighty-armed son of Devakī! You are Nanda’s son. I have come here from Gokula. May all be well with you. Son of the cowherd, quickly take me to the other shore.” [p. 299] <sup>14</sup>*’Kṛṣṇa replied:* ”Come here, deer-eyed woman! Tell me where you are going. What stuff do you have in that jewelled pot – yoghurt, milk or ghee?” <sup>15</sup>Pārvatī! So saying, Kṛṣṇa forcefully took hold of Rādhā. Then, lotus-eyed one, mighty-armed Kṛṣṇa and all the cowherdesses quickly led Rādhikā into the boat.

<sup>16</sup>*‘Kṛṣṇa said:* “Listen, wise woman, to my words: give me toll, my dear. Without toll, I will never cross over.”

<sup>17</sup>*‘Rādhikā said:* “Listen, mighty-armed, lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa! Whose toll are you asking me for? Who has made you a chief, and when?”

<sup>18</sup>*‘Kṛṣṇa replied:* “How does it concern you by whom and when I was made a chief? I am certainly the toll collector for King Kaṃsa, lord of men. <sup>19</sup>Therefore, deer-eyed woman, I am a toll collector; it is in no other way, when buying or selling, coming or going, <sup>20</sup>drinking the water of the Yamunā or getting into a boat. Dear one, I also collect the toll of youth. <sup>21</sup>For general youth I take a hundred thousand gold, but the youth I see in you is rarely found in the three worlds. I will take this and that of the choice cowherdesses.”

*‘Candrāvalī said:* <sup>22</sup>“Listen, mighty-armed Kṛṣṇa! Take us to the other shore in the ordinary way. Cowherd boy! By the order of Cowherd Nanda, there is no toll in Vraja. <sup>23</sup>Gopāla, Śyāmasundara! Noble Nanda is your father. He is virtuous, truthful and devoted to all *dharma*. <sup>24</sup>When your mother Yaśodā hears what you have been saying, Kṛṣṇa, she will beat you with her hand. Take us to the other shore, Kṛṣṇa, if you mind your own best.”

*‘Kṛṣṇa replied:* <sup>25</sup>“Deer-eyed woman! Give me toll for all of your cow products. Quickly also give me toll for your youth, one after the other, <sup>26</sup>on your bosom rest hidden jewels, and that jewelled locket, very valuable and extremely rare. <sup>27</sup>You are a thief, deer-eyed woman! Tell me where you are going. Whose wealth have you stolen, my dear, very valuable and enchanting!
“My dear, my mind burns when I see what is resting on your bosom, <sup>28</sup>that jewel on your chest that enchants the three worlds. <sup>29</sup>Whose mind would not burn to see that jewel? Woman of lovely thighs, whom do you all belong to? Tell me where you are going. <sup>30</sup>Since a long time back, I am a great toll collector; we are under the command of Kaṃsa. Today, beautiful one, all of you women of restless eyes have fallen into my hands.
<sup>31</sup>“My dear, where did you get this jewel that you carry on your chest, shining and red like a ruby, and why are you going to Mathurā? <sup>32</sup>My heart is convinced that you certainly must be a thief, my dear, for rubies and all other jewels never have any fragrance, my friend, <sup>33</sup>but the jewel on your chest is very fragrant! This jewel is called Kāmasandīpana (Enflamer of love), and it enchants the three worlds. <sup>34</sup>Beautiful one! It is called Saubhāgyavardhana (Increaser of fortune), it has the colour of aloe and it makes you smell of all kinds of flowers. <sup>35</sup>You have carefully covered this locket with a tightly tied [p. 300] delightful bodice, coloured like Kadamba buds and decorated with various wonderful patterns.”

*‘Kṛṣṇa continued:* <sup>36</sup>“Listen, my dear, beloved friend of Candrāvalī! Where did you get this bodice? Whose is it? I am certain you are a thief. <sup>37</sup>Tell me the truth, deer-eyed woman! Where are you going and where did you get it? Today I will take away all of this, all the many jewels and everything. <sup>38</sup>All these women have become like thieves. Sudāma and Vasudāman! I am sure that they are thieves.”
<sup>39</sup>‘When Padminī, fragrant as a lotus, heard these words, she angrily pressed together her lips and then spoke some select words.’

## Twenty-fifth Paṭala

*Pārvatī said:* <sup>1</sup>‘What did Padminī do when she heard these words of Kṛṣṇa? Please reveal all the details of this secret, Lord of gods!’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>2</sup>‘Listen, dear Pārvatī, and I will tell you what Padminī said, the harsh words that she said to Kṛṣṇa in the boat, handsome woman!

*‘Padminī said:* <sup>3</sup>“Listen, son of Nanda, Kṛṣṇa! You are Hari, the son of Yaśodā. Listen, son of Devakī, Kṛṣṇa, son of Vasudeva! <sup>4</sup>You never have any fortune, for you were born in the family of cowherds. You are Nanda’s thriving son, but you constantly steal milk and butter. <sup>5</sup>You are never blissful, you never perform any virtuous deeds. You have no mother, no father, no friend, no follower or anyone else; <sup>6</sup>there is no beginning or end to you, fool! You know no shame. You never feel any shame, fool, you always cling to others, <sup>7</sup>you make love to others’ wives, for you love that which belongs to other people. Hey cowherd! You always harm others and you always dress up like others. <sup>8</sup>You were born in the family of Kṣatriyas, but now you stay in Nanda’s house, always wandering about with the cows and constantly hanging out with the cowherds. <sup>9</sup>You like milking the cows but still you always steal milk and butter. You kill cows, you kill birds, you are a killer of women and a sinner − after all, you are a cowherd. What more can I say?”

*‘Kṛṣṇa said:* <sup>10</sup>“Whatever you have said is true; your words are not false – but give me the toll, deer-eyed one! I will never give up.”

*‘Padminī said:* <sup>11</sup>‘The king of this land is Kaṃsa and he holds fast to the truth. When truthful Kaṃsa is the king, <sup>12</sup>why would I ever give you any toll, cowherd? Kaṃsa is the sovereign of this world, the best of kings and the abode of all good qualities. <sup>13</sup>Under his government, I will not give any toll. He will kill anyone who asks people for toll!”

*‘Kṛṣṇa replied:* <sup>14</sup>“Deer-eyed woman, I see a gem that shines like a fixed bolt of lightning at the tip of your nose. Quickly now give that to me as toll. <sup>15</sup>Give me the toll, deer-eyed one, and go to Mathurā, beautiful one! Otherwise I will just steal the gem – and your clothes.” [p. 301] *‘Rādhikā replied:* <sup>16</sup>“A cowherd has always and at all times so many faults. Listen, cowherd, you fool, to what is proper for my gem. <sup>17</sup>This gem at my heart enchants the three worlds, my budlike breast is a form of the highest Brahman <sup>18</sup>and my bodice is Kuṇḍalī herself, a form of Śabdabrahman. Cowherd! You see this pearl at the tip of my nose <sup>19</sup>and this Kaustubha on my chest, now listen! This gem at my heart generates my pearl, cowherd. <sup>20</sup>This pearl, Kṛṣṇa, is eternally the heroine Citriṇī; these breasts, Kṛṣṇa, are the drunk enchantress Gandhinī; <sup>21</sup>and this bodice is Hastinī of tight fetters.<sup>†ǁ</sup>
‘“Listen Kṛṣṇa, great fool! I am Padminī Rādhikā, <sup>22</sup>the Kalāvatī garland itself that hangs around her[^cx-8] neck. Hey cowherd, we are all her beautiful attendants. <sup>23</sup>You are fickleminded; therefore you do not know yourself – you are always fickle, Kṛṣṇa, attracted to others’ women, and these unfortunate fools are fond of your company!”

*‘Kṛṣṇa said:* <sup>24</sup>“Smiling lotus-face! I will ask you one thing, Padminī. When I see your lotuslike face and the pearl at the tip of your nose that shines like a fixed bolt of lightning, <sup>25</sup>I blaze up with passion. The pearl at the tip of your nose inflames passion!”

## Twenty-sixth Paṭala

*‘Rādhikā said:* <sup>1</sup>“This pearl, Kṛṣṇa, has the form of the seed of the three worlds, for one cannot describe the greatness of this pearl. <sup>2</sup>Kṛṣṇa, this pearl is a form of Mahāmāyā; in this pearl reside millions and millions of universes. <sup>3</sup>Best of cowherds, Hari, it is through extremely good fortune that I have gained this pearl – I have gained the pearl by worshipping Tripurā’s feet.” Having said this to Kṛṣṇa, Padminī Rādhā ceased.

*‘Kṛṣṇa said:* <sup>4</sup>“Listen to my words Rādhikā, loving one! Please tell me about this pearl, Rādhā, Cupid’s temple! <sup>5</sup>Your nose, handsome woman, is always Cupid’s quiver, and the corner of your eyes completely eradicates my *karman*. <sup>6</sup>Touching your limbs, beautiful one, destroys all diseases; your body, beautiful one, is liquid nectar, increasing love! <sup>7</sup>The full moon, beautiful one, eternally stays here in the form of your moonlike nails; at the edge of your nails, delicate one, rests a fixed bolt of lightning. <sup>8</sup>Give me your embrace, beautiful one, lift me up, for I have fallen! Save me from an ocean of sin, dear, beautiful one! I am your servant.”

*‘Rādhikā replied:* <sup>9</sup>“Listen to my words, beautiful Kṛṣṇa, mighty-armed one! Immediately worship Śiva, then auspicious Kātyāyanī, <sup>10</sup>and at the end, best of men, your cherished, eternal *vidyā*, a complete form of great Kālī, the [p. 302] unobstructed Sarasvatī.[^ca-8] <sup>11</sup>This boat, difficult to approach, is always a form of Kālī, and all of these associates are known as great Bhairavas; they are well-born, knowledgeable in Tantra and devoted to the Clan practice.”’

*Īśvara continued:* <sup>12</sup>‘Hearing her speak in this way, lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa worshipped an earthen Liṅga and then auspicious Kātyāyanī. <sup>13</sup>The goddess, the mother of the world, the world itself, was then pleased with him; the goddess herself appeared for Kṛṣṇa’s welfare.

*‘Kātyāyanī said:* <sup>14</sup>“Listen Kṛṣṇa, mighty-armed one, my son! Ask for a boon. Ask for a boon! May you be blessed! I will give you an incomparable boon.”

*‘Kṛṣṇa said:* <sup>15</sup>“I bow to you, dear to Śaṅkara! Give me a boon, Mahāmāyā, by which my *vidyā*, Kālī, Brahman itself, may be perfected.”

*‘Kātyāyanī said:* <sup>16</sup>“Just so it will be, Kṛṣṇa, Mādhava, through union with Rādhā. Make great efforts to do what Rādhā has said, Kṛṣṇa. <sup>17</sup>Kṛṣṇa! Through union with Rādhā you will certainly extract the Flower, and this Flower is of three types, Kṛṣṇa. The Drop of the Pond is best of all, but <sup>18</sup>the Selfborn is also delightful and an increaser of all kinds of happiness.[^cb-8] It gives *dharma*, *kāma, artha* and *mokṣa –* <sup>19</sup>through union with Rādhā you will attain the Flower that awards the four goals of life. This is the doctrine of the Clan, Kṛṣṇa, accompanied by recitations and worship.
<sup>20</sup>‘“Mahākālī Mahāmāyā is your cherished, eternal goddess. Incessantly recite the Kālī mantra as explained in the Clan together with Rādhā. <sup>21</sup>What I have told you, pervasive one, is a most confidential secret. This supreme secret is beyond even Brahmā and the other gods. <sup>22</sup>Whatever else pertains to the Clan vow, mighty-armed Kṛṣṇa, all of that you will hear from the mouth of Padminī, from Rādhā. <sup>23</sup>Without the Clan vow, my son, there can be no perfection. Listen, Kṛṣṇa, mighty-armed one: recite Kālikā’s mantra!”
‘Having said this, Mahāmāyā disappeared.’

## Twenty-seventh Paṭala

*‘Padminī said:* <sup>1</sup>“Listen Kṛṣṇa, you who dress like a cowherd, to this supreme statement! Whatever can be seen on your body, lotus-eyed one, <sup>2</sup>belongs to [p. 303] someone else; nothing is yours, beautiful one. Tell me where you got these ornaments, Kṛṣṇa! <sup>3</sup>You are always dependent on others, lotus-eyed one. Where did you then get this dark body, mighty-armed one, decked with all kinds of ornaments? Tell me the truth, Keśava!”

*ṛṣṇa replied:* <sup>4</sup>“Listen, doe-eyed Rādhā to my words full of meaning! This body, delicate one, decorated with all kinds of clothing, <sup>5</sup>this form of mine, shining dark as ground collyrium – all of this, deer-eyed one, comes from worshipping the feet of Tripurā. <sup>6</sup>This body is a verbal form of Kālī herself, for without a body, beautiful one, Brahman is like a dead corpse. <sup>7</sup>By constant, devoted worship of Tripurā, lotus-eyed one, I also always have a body, my dear, deer-eyed one, <sup>8</sup>and today only, by the touch of your feet on me, it has become meaningful. All of this, deer-eyed one, comes from worshipping the feet of Tripurā.
<sup>9</sup>‘“By the grace of Tripurā, I will become Śabdabrahman. I have no mother, no father, no friend and no relative; <sup>10</sup>I am alone, unsullied, subtle, devoid of cause and effect. By devoted worship of Tripurā, I have now attained a body. <sup>11</sup>By the worship of Tripurā, nothing is impossible for me!
‘“This flag, thunderbolt, hook[^cc-8] and whatever else you see on my body,
<sup>12</sup>beautiful one, all of this is a form of Mahāmāyā. My lock of hair, my earrings, the pearl at the tip of my nose, <sup>13</sup>my bracelets, necklace, flutes and this yellow *dhoṭī*, beautiful one, is the highest mistress Kuṇḍalī. <sup>14</sup>Whatever is on my body, true or false, beautiful one, all of this, deer-eyed one, is Mahāmāyā, the world itself.
<sup>15</sup>‘“Myself, deer-eyed one, I am always bereft of senses. The peacock feather in my hair, sweet one, <sup>16</sup>is the *yoni*, the Mahāmudrā that I have placed on my head and carry around.[^cd-8] This incomparable body is sheltered in Mahāmāyā. <sup>17</sup>Māyā Jagaddhātrī, the world itself, is my shelter. Having pervaded this shelter I eternally remain here, lotus-eyed one.
<sup>18</sup>‘“My dear, of eyes long as fishes, this incomparable gem called Kaustubha, my disk, club, conch and lotus – <sup>19</sup>all is a form of the highest mistress Prakṛti, deer-eyed one.
‘“Give me your embrace, beautiful one; Cupid burns me!”

*ādhikā said:* <sup>20</sup>“Listen Kṛṣṇa, mighty-armed one! You have taken on the form of a human with two arms. One with a human form can never have congress with me. <sup>21</sup>You are a human, best of men, and I am Prakṛti Padminī. At the tip of my nose is a garland of rubies. <sup>22</sup>Carefully see the secret of this pearl within it: this is a supreme secret, always extremely confidential!”’ [p. 304] *Īśvara continued:* <sup>23</sup>‘Listen carefully, fortunate one, to the most confidential secret that she told Kṛṣṇa! <sup>24</sup>“Listen, Kṛṣṇa, you are a human and you always find your pleasure among humans. Intelligent one! Drink the nectar of the jewelled pot, <sup>25</sup>for one who recites the supreme mantra of Kālī without the nectar, Kṛṣṇa, will lose everything and at the end become an animal.”
