<sup>26</sup>‘When Kṛṣṇa heard her words, he immediately drunk this wonderful nectar that gives *dharma*, *kāma*, *artha* and *mokṣa*, <sup>27</sup>and having drunk Kṛṣṇa immediately started reciting the Kālikā mantra. The nectar of the jewelled pot is the wine called Kādambarī.<sup>‡ǁ</sup> After drinking the Kādambarī wine, Kṛṣṇa recited the mantra with a concentrated mind.

*‘Rādhikā said:* <sup>28</sup>“See Kṛṣṇa, mighty-armed one, you have now become the master of all givers of toll! See the power of my pearl, lotus-eyed one!”
<sup>29</sup>’At that moment, Rādhā Padminī, fragrant as a lotus, bowed her head to Kālī, the beautiful mother of Brahman, <sup>30</sup>recited the mantra of this beautiful mother of Kṛṣṇa, the very form of liberation, and praised her.
“Look, look, mighty-armed one, at the supreme position of the pearl!”
<sup>31</sup>‘From the pearl, great mistress, immediately appeared a cosmic egg, and in this egg, great mistress, were millions and millions of heaps of Kṛṣṇas. <sup>32</sup>Seeing this, greatest mistress, Kṛṣṇa was bewildered. Greatest mistress, fair-complexioned one, <sup>33</sup>the splendour of the toenails of one Kṛṣṇa of this egg covered Kṛṣṇa and remained in him.
‘My dear, by the power of the Mahāvidyā, Kṛṣṇa regained his calm. <sup>34</sup>My dear, goddess Padminī then all at once contracted the egg, and then this Rādhā dissolved this universe into the pearl. <sup>35</sup>In this way, handsome woman, through the worship of Tripurā’s feet, she showed millions of universal eggs to Kṛṣṇa.
<sup>36</sup>‘“Hey Kṛṣṇa, two-armed god! What did you see in my pearl? What did the two-armed Hari see in the pearl that Tripurā gave me? <sup>37</sup>Look at something else, hey Kṛṣṇa, at the marks on the pearl, Hari!”
‘Daughter of the mountain! From the pearl came another pearl, <sup>38</sup>my dear goddess; all at once appeared millions of pearls, and Kṛṣṇa, fair-complexioned one, saw this greatly wonderful feat. <sup>39</sup>Lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa reproached himself.
‘Mistress of the gods, one pearl created millions of eggs, <sup>40</sup>and then, Pārvatī, contracted them, one after the other. Mistress of the gods! One pearl created <sup>41</sup>millions of manifold pearls. One after the other created and then dissolved all of them, lotus-eyed mistress of the gods! <sup>42</sup>The pearl at the tip of her nose contracted all of them and remained there. This pearl eternally remains at the tip of her nose like bound-up lightning. [p. 305] <sup>43</sup>‘Once more, listen carefully to a great wonder! The pearl at the end of her nose contracted the boat, beautiful mistress of the gods, <sup>44</sup>together with Kṛṣṇa and the cowherdesses, together with Sudāman and the cowherd boys, goddess of eyes long like fishes, <sup>45</sup>it contracted into the pearl the whole universe, moving and unmoving. Pārvatī! There it showed itself together with Padminī. <sup>46</sup>Greatest mistress! Lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa saw there the village of Vṛndāvana, but no other Seats.
<sup>47</sup>‘Handsome woman, greatest mistress, then the pearl manifested the boat and again rested on her again as before. <sup>48</sup>Seeing this, goddess, Kṛṣṇa’s amazement disappeared. Seeing this unparalleled wonder, he rebuked himself. <sup>49</sup>He recited the supreme *vidyā* of enchanting great Kālī. Seeing Rādhikā’s face, he recited Kālikā’s mantra. <sup>50</sup>Seeing Rādhā’s body, beautiful one, lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa incessantly recited the *vidyā* of unobstructed Sarasvatī.’

## Twenty-eighth Paṭala

*Īśvara continued:* <sup>1</sup>‘In this way, sweetly smiling one, Kṛṣṇa’s Clan practice led on towards the Flower of the Drop of the Pond. <sup>2</sup>The substance of the Drop of the Pond is extremely rare in the three worlds. I will tell you what Padminī Rādhā told Kṛṣṇa!

*‘Rādhikā said:* <sup>3</sup>“Listen Kṛṣṇa, mighty-armed one, to what I say for your benefit! Kṛṣṇa, through knowledge I have fixed myself on that which is above Vāsudeva. <sup>4</sup>If you can take on the body of Vāsudeva, Kṛṣṇa, then my great Prakṛti will appear, and <sup>5</sup>I will immediately give you my love, Kṛṣṇa. Otherwise, lotus-eyed one, I will think of you as a human, and <sup>6</sup>I can never have congress with humans or animals. Lotus-eyed one! If I have congress with a human being, <sup>7</sup>your mother Tripurā will be very upset and immediately burn me to ashes, Kṛṣṇa, like a bunch of straw and sticks!”
<sup>8</sup>‘Mistress of the gods! When Kṛṣṇa of eyes like lotus petals heard these words of hers, he fixed his mind on the lotus feet of Kālikā. <sup>9</sup>Reciting the supreme *vidyā*, he attained his true form. Daughter of the mountain, Kṛṣṇa of eyes like lotus petals again assumed the form that Vāsudeva previously had had.

*‘Vāsudeva said:* <sup>10</sup>“Padminī, listen to my words! Why do you speak contrarily to me? He who is Kṛṣṇa is Vāsudeva, me – I am eternally Mahāviṣṇu. <sup>11</sup>To conceal myself I have taken on a two-armed form, sweet one, not for any other reason. For your sake, brightly-coloured one,[^cx-9] have I performed terrible austerities. <sup>12</sup>By this true *dharma* I should attain union with Padminī, for [p. 306] without union with you, Rādhā, how can there be any perfection of the *vidyā*? Order me, beautiful one, and I will again assume my human body.”

*‘Padminī said:* <sup>13</sup>“Vāsudeva, mighty-armed one! Now become a human. I am satisfied with you, pervasive one! I can see the fruit of your austerities.”
‘Hearing her speak in this way, Hari took on his two-armed form.

*‘Padminī said:* <sup>14</sup>“Listen, Kṛṣṇa, mighty-armed one! You are indeed Vāsudeva! You are without a doubt Śiva, Kṛṣṇa! You are Śiva, darkly handsome one! <sup>15</sup>This dark body of yours is nothing but Kālikā’s body.
”Listen to the truth, mighty-armed one, to a secret most confidential! <sup>16</sup>I am eternally Tripurā’s *dūtī*, the supreme part of Padminī, and my *yoni*, lotus-eyed one, is forever unsullied. <sup>17</sup>Darkbodied one! In order to perfect the Flower and the Drop of the Pond, you should release your semen in my *yoni* and nowhere else. <sup>18</sup>Mighty-armed one! Then the Flower will perfect itself.”
‘When Kṛṣṇa heard her speak in this way, he spoke these words: <sup>19</sup>“Padminī, lotus-face, I have become your servant.”
‘This supreme Padminī was pleased when she heard Kṛṣṇa’s words.
<sup>20</sup>‘Padminī, fragrant as a lotus, stands on the left side of Kṛṣṇa in the night of the full moon in Kārtika by the Yamunā river. <sup>21</sup>Rādhā is decorated with all kinds of erotic garments; she is an enchanting form of love, supremely cunning and an expert in the battle of passionate love. <sup>22</sup>Goddess Pārvatī! Kṛṣṇa Vāsudeva is like Cupid himself, and their meeting is like a stroke of lightning. <sup>23</sup>Goddess! Rādhā is always lightning, Kṛṣṇa is like an emerald mountain. My dear goddess, she is like a red bolt of lightning.
<sup>24</sup>‘Rādhā is adolescent, Hari is adolescent; their meeting, goddess, is like a stroke of lightning. <sup>25</sup>Kṛṣṇa is an emerald mountain, Rādhā a fixed bolt of lightning; in the middle of the full moon night of Kārtika they have entered the boat.
<sup>26</sup>‘With various foodstuffs he worshipped Kālī, liberator from the world, then he mentally recited the *vidyā*, full of the nectar of passionate love,
<sup>27</sup>and then came the embracing and all the rest described in the Tantras, lotus-eyed one.
‘Daughter of the mountain! At pleasure, he pounded Rādhā’s *yoni*, <sup>28</sup>after he had worshipped this house of love with sandalwood pulp, flowers and so on, for Rādhā’s house of love increased Kṛṣṇa’s fortune. <sup>29</sup>He began at dusk and finished at the end of the night, but then Padminī Rādhā disappeared. <sup>30</sup>She mentally bowed down to Kālī and then immediately left for her own abode.
‘Greatest mistress! At this time goddess Kālī, Mahāmāyā, the world itself, appeared before Kṛṣṇa.

*‘Kālikā said:* <sup>31</sup>“Listen Kṛṣṇa, mighty-armed one! Your great exertions have now brought you perfection. By worshipping Tripurā’s feet, Padminī is supremely [p. 307] fortunate. <sup>32</sup>My son, I will now withdraw the boat and return to my abode. Best of sons, by your fortune and great efforts you have now attained everything: <sup>33</sup>perfection in the Pond, perfection in the *yoni* and also the Selfborn. Now, my son, go on and perform your final pastimes with the cowherdesses; <sup>34</sup>speak with them in various ways and wander about as you wish.”
‘Saying this, this Mahāmāyā disappeared.’

## Twenty-ninth Paṭala

*Īśvara said:* ‘After this, mighty-armed Kṛṣṇa went to the house of cowherd Nanda. This Janārdana himself took on many adolescent bodies, <sup>2</sup>day after day, great mistress. Handsome woman, he played with all the cowherd women, <sup>3</sup>laughing, embracing them and pounding their *yonis*, day after day, together with his people.
<sup>4</sup>‘Kṛṣṇa, of eyes like lotus petals, went to the bank of the Kālindī river. Playing his horn, his different flutes and shouting, <sup>5</sup>Kṛṣṇa called out “Rādhā! Rādhā! Where have you gone, dear, beautiful Rādhā? I am your servant. <sup>6</sup>I have fallen head over heels into an ocean of grief. Look at me once again, lotus-eyed one! <sup>7</sup>I have fallen into a fire lit by lust. Where have you gone, my love? You have thrown me into an ocean of fire – where have you gone?”
<sup>8</sup>‘In this way Keśava spoke in many different ways in the company of his people in a grove by the Yamunā, beautified by Aśoka trees and fresh blossoms. <sup>9</sup>Kṛṣṇa, of eyes like lotus petals, enjoyed himself in the circle of Vraja. Handsome woman! He killed the demoniac Kaṃsa and others in Mathurā. <sup>10</sup>After some days, my dear, Kṛṣṇa gave up the city of Mathurā and quickly went to Dvārakā, my dear, where Dvārāvatī stays.
<sup>11</sup>‘Mistress of the gods! Dvārāvatī is Mahiṣamardinī[^da-8] herself, a city made of gold and extending over a hundred *yojanas* <sup>12</sup>and where Kuṇḍalinī herself is the ocean all around. In its nine hundred thousand houses embellished with glittering gold, <sup>13</sup>in this city shining with the nine jewels and splendid all around, dwell eternally spring and all the seasons, goddess, greatest mistress, and the Witness in her partial form. <sup>14</sup>There stay eternally nine hundred thousand Śiva temples with hundreds of walls made of pure gold, <sup>15</sup>studded with diamonds, cat’s eye gems and rubies, made by all the gods, <sup>16</sup>overspread with Apsaras, frequented by gods and Gandharvas. <sup>17</sup>There in Dvārakā, sweetly smiling one, stay forever one million two hundred thousand wonderful Śakti temples. There in Dvārakā she stays, mistress of the gods, sweetly smiling one. [p. 308] <sup>18</sup>’Splendid goddess, this Dvārakā city is made of all Śaktis; this charmingly perfumed city is surrounded by a hundred walls <sup>19</sup>and stretches over an area of ten *yojanas*, charmingly perfumed with different scents. In its middle, greatest mistress, is an uppermost area of five *yojanas*, <sup>20</sup>shining with huge emeralds and adorned with various wonderful decorations, named after the eternal Lotus-dweller, greatest mistress. <sup>21</sup>In its middle, great mistress, is an uppermost area of three *yojanas*, shining with rubies and adorned with various wonderful decorations. <sup>22</sup>Above it, greatest mistress, my dear, is the open hall Candra. This open hall, handsome woman, is decorated with garlands of pearls, <sup>23</sup>it has white chowries held by thousands of people of the four classes. This open hall, great mistress, shines with millions of moonbeams.
<sup>24</sup>‘Within these three *yojanas* is a great area of one *yojana*. That area is made of eternal bliss, it always contains both Śiva and Śakti. <sup>25</sup>An area measuring twelve of my forearms is the uppermost land, red like a hibuscus flower, barley salt or vermillion, lotus-eyed one. <sup>26</sup>This land is made from the space between your lips, fair-complexioned one, for your Māyā, daughter of the mountain, is always hard to approach. <sup>27</sup>Having surrounded this place of eternal bliss, Mādhava created this city. In this city of eternal bliss <sup>28</sup>resides the great Māyā, Yoganidrā, the form of consciousness and bliss, the mother of the world.
‘It is surrounded by a splendid wall of a hundred sections, resembling a coiled snake, <sup>29</sup>a hundred *yojanas* long, four *yojanas* tall and having four gates; Kuṇḍalī Prakṛti herself.
<sup>30</sup>‘What more can I say, goddess? At each and every crossing, greatest mistress, are billions of Śiva temples.
<sup>31</sup>’Goddess, greatest mistress, there is also a wonderful temple of jewels for Bhavānī, always full of Śiva and Śakti. <sup>32</sup>Furnished with golden pots, decorated with white chowries, filled with the tingling of anklebells and pitchers shining like the sun, <sup>33</sup>delightful with millions and millions of houses made of pure gold, this building is always surrounded by a wall, greatest mistress. <sup>34</sup>This building is a dwelling for the houses of the Brahmins and the others, sweetly smiling one, my dear, one after the other inside the wall.
<sup>35</sup>The wall, greatest mistress, is Prakṛti Kuṇḍalinī herself. Kuṇḍalinī herself has surrounded this Seat of eternal bliss. <sup>36</sup>This Seat of eternal bliss is made of *rajas*, *sattva* and *tamas*. If one were to give up this place of eternal bliss, where could one find Brahmā or Hari?
<sup>37</sup>‘Where this Seat of eternal bliss exists, highest mistress, there is an auspicious house inlaid with all kinds of jewels, <sup>38</sup>always meditated on by Brahmā and others, furnished with a Seat of Union and eternally praised by great yogins and by Sanaka and others.
<sup>39</sup>‘Lord Kṛṣṇa’s building surrounds the house of the Seat of Union. This house measures nine hundred thousand and is made of gold. <sup>40</sup>Greatest [p. 309] mistress! Who can count the relatives and others, the houses of the relatives and friends of Kṛṣṇa? <sup>41</sup>Who will count them, goddess? I have just given a brief description.
‘Like this, great mistress, is the eternal city of Dvārāvatī, <sup>42</sup>and Dvārāvatī, great mistress, is a form of Prakṛti herself. Great mistress! There lives Kṛṣṇa of eyes like lotus petals. The lotus-eyed one heard everything from the mouth of Nārada.’

## Thirtieth Paṭala

*Īśvara continued:* <sup>1</sup>‘My dear goddess, when Nārada, the best of ascetics, heard the story of Kṛṣṇa from the people, he desired to see this Kṛṣṇa. <sup>2</sup>He immediately went to Gokula, to Mathurā in the circle of Vraja, and when he saw Kṛṣṇa, with eyes like lotus petals, he said some words to him.

*‘Nārada said:* <sup>3</sup>“Welfare to you, best of Yādavas. I have come to take shelter of you.” ‘When Kṛṣṇa heard this, he welcomed him by washing his feet and so on. <sup>4</sup>“I bow to you, best of ascetics, I bow to you, best of the twice-born! I bow to you, honourable one, I bow to you, I bow to you!”

*‘Nārada said:* <sup>5</sup>”Listen carefully to why I have come, best of the Yadu clan! Keśava, lord of Lakṣmī, you are complete by your own bliss. <sup>6</sup>Lord of gods, I have one question; please answer me, master! Kṛṣṇa, this enchanting hair of yours bewilders the worlds; <sup>7</sup>this yellow and enchanting *tilaka* Dṛṣṭimohana, Kṛṣṇa, always resembles a yellow bolt of lightning; <sup>8</sup>this flute Bhuvanamohinī is perpetually playful; your signet ring is Ratnamukhī, Kṛṣṇa; your clothes shining as gold, <sup>9</sup>darling of the Yadus, are called Niga māgamasaṃyukta;[^db-8] your waist belt is Kalavākāra;[^dc-8] your ankle bells are called Haṃsagañjana; <sup>10</sup>your necklace is called Tārāvalī; your pearl necklace, Taḍitprabhā; and the gem that shines on your chest, Kṛṣṇa, is called Kaustubha. <sup>11</sup>Your enchanting and delightful hair, cultivated and wonderful, is an embodied form of Mahāvidyā, staying in your hair <sup>12</sup>decorated with a peacock feather and most wonderful; the ribbon that binds your hair and resembles a fixed bolt of lightning <sup>13</sup>I always see as Kuṇḍalinī herself and as nothing else. Master, Mahāmāyā, the enchantress of the cultivated ones, <sup>14</sup>the supreme part of Prakṛti, stays in the form of a *yoni* in the middle of a peacock feather. The garland of Mallikā and Mālatī flowers is surrounded by bumblebees <sup>15</sup>that intoxicated by wine sing ‘Rādhā! Rādhā!’; this quivering hair of yours, beautified by flocks of cuckoos, <sup>16</sup>is surrounded by masses of white, red, yellow, black, green and multicoloured cuckoos. Darling of the Yadus, <sup>17</sup>when I see this hair, best of Yadus, my heart burns, for where did you get this hair that enchants the world forever? <sup>18</sup>Offspring of Yadu, there I can see a [p. 310] wonder for the bumblebees, beautified by all the young women of Vraja, <sup>19</sup>all the women of Vraja in the form of cuckoos.[^dd-8] Offspring of Yadu! If you are unchangeable, Lord, why then do women always surround you?
<sup>20</sup>‘The rings that can be seen in your ears, Lord Hari, is the supreme wonder of Kuṇḍalinī as lightning; <sup>21</sup>the pearl at the tip of your nose shines like a mass of lightning – she who stays at the tip of your nose is a form of the goddess who enchants the universe. <sup>22</sup>This beautiful fan Madhumāruta, Kṛṣṇa, glows like an autumnal moon, like nectar; <sup>23</sup>this bow is Vilāsakarmika, Kṛṣṇa, decked with flowers; the little knife is Tuṣṭidā, the hilt of which is decorated with divine gems; <sup>24</sup>the armlet is Raṅgada, Kṛṣṇa, your anklets tingle. Where did you get your horn and flute, your Kaustubha and your *tilaka*, Hari, <sup>25</sup>this sevenfold redness, enchanting all the peoples; this yellow *dhoṭī*, Kṛṣṇa, the supreme Kuṇḍalī Prakṛti; <sup>26</sup>your waist belt, tingling with bells and inlaid with various gems; this dark form of yours, marked with a flag, thunderbolt and so on – <sup>27</sup>where did you get all of this, considering you are always formless?
“Your skin shines like ground collyrium and enchants the world. <sup>28</sup>Wherever this body is, Kṛṣṇa, descendant of Yadu, there Kālī herself is found. If you are spotless, Lord, why then do women always surround you? <sup>29</sup>And why, Govinda, are your lips, fond of the joys of love, at the circle of Padminī’s face? <sup>30</sup>Best of the Yādavas! Why did you make such an effort to drink wine? Govinda! Playful Rādhā herself is at your lips. <sup>31</sup>Your form is covered with the scent of Padminī’s body. Best of the Yadus, Master! I wish to know the doctrine of the Clan behaviour, for without the Clan behaviour, Kṛṣṇa, there can be no attainment of Brahman.”

*‘Kṛṣṇa replied:* <sup>32</sup>“Listen, best of Brahmins, foremost of ascetics! I will explain to you what you spoke about. Best of Brahmins, you have spoken the truth, the truth; you have told no lies. <sup>33</sup>Know that this body that you see, foremost of Brahmins, is all made of Prakṛti. It is in no other way, darling of Brahmins! <sup>34</sup>Best of Brahmins, I am myself unchangeable through the worship of Tripurā’s feet.”
‘Greatest mistress! He told Nārada what I have told you before.<sup>§ǁ</sup> Hearing Kṛṣṇa’s words, the Brahmin was struck with wonder.

*‘Nārada said:* <sup>35</sup>“Lord, I bow to the highest mistress Prakṛti, by the sidelong glance of whom even the unqualified becomes qualified. <sup>36</sup>You are the foremost, higher than Brahman, unchangeable and eternal.
“Listen, Kṛṣṇa, mighty-armed one! Quickly go to Dvārakā, <sup>37</sup>similar to Vaikuṇṭha, decorated with pearl garlands! Dvārakā is Prakṛti Māyā and the [p. 311] bestower of great perfections. <sup>38</sup>It is suitable for you, Madhusūdana, best of Yadus, it is in no other way, lotus-eyed one, <sup>39</sup>for there resides eternally all-powerful Durgā with her eight heroines.”
‘What I told you previously, mistress of the gods, that Mādhava explained.[^dx-8]
<sup>40</sup>”I wish you all fortune, best of Yadus! Collect all your relatives, and quickly go, go to the city of Dvārakā. <sup>41</sup>I certainly cannot see any other place that would be suitable for you. The city of Dvārakā will always suit you, best of Yadus. <sup>42</sup>When you have gone there, you should properly worship the great goddess and mistress, the destroyer of birth and death, with devotion and with enchanting articles. <sup>43</sup>Then only, Kṛṣṇa, will you quickly and certainly attain perfection. Mighty-armed one! Go quickly to Dvārakā, the supreme Prakṛti!”
‘Having said this, this twiceborn Brahmin went on his way according to his sweet will.’

*Īśvara continued:* <sup>44</sup>‘Mighty-armed Kṛṣṇa immediately collected his relatives, and after he had killed Kaṃsa and other demons, fair-complexioned one, <sup>45</sup>he quickly went to Dvārakā, where the supreme mistress resides, where resides Mahāmāyā, eternal Yoganidrā. <sup>46</sup>Delicate one! Kṛṣṇa, the Lord himself, bowed his head to the goddess and together with all his relatives praised her with a Vedic hymn. <sup>47</sup>Kṛṣṇa worshipped her with various foodstuffs, flowers and most enchanting scents and then mentally recited her mantra with devotion and great effort. <sup>48</sup>Mahāmāyā, Mahiṣamardinī herself, was pleased by this.
“Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa, mighty-armed one! Listen to my supreme words! <sup>49</sup>Do not fear! By the power of the Clan behaviour I can see. Kṛṣṇa, mighty-armed one! Go quickly to the jewelled temple. <sup>50</sup>By the power of this temple, you will attain everything.”
‘Kṛṣṇa, Mahāviṣṇu of eyes like lotus petals, placed her words on his head, <sup>51</sup>bowed to the mistress of the world, and then went there.
‘Having seen this most delightful city surrounded by the sea, <sup>52</sup>equipped with all kinds of delights, bereft of all kinds of suffering, filled with flowers and perfumes and resembling Vaikuṇṭha, <sup>53</sup>everywhere full of houses jammed with the nine jewels, my dear, greatest mistress, eternally devoid of the six kinds of waves,[^a-9] <sup>54</sup>and then after some days Rukminī and other excellent women, Kṛṣṇa married Rukminī and other women.
<sup>55</sup>‘Now listen, impudent, lotus-eyed lady, daughter of the mountain, to the great secret by which Kṛṣṇa Mahāviṣṇu attained perfection, and hide it within your heart!’ [p. 312] *Īśvara continued:* <sup>56</sup>‘Rukmiṇī, Satyabhāmā, Śaibyā, Jāmbavatī, Kālindī, Lakṣmaṇā, Mitravindā as the seventh <sup>57</sup>and Nagnajitā: these are the eight primary queens, great mistress. Lord Kṛṣṇa, the mighty-armed one, then married them. <sup>58</sup>After Mādhava had married them with great pomp, greatest mistress, he married another sixteen thousand <sup>59</sup>and one hundred women of various kinds of beauty, great mistress. These are the sweet-eyed wives of Kṛṣṇa, mistress of the gods, <sup>60</sup>but Rukmiṇī and the other eight Padminīs are the principal ones. Lotus-eyed one! All of these eight Prakṛtis are devoted to the Clan behaviour, <sup>61</sup>day after day, by night and at midnight.
‘Also at midnight, Kṛṣṇa together with the eight queens <sup>62</sup>worshipped with various foodstuffs, with splendid rice cooked in sweetened milk; with eight types of grain and Kuśa grass he worshipped her who rides a lion. <sup>63</sup>In this way, Hari, the descendant of Yadu, constantly recited the Mahāvidyā of ten syllables and performed his daily rites in Dvārakā. <sup>64</sup>Hari became the master of minification and the other eight perfections.
‘Thus have I told you the truth of Keśava, handsome woman. <sup>65</sup>This truth of Keśava is the supreme of all truths. Pārvatī, greatest mistress! One who <sup>66</sup>worships Viṣṇu or Rudra without knowing the truth of Keśava loses everything and faces ruin at the end of all.
<sup>67</sup>‘Listen, beautiful one, to this most confidential and enchanting truth! Sweetly smiling one, my dear, once the intelligent one has honoured the secret Mahāvidyā, he should hear Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa from the mouth of the preceptor.’

*Pārvatī said:* <sup>68</sup>‘Lord, master! Please describe in detail on what you said about the temple. Please speak, master, conqueror of death, eternal one!’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>69</sup>‘This temple, dear, greatest mistress, consists of the nine gems, it is eternal, not constructed, and it has the six colours. <sup>70</sup>It is goddess Kuṇḍalinī herself, the eternally youthful Clan goddess, the mother of the wish-fulfilling tree, a form of the mother of the Veda. <sup>71</sup>Sometimes it becomes white, sometimes red; supremely beautiful it assumes all the six colours, one after the other. <sup>72</sup>My dear, this house is a form of Prakṛti herself, the cause of the universe, four hundred cubits long and one thousand tall. <sup>73</sup>It is made of gems and always shines like a thousand suns. Spring and the other seasons, Pārvatī, greatest mistress, <sup>74</sup>always stay there in their embodied forms. It has eight gates, and it is attended upon by minification and the other perfections. <sup>75</sup>There are always millions and millions of women there, fanning the temple with white chowries in their hands. <sup>76</sup>My dear, this temple, rare even among the gods, is devoid of the six waves and is eternally furnished with the four Vedas and all the Āgamas. <sup>77</sup>In all the ten directions of this building, handsome woman, my dear, greatest mistress, stand the Guardians of the directions immovable as pillars. <sup>78</sup>In many ways does this temple shine, daughter of the mountain! It is approachable by anyone, and it awards everything, goddess, as an embodied form of the four goals of [p. 313] life. <sup>79</sup>It is liberation, greatest mistress, the abode of the bliss of Brahman! What use is there for more words? Indra and all the gods, <sup>80</sup>thousand-headed Ananta and Brahmā stay there, daughter of the mountain. <sup>81</sup>In this building, great mistress, my dear goddess, are always millions of masses of universal eggs. Who can count them all? <sup>82</sup>It has all the places of pilgrimage, goddess, and the fifty Seats. The Seat of Mathurā, Padminī and the others stay here, greatest mistress – <sup>83</sup>there stay also millions of masses of Kṛṣṇas.
‘When Kṛṣṇa saw this temple of Tripurā he was amazed, <sup>84</sup>for this temple is Tripurā Sundarī herself. This temple, great mistress, my dear, is the shelter of all. <sup>85</sup>In the southern part of the blessed temple stands Mahiṣamardinī in her eternal form. The blessed temple, great mistress, is peaceful and turned to the South. <sup>86</sup>Seeing it, greatest mistress, all the people immediately take on forms carrying the conch, disk and the club.
<sup>87</sup>‘All that lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa saw in the blessed temple then disappeared, greatest mistress. <sup>88</sup>When Kṛṣṇa of eyes like lotus petals had thus reached this house of liberation, what could he not achieve through the worship of Tripurā’s feet? <sup>89</sup>The blessed temple is Tripurā Sundarī herself.
‘Greatest mistress, my dear, after Kṛṣṇa had attained this house of liberation, <sup>90</sup>he most vigorously enjoyed himself with his sixteen thousand wives. Great mistress, goddess, by Kṛṣṇa’s worshipping the feet of Tripurā, this happened <sup>91</sup>in every *kalpa*[^b-9] with the temples of Dvārakā: dear goddess, when Dvārakā is submerged, it is collected up there. <sup>92</sup>My dear, when mighty-armed Vāsudeva went to his own abode, then all of a sudden it was flooded and remained under the water. <sup>93</sup>Greatest mistress, sweetly smiling, lotus-eyed one! At the winding up of creation, all the universes quickly go to this blessed temple and remain there.
<sup>94</sup>‘My dear mistress of the gods, the blessed temple is always heap of Śakti. The blessed temple creates the city of Dvārakā. <sup>95</sup>Mistress of the gods! The temple is none other than the mother of the world, goddess, the highest mistress Tripurā, Kṛṣṇa’s mother.’

## Thirty-first Paṭala

*Devī said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Once again I ask you, great god, greatest master, if I may, whether there are any rules for the worship of Padminī? <sup>2</sup>Please tell me, lord of gods, you who carry the spear and the trident, for if you do not, lord, I will give up my body.’ [p. 314] *Īśvara replied:* <sup>3</sup>‘Beautiful one, great mistress, I will tell you the minor *vidyā*[^c-9] of Padminī Rādhikā, following the order of the minor *vidyās*. <sup>4</sup>Just as the Vijayā mantra, as the Jayā mantra, my dear, and as the incomparable Aparājita mantra, <sup>5</sup>so this Rādhā Tantra also always has an Armour.[^d-9] I will tell you the hymn of the thousand names of Rādhā, my dear, <sup>6</sup>and this Tantra without an Installation; listen carefully – first the Metre, then the mantra and finally the Armour. <sup>7</sup>Listen to Padminī’s Metre, handsome, lotus-eyed woman!
<sup>7</sup>’The seer for blessed Rādhikā Tantra is the cowherdess, the metre is Bṛhatī and the application is concealing the Mahāvidyā.
<sup>8</sup>‘Listen, beautiful one! I will tell you Rādhikā’s mantra. Extract the Kāma-seed and then the Vāgbhava, <sup>9</sup>then extract Rādhā in the dative case and finally carefully extract the first two seeds again. <sup>10</sup>Lotus-eyed one, this is called Rādhā’s eight-syllable mantra.<sup>ǁ</sup>
‘Now listen to Rādhā’s supreme one-syllable mantra! <sup>11</sup>Extract the Raṅginī-seed, then add the Vana-seed and half a Bindu and then make this one syllable, my dear. <sup>12</sup>This is the one-syllable *vidyā* that rests in Rādhā’s heart.[^x-9]
‘Again listen to a mantra of Rādhā’s, my dear, great mistress! <sup>13</sup>Extract two Manmathas, extract two Vāgbhavas, extract two Māyās, then the word Rādhā in the genitive case <sup>14</sup>and then again extract the previous seeds. This is the sixteen-syllable Kiśorī mantra.[^ba-9]
‘First extract the Praṇava, then Rādhā in the dative case <sup>15</sup>and then add Māyā at the end. This is six-syllable mantra, my dear.[^bb-9]
‘First extract the Praṇava, then two Kūrca-seeds, <sup>16</sup>then Rādhā in the dative case and then again extract the previous seeds. Lotus-eyed one, this is Padminī’s ten-syllable *vidyā*.[^bc-9]’

*Devī said:* <sup>17</sup>‘Mighty-armed master! Please tell me the Jayā mantra, please explain the one that is not told in all the Tantras, Śaṅkara!’ [p. 315] *Īśvara replied:* <sup>18</sup>‘Listen, Pārvatī, handsome woman! I will explain the Jayā mantra; incidentally I will tell it to you, greatest mistress, sinless one. <sup>19</sup>First extract the Vāgbhava and then extract the Māyā-seed, then the word Jayā in the dative case and then extract the previous seeds. <sup>20</sup>This is Jayā’s eight-syllable *vidyā*, lotus-eyed one.[^bd-9]
‘Extract the Hara-seed, then add the Vana-seed <sup>21</sup>and a Bindu with a half moon. This is known as the one-syllable mantra.<sup>†ǁ</sup>
‘First extract the Praṇava, then the word Jayā <sup>22</sup>in the dative case and then zealously again extract the Praṇava. This, daughter of the mountain, is Jayā’s six-syllable *vidyā*.[^bx-7]
<sup>23</sup>‘Extract two Māyās, then two Kūrcas and then after that, my dear goddess, extract two Vāgbhavas. <sup>24</sup>Yoginī! Carefully place the word Jayā in the dative case, then extract the previous seeds and extract Praṇava at the end. <sup>25</sup>Greatest mistress, Kālī, enchantress of the universe, is sixteen,[^ca-9] and this is the sixteen-syllable *vidyā*, the age of your youth.[^cb-9]
<sup>26</sup>‘My dear, extract two Māyās, then place the word Jayā in the dative case and then again the two seeds. <sup>27</sup>This is the eight-syllable *vidyā* concealed in all the Tantras.[^cc-9]
‘My dear, add Praṇava at the beginning and end; this is the ten-syllable mantra.[^cd-9]
<sup>28</sup>‘In this way, passionate, greatest mistress, one should in the case of Vijayā, Padmā or Padmāvatī mantras <sup>29</sup>extract a seed in the beginning and end and always place the names in the fourth case.
‘Thus have I explained the truth of the *dūtī* to you, sweetly smiling one. <sup>30</sup>Goddess! The worship of one who worships Pārvatī without the truth of the *dūtī* is fruitless; it will never bear fruit. <sup>31</sup>For Padminī and the others one should never do Installation, beautiful one, for there is no Installation for any of the minor *vidyās*. [p. 316] <sup>32</sup>‘Beautiful one! Having performed Purification of the elements<sup>‡ǁ</sup> and the Mātṛkā Installation,[^cx-10] one should do the Meditation and then carefully recite the Metre. <sup>33</sup>Mistress of the gods! I will now tell you the Meditation on Rādhā; listen attentively! Beautiful one, I will speak following the order of the minor *vidyās*.[^da-9]
<sup>34</sup>‘Red like the asparagus flower, the supreme part of Padminī, whose hair is spotlessly dark and curled like the tail of a yak; <sup>35</sup>whose enchanting, gleaming cheeks are touched by her jewelled earrings; whose nose is illumined by a pearl from the frontal globe of a king of elephants; <sup>36</sup>the circles of whose breasts are richly decked with gems, pearls and coral; the ornaments around whose neck includes the sunstone, the moonstone and the touchstone; <sup>37</sup>whose gleaming teeth resemble glittering citron seeds; who showers sidelong glances under eyebrows resembling Cupid’s bow; <sup>38</sup>whose breasts are like the frontal globes of an elephant; whose eyes are like glittering red water lilies; whose ears have delightful orifices; whose gait resembles that of an elephant;[^db-9] <sup>39</sup>whose two arms are decked with luminous ruby armlets; who wears bracelets of pure gold studded with various gems; <sup>40</sup>whose handsome wrists are decked with rings made of ivory; whose body is decorated with ornaments; who wears wonderful small bells; <sup>41</sup>who wears silken clothing; who is fond of her softly tinkling anklets; who is anointed with a paste of camphor, aloe, musk and saffron; <sup>42</sup>who sometimes assumes a white form, sometimes a red form, sometimes a yellow form and sometimes a black form, my dear – <sup>43</sup>this Rādhā of many forms, my dear, always and at every time of the day does this Padminī Rādhā remain by the side of Tripurā.
<sup>44</sup>‘In this way, goddess, have I told you the enchanting truth of her Meditation. Next I will tell you the Armour named after Rādhikā. <sup>45</sup>Pārvatī! That Armour that has not been described in any of the Tantras or among the minor *vidyās* I will now explain to you, greatest mistress. <sup>46</sup>This Armour called Enchanter of the three worlds has come from me; this Armour, greatest mistress, makes Padminī submit to you. <sup>47</sup>This Armour, goddess, is rare among the minor *vidyās*, for wherever the minor *vidyās* have been indicated, handsome one, great mistress, they have all been devoid of an Armour.’

[p. 317]

## Thirty-second Paṭala

*Devī said:* <sup>1</sup>‘God of gods, great god, cause of creation, maintenance and destruction, Lord, ocean of compassion! Please explain Rādhikā’s Armour.’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>2</sup>‘Listen, handsome goddess, to the Armour that enchants the people! It is hidden in all the Tantras and only now revealed. <sup>3</sup>Rādhā, Tripurā’s *dūtī*, is always a minor *vidyā*. Listen to her Armour, Pārvatī, following the order of the minor *vidyās*! <sup>4</sup>The recitations and acts of worship of one who never reveals this greatly confidential Armour will award all kinds of perfection. <sup>5</sup>Greatest mistress, mistress of the gods, it should never be told to one bereft of Śakti initiation, to a cheat, to one without devotion, to one who enjoys slandering Brahmins, <sup>6</sup>to a rogue, great mistress, to a Brahmin who advises Śūdras or to a twice-born who sacrifices on behalf of Śūdras. <sup>7</sup>It should never be told to a Śūdra who enjoys slandering Brahmins, it should never, never, never be told, <sup>8</sup>it should never be told, great mistress, if one cares about one’s welfare. My words should be told to a disciple who has devotion and who delights in his Śakti initiation, <sup>9</sup>and particularly to a Vaiṣṇava who has fond devotion to his preceptor; not to anyone else.
<sup>10</sup>‘The seer of blessed Rādhā’s Armour that blesses the three worlds is the cowherdess, the metre is Anuṣṭubh, the divinity is blessed Rādhikā and the application is hiding the practice of the Mahāvidyā.
<sup>11</sup>‘ *Oṃ*. May goddess Rukminī, bestower of auspiciousness, protect in the East! *Hrīṃ*. May Satyā, the fulfiller of every desire, protect in the West! <sup>12</sup>*Hrīṃ*. May Jāmbavatī, the bestower of the object of every desire, protect in the South! *Hrīṃ*. May Bhadrā, endowed with auspicious power, protect in the North! <sup>13</sup>*Hrīṃ*. May Mahādevī, the splendid one, dear to Kṛṣṇa, protect above! *Aiṃ*. May the goddess who dwells on the plane of Pātāla protect me below!
<sup>14</sup>‘ *Aiṃ*. May Rādhikā protect me below; *Oṃ*. The fourth state, in the heart! “Obeisance”, “may”, her name in the fourth case, “protect” and “all of my limbs”, again and again! May goddess Īśvarī, mistress of the universe, protect me everywhere!
<sup>15</sup>‘ *Aiṃ Hṛīṃ Rādhikāyai Hrīṃ Aiṃ*. May she protect my head!
<sup>16</sup>‘ *Rāṃ.* May the passionate one-syllable seed protect my heart!
<sup>17</sup>‘ *Klīṃ Klīṃ Rādhikāyai Klīṃ Klīṃ*. May she protect my right arm!
<sup>18</sup>‘ *Hrīṃ Hrīṃ Rādhikāyai Hrīṃ Hrīṃ*. May Padminī, fragrant as a lotus, protect my left side!
<sup>19</sup>‘ *Aiṃ Aiṃ Rādhikāyai Aiṃ Aiṃ*. May she protect my right leg!
<sup>20</sup>‘ *Klīṃ Klīṃ Aiṃ Aiṃ Rādhikāyai Hrīṃ Hrīṃ Aiṃ Aiṃ Klīṃ Klīṃ Oṃ*. May she protect all of my limbs!
<sup>21</sup>‘ *Klīṃ Rādhikāyai Klīṃ*. May Padminī always protect my left leg!
<sup>22</sup>‘ *Hrīṃ Rādhikāyai Hrīṃ*. May she protect my eyes!
<sup>23</sup>‘ *Aiṃ Rādhikāyai Aiṃ*. May she always protect my ears! [p. 318] <sup>24</sup>‘ *Hrīṃ Rādhikāyai Hrīṃ.* May she always protect my nostrils!
<sup>25</sup>‘ *Oṃ Rādhikāyai Oṃ*. May she always protect my lips!
<sup>26</sup>‘ *Hrīṃ*. May Bhuvaneśvarī protect my brow!
<sup>27</sup>‘ *Hrīṃ*. May Kālī always protect the circle of my face!
<sup>28</sup>‘ *Hrīṃ Rādhikāyai Hrīṃ Oṃ*. May Sarasvatī always protect the teeth!
<sup>29</sup>‘ *Hrīṃ Hrīṃ Hrīṃ Mahiṣamardinyai Hrīṃ Hrīṃ*. May Mahiṣamardinī who dwells in Dvārakā protect my thousand-petalled lotus[^dc-9]!
<sup>30</sup>‘ *Aiṃ Hrīṃ Aiṃ*. May Mātaṅgī always protect my heart!
<sup>31</sup>‘ *Hrīṃ Aiṃ Hrīṃ*. May Ugratārā always protect my lotus of my navel!
<sup>32</sup>‘ *Klīṃ Aiṃ Klīṃ Sundarī Klīṃ Aiṃ Klīṃ*. May she protect the private place[^dd-9] at the root of my penis!
<sup>33</sup>‘ *Laṃ Aiṃ Laṃ*. May Pṛthvī protect my anus!
<sup>34</sup>‘ *Aiṃ Aiṃ Aiṃ Vagalā Aiṃ Aiṃ Aiṃ*. May she protect my breasts!
<sup>35</sup>‘ *He Sauḥ Bhairavī He Sauḥ*. May she protect my shoulders!
<sup>36</sup>‘ *Hrīṃ Annapūrṇā Hrīṃ*. May she always protect the back of my neck!
<sup>37</sup>‘ *Aiṃ Hrīṃ Aiṃ*. May these three seeds always protect my back!
<sup>38</sup>‘ *Oṃ*. May Mahādeva always protect all of my body!
<sup>39</sup>‘ *Oṃ*. May Nārāyaṇa always protect all of my body!
<sup>40</sup>‘ *Oṃ*. May Kṛṣṇa, the husband of Rukmiṇī, always protect my frame!
<sup>41</sup>‘Rukmiṇī, Satyabhāmā, Śaibyā, Jāmbavatī, Lakṣmaṇā, Mitravindā, Bhadrā and Nāgnajitī: <sup>42</sup>they are all youthful, beautiful and sweet-eyed. May these beauties always protect me in the eight directions! <sup>43</sup>‘ *Oṃ*. May Nārāyaṇa, Govinda, lotus-eyed Śiva, Keśava, the killer of Keśi, Hari, always protect all of my body!
<sup>44</sup>’This, my dear, greatest mistress, is Padminī’s splendid Armour that blesses the three worlds, suitable for the minor *vidyās*. <sup>45</sup>Padminī subjugates herself to one who constantly and devotedly recites or makes other recite it, forsaking food and drink, <sup>46</sup>for a whole year.
‘Thus have I told you this Armour, goddess, rare on earth. <sup>47</sup>“There is never any Armour connected with a minor *vidyā*” – therefore, great mistress, this Armour is rare on earth.
<sup>48</sup>‘Daughter of the mountain! If one recites it during a whole year, giving up fruits, roots and water, Padminī certainly subjugates herself. <sup>49</sup>One who recites this supreme Armour in this manner will attain Viṣṇu’s world; I say nothing wrong. [p. 319] <sup>50</sup>‘Handsome woman! One should worship the *vidyā*, a Mahāvidyā in secret, but this Armour, goddess, one should always recite openly. <sup>51</sup>My dear, one who recites the Armour without a Mahāvidyā, my dear, will immediately go to the Kumbhīpāka hell.<sup>§ǁ</sup> ’

## Thirty-third Paṭala

*Īśvara said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Thus have I spoken, goddess. What else should I tell you? You are always the listener, greatest mistress, and I am the speaker.’

*Devī said:* <sup>2</sup>‘Once again I ask you, great god, Lord of gods, if I may, for in your heart dwell all the Tantras. <sup>3</sup>Lord of gods, greatest master, god, virtuous one, ocean of compassion! Please tell me the various Tantras and the many secrets that dwell in your heart.’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>4</sup>‘Greatest mistress, beautiful one, no secret of Padminī remains, great mistress, for I have told you all, greatest mistress! <sup>5</sup>Nothing else comes within my purview, my dear, great mistress, for I have told you whatever secrets there are, great mistress.’

*Devī said:* <sup>6</sup>‘Tell me Padminī’s secret, Lord, greatest master, for if you do not tell me, Lord, I will give up my body.’

*Īśvara said:* <sup>7</sup>‘Listen, dear, deer-eyed one, this talk of yours is impudent. Give up your impudence, sweet one, and I will tell you a secret. <sup>8</sup>Listen, sweet one, to a most rare hymn, a hymn of a thousand names, suitable for the minor *vidyās*. <sup>9</sup>Among the minor *vidyās*, mistress of the gods, this hymn is enchanting and very confidential. Great mistress, it is always suitable for Padminī. <sup>10</sup>This hymn of Padminī is amazing and most wonderful; it has never been given in any of the Tantras, but your devotion has now revealed it.
<sup>11</sup>‘The seer of blessed Padminī’s hymn of a thousand names is blessed Kṛṣṇa, the presiding divinity is Mahiṣamardinī, the metre is Gāyatrī and the application is attaining perfection in the Mahāvidyā.
<sup>12</sup>‘ *Oṃ Hrīṃ Aiṃ Padminyai Rādhikāyai Hrīṃ Oṃ Aiṃ*. Raṅginī (Fervent), Rādhikā,[^dx-9] Rādhā, Ramaṇī (Enjoyer), Ramā (Rejoicing), <sup>13</sup>Ramyā (Pleasing), Rāmā (Lovely), Rāgavatī (Impassioned), Rāgayuktā (Passionate), Rajoguṇā (The quality of passion), Raktāṅgī (Red-bodied), Raktapuṣpābhā (Red as a flower), Rādhyā (To be worshipped), Rāsaparāyaṇā (Enjoyer of the Rāsa dance), <sup>14</sup>Rambhāvatī (Resounding), Rūpamālā (Garlanded with beauty), Rajanī (Dark one), Rañjanī (Charming), Rati (Passion), Ratipriyā (Dear to passion), Ramaṇīyā (Agreeable), Rasapuñjā (Mass of Rasa), Rasāyanā (Elixir), <sup>15</sup>Rāsamadhyā (Standing in the middle of the Rāsa dance), Rāsarūpā (The form of the Rāsa dance), Rāsaveṣā (Dressed for the Rāsa dance), Rasotsukā (Greedy for Rasa), Rāsavatī (Owning the Rāsa [p. 320] dance), Rasollāsa (The happiness of Rasa), Rasikā (Woman of taste), Rasabhuṣaṇā (Ornamented by Rasa), <sup>16</sup>Rasamālādharī (Wearing a garland of Rasa), Raṅgī (Fond), Raktapaṭṭaparicchadā (Dressed in red silk); ‘Kamalā (Lotus), Kalpalatikā (Wish-fulfilling creeper), Kulavrataparāyaṇā (Devoted to the Clan vow), <sup>17</sup>Kāminī (Passionate), Kamalā (Lotus), Kuntī, Kalikallolanāśinī (Destroyer of the waves of the Kali age), Kulinā (Well-born), Kulavatī (Noble), Kāmī (Impassioned), Kāmasandīpanī (Enflamed by love), <sup>18</sup>Kaumārī (Maiden), Kṛṣṇavanitā (Mistress of Kṛṣṇa), Kāmārtā (Struck by love), Kāmarūpiṇī (The form of love), Kāmukā (Desirous), Kaluṣaghnī (Destroyer of impurity), Kulajñā (Knower of the Clan), Kulapaṇḍitā (Learned in the Clan), <sup>19</sup>Kṛṣṇavarṇā (Black), Kṛṣṇāṅgī (Black-bodied), Kṛṣṇavastraparicchadā (Clad in black garments), Kāntā (Lover), Kāmasvarūpā (The very form of love), Kāmarūpā (The form of love), Kṛpāvatī (Gracious), <sup>20</sup>Kṣemā (Peace), Kṣemaṅkarī (Giver of peace);
‘Khelatkhañjanagāminī (Moving like a playful wagtail), Khasthā (Standing in the sky), Khagā (Moving in the sky), Khagastātrī (Immovable in the sky), Khagavihāriṇī (Strolling in the sky);
<sup>21</sup>‘Gariṣṭhā (Most venerable), Garimā (Venerability), Gaṅgā, Gayā, Godāvarī, Gāndhārī, Guṇinā (Endowed with qualities), Gaurī (Golden), Guṇajñā (Knower of the qualities), Guṇaśālinī (Abounding in qualities), <sup>22</sup>Godāvarī, Gomatī, Gaurāṅgī (Golden-bodied), Gaṇakā (Calculator), Guhā (Concealed), Gariṣṭhā (Most venerable), Garimā (Venerability), Gaṅgā, Goṣṭhī (Assembly), Gokulavāsinī (Living in Gokula), <sup>23</sup>Gandharvī (Gandharva-woman), Gāṇakuśalī (Skilful singer), Guptā (Hidden), Guptavilāsinī (Enjoying secretly);
‘Ghargharā (Small bell), Gharmadā (Giver of heat), Gharmā (Heat), Ghanasthā (Standing among the masses), Ghanavāsinī (Living among the masses), <sup>24</sup>Ghṛṇā (Tenderness), Ghṛṇavatī (Tender), Ghorā (Terrible), Ghorakarmavivarjitā (Devoid of terrible deeds);
‘Candrā (Moon), Candraprabhā (Moonlight), Candra mūrtiparicchadā (Dressed in the form of the moon), <sup>25</sup>Candrarūpā (Form of the moon), Candrākhyā (Called the moon), Cañcalā (Fickle), Cārubhūṣaṇā (Ornamented with loveliness), Caturā (Ingenious), Cāruśīlā (Of lovely disposition), Campā (Campa-flower), Campāvatī (Possessor of Campa flowers), <sup>26</sup>Candrarekhā (Digit of the moon), Candrakalā (Part of the moon), Cāruvīṇāvinodinī (She who delights in a lovely lute), Candracandanabhūṣāṅgī (Whose body is decorated with moonlike sandalwood), Cārvāṅgī (Lovely-bodied), Candrabhūṣaṇā (Decorated with the moon), <sup>27</sup>Citriṇī (Endowed with variety), Citrarūpā (Whose body is variegated), Citramūrtidharā (Who wears a variegated form);
‘Chadmarūpā (Disguised form), Chadmeśī (The mistress of disguise), Śveta Chatravidhāriṇī (Carrier of a white parasol), <sup>28</sup>Chatrātapā (Burner of the parasol), Chatrāṅgī (Whose limbs are a parasol), Chatraghnī (Destroyer of the parasol), [p. 321] Chatrapālinī (Guardian of the parasol), Churitāmṛtadhāraughā (Flood of streaming, flashing nectar), Chadmavāsinī (Living in disguise), <sup>29</sup>Chaṭīkṛtamarālaughā (Making herds of flamingos shine), Chaṭīkṛtanijāmṛtā (Making her own nectar shine);
‘Jamunā,[^a-10] Jaganmātā (Mother of the world), Jananī (Mother), Janmadāyinī (Giver of birth), <sup>30</sup>Jayā (Victory), Jayantā (Victorious), Jaśodhā, Jīvanā (Life), Jagadambikā (Mother of the world), Jīvā (Living being), Jīvasvarūpā (Form of a living being), Jāḍyāvidvaṃsakāriṇī (Destroyer of inertia), <sup>31</sup>Jagadyonī (Source of the world), Ayoni Jā (Unborn), Jagaddhetu (Cause of the world), Jaganmayī (The world itself), Jagadānandajananī (Giver of bliss to the world), Jananī (Mother), Janasaṃpadā (Full of people);
<sup>32</sup>‘Jhaṅkāravāhinī (Maker of a tinkling sound), Jhañjhā (Wind and rain), Jhaṅkāra (Tinkle), Jharjharāvatī (Carrier of the Jharjhara drum);
‘Ṭaṅkarā (Sealer), Ṭaṅkinī (Coverer), Ṭaṅkā (Chisel), Ṭaṅkitā (Covered), Ṭaṅkarūpiṇī (Form of a chisel);
<sup>33</sup>‘Ḍambarādambarā (Noisy war drum), Ḍambā (Impeller), Ḍamaḍambā (Impeller of impulses), Ḍambarā (Noise);[^b-10]
‘Ḍhaukitāśeṣanirghoṣā (She who brings together all sounds), Ḍhalaḍhalitalocanā (Quivering eyes);
<sup>34</sup>‘Tapanī (Heat), Tīrthavāsī (Dweller at places of pilgrimage), Tripathā (Reached by three paths), Tridaśeśvarī (Mistress of the thirty gods), Trilokagāmī (Directed to the three worlds), Trailokyā (The three worlds), Taruṇī (Tender girl), Taruṇekṣaṇā (Tender-eyed), <sup>35</sup>Tāpahantrī (Remover of affliction), Tapā (Warmth), Tāpā (Affliction), Tapanīyā (Attained by penance), Tapāvatī (Warm), Tāpasī (Ascetic), Tripurādevī (Goddess Tripurā), Tripurājñākarī (Servant of Tripurā), <sup>36</sup>Trilakṣā (Three hundred thousand gods), Tāriṇī (Saviour), Tārā (Protector), Tārānāyakamohinī (Enchanter of the heroines of Tārā), Trailokyagamanahlādā (Enjoying entering the three worlds), Tuṣṭidā (Giver of satisfaction), Tvaritā (Quick), Tvarā (Speed), <sup>37</sup>Tṛṣṇā (Thirst), Taraṅginī (Wave), Tīrthā (Place of pilgrimage), Trivikramavihāriṇī (Delighting in the three steps), Tamomayī (Consisting of Tamas), Tāmasī (Ignorant), Tapasyā (Austerity), Tapasaḥ phalā (The fruit of austerity), <sup>38</sup>Trailokyavyāpinī (Pervading the three worlds), Tuṣṭā (Satisfied), Tṛptikṛtyā (Who ought to be satified), Tulātulī (Weigher of balances), Trailokyamohinī (Enchanter of the three worlds), Tūrṇā (Quick), Trailokyavibhavapradā (Bestower of might on the three [p. 322] worlds), <sup>39</sup>Tripadī (Taking three steps), Tathyā (Truth), Timiracandrikā (Moonlight in darkness), Tejorupā (Form of light), Tapaḥsārā (The essence of austerity), Tripurāripadasthitā (Staying at the feet of Śiva), <sup>40</sup>Trayī (Threefold), Tanvī (Delicate), Tāpaharā (Remover of affliction), Tapanāṅgajavāhinī (Carrier of the son of the sun), Tari (Boat), Taraṇi (Saviour), Tāruṇyā (Youth), Tapitā (Refined), Taraṇīpriyā (Dear to saviours), <sup>41</sup>Tīvrapāpaharā (Remover of severe sins), Tulyā (Equal), Pāpā Tṛṇatanūnapā (Grass and ghee for sin);
‘Dāridranāśinī (Remover of poverty), Dātā (Giver), Dakṣā (Expert), Deyā (To be given), Dayāvatī (Compassionate), <sup>42</sup>Divyā (Divine), Divyasvarūpa (Divine nature), Dīkṣādakṣā (Expert at initiation), Dayādravā (Stream of compassion), Divyarūpā (Divine body), Divyamūrti (Divine form), Daityendraprāṇanāśinī (Killer of the king of demons), <sup>43</sup>Drumā (Tree), Drumārūpā (Form of a tree), Dandaśūkavināśinī (Killer of snakes), Durvārā (Irresistible), Damagrāhyā (Attainable by self-restraint), Devakāryakarī (Servant of the gods), <sup>44</sup>Devapriyā (Dear to the gods), Devayājyā (Sacrificed to by the gods), Daivādaivadhiyā (Dear to the divine and the demoniac), Dikpālapadadātā (Giver of a position to the guardians of the quarters), Dīrghāyuḥ (Long life), Dīrghalocanā (Broad-eyed), <sup>45</sup>Kāma Dughā (Milk of desire), Daugdhī (Milker), Dūṣaṇavarjitā (Free of faults), Dugdhāmbusadṛśābhāṣā (The complexion of whom resembles fresh milk), Dhruvā (Constant), Dhenunādā (The bellowing of a cow), Dhruvasparśā (The constant touch), Dharmakāmārthamokṣadā (Giver of *dharma*, *kāma*, *artha* and *mokṣa*), <sup>54</sup>Dharmiṇī (Virtous), Dharmamātā (The mother of Dharma), Dharmadātrī (Giver of Dharma), Dhanurdharā (Carrier of a bow), Dhātrī (Nurse), Dhyeyā (To be meditated on), Dharā (Stream), Dhoyī (Runner),[^c-10] Dhāriṇī (Carrier), Dhr?takalmaṣā (Carrier of darkness), <sup>55</sup>Dhanadā (Giver of wealth), Dharmadā (Giver of Dharma), Dhānyā (Grain), Dhānyadā (Giver of grain), Dhanyadā (Giver of riches), Dhanā (Riches), Dhanyā martial art);
<sup>58</sup>‘Nirānandā (Bereft of bliss), Nirīhā (Desireless), Nirvāṇadvārasaṃsthitā (Standing at the gate of liberation), Nirvāṇapadavīdātrī (Giver of the state of liberation), Nandinī (Rejoicing), Nākanāyikā (Naked heroine), <sup>59</sup>Nārāyaṇī, Niṣiddhaghnī (Destroyer of the forbidden), Nijarūpaprakāśinī (Revealer of her own form), Namasyā (Deserving homage), Nirṇayā (Settled), Nandā (Enjoyer), Nūtānūtanarūpiṇī (Assumer of newer and newer forms), <sup>60</sup>Nirmalā (Faultless), Nirmalābhāsā (Faultless light), Nirakhyā (Indeterminate), Nirupadravā (Free from affliction), Nityānandamayī (Eternal bliss itself), Nityā (Eternal), Nityanūtanavigrahā (Whose form is eternally young), <sup>61</sup>Niṣiddharativarjyā (Excluder of forbidden passion), Nīrvāṇapadadīpikā (Light on the abode of liberation), Niḥśaṅkā (Fearless), Nirātaṅkā (Painless), Nirṇāśitamahāmanās (Indestructibly magnanimous), <sup>62</sup>Nirmalānandajananī (The mother of spotless bliss), Nirmalaśyāmakeśinī (Whose hair is spotlessly dark), Niravadyakulaśreṣṭhā (Best of a blameless family), Nityānandasvarūpiṇī (The form of eternal bliss), <sup>63</sup>Nirṇayā (Conclusion), Nirṇayaprītā (Pleased by conclusions), Niṣiddhakarmavarjitā (Devoid of forbidden deeds), Nityotsavā (Eternal festival), Nityatṛptā (Eternally satisfied), Namaskāryā (Worthy of obeisance), Nirañjanā (Spotless), <sup>64</sup>Niṣṭhāvatī (Steady), Nirātaṅkā (Painless), Nirlepā (Unstained), Niścalātmikā (Whose self is motionless), Niravadyā (Blameless), Nirīhā (Desireless), Nirañjana purasthitā (Dweller in a spotless place);
<sup>65</sup>‘Puṇyapradā (Bestower of merit), Puṇyakarī (Performer of merit), Puṇyagarbhā (Origin of merit), Purātanī (Ancient), Puṇyarūpā (Form of merit), Puṇyadehā (Body of merit), Puṇyagītā (Song of merit), Pāvanī (Purifier), <sup>66</sup>Pūtā (Pure), [p. 324] Pavitrā (Clean), Paramā (Higher), Parā (Supreme), Puṇyavibhūṣaṇā (Ornament of merit), Puṇyadātrī (Giver of merit), Puṇyadharā (Stream of merit), Puṇyāpuṇyapravāhinī (Bearing away merit and demerit), <sup>67</sup>Puṇyadehā (Body of merit), Puṇyavatī (Meritorious), Pūrṇimā (Full moon), Pūrṇacandrimā (Full moon), Paurṇamāsī (Of the full moon), Parāpadmā (Supreme lotus), Padmajā (Lotusborn), Padmagandhinī (Fragrant as a lotus), <sup>68</sup>Padminī, Padmavaktrā (Lotus-face), Padmamālādharā (Wearing a lotus garland), Padmodbhavā (Born of a lotus), Parākhyā (Supreme utterance), Paramānandarūpiṇī (Form of supreme bliss), <sup>69</sup>Prakāśyā (To be revealed), Paramāścāryā (Supremely wonderful), Padmagarbhanivāsinī (Dweller in the womb of a lotus), Pāvanī (Purifier), Pūtā (Pure), Pavitrā (Clean), Paramākalā (Supreme part), <sup>70</sup>Padmārcitā (Worshipped with lotuses), Padmasaṃsthā (Standing in a lotus), Padmamātā (Mother of a lotus), Purātanī (Ancient), Padmāsanagatā (Sitting on a lotus seat), Nityā Padmāsanaparicchadā (Furnished with an eternal lotus seat), <sup>71</sup>Śukla Padmāsanagatā (Sitting on a white lotus seat), Rakta Padmāsanā (Red lotus seat), Pītapadmāsanagatā (Sitting on a yellow lotus seat), Kṛṣṇa Padmasthitā (Standing on a blue lotus), <sup>72</sup>Padārthadāyinī (Giver of principles), Padmavanavāsī (Living in a lotus grove), Parātparā (Higher than the highest), Prakāśinī (Illuminator), Pragantā (Attained), Puṇyaślokā (Well praised), Pūraṇī (Completer);
<sup>73</sup>‘Phalahastā (Fruit in her hands), Phalahārī (Remover of fruits), Phalinī (Fruitful), Phalarūpiṇī (The form of fruit), Phullendīlocanā (Eyes like blooming lotuses), Phullā (Blooming), Phullakairavagandhinī (Fragrant as blooming white lotuses), <sup>74</sup>Phelinī (Remnants), Phalinī (Fruitful), Phenā (Foam), Phullocchāṭitapātakā (Cleaver and driver away of sin);

‘Kṣemadā (Giver of safety), Kṣalitā (Purified), Kṣomā (Linen), Kṣudraghaṇṭavidhāriṇī (Carrier of small bells).
<sup>118</sup>‘Listen once again, impudent woman, to the vocalic letters, to a hymn of a thousand names consisting of vowels and consonants!

<sup>128</sup>‘Ārogyadātrī (Giver of health), Ānandā (Bliss), Āpannārtivināśinī (Remover of misfortune and pain), Āścāryarūpā (Wonderful form), Ādyasthā (Standing at the top), Āptavidyā (She who has attained the Vidyā), <sup>129</sup>Āpyāyanī (Causing fullness), Ālasyā (Idleness), Āpadāhāmṛtapradā (Bestower of the nectar that removes misfortune);

<sup>134</sup>‘Ūhā (Consideration), Ūhavitarkā (Considered conjecture), Ūrdhvadhārā (The upper flow), Ūrdhvaretā (Updrawn semen), Ūrmilā, Ūrdhvagā (Gone above);
<sup>135</sup>’Ṛṣivṛndastutā (Praised by groups of seers), Ṛddhi (Increase), Ṛṇatrayavināśinī (Remover of the three debts[^d-10]), Ṛtambharā (Bearing truth [p. 329] within), Ṛddhidātrī (Giver of increase), Ṛkthā (Consisting of hymns of the Ṛgvedā), Ṛksvarūpiṇī (The form of the hymns of the Ṛgvedā), <sup>136</sup>Ṛtupriyā (Dear to the seasons), Ṛkṣamātā (Mother of the Pleiades), Ṛkṣārci (Ray of the Pleiades), Ṛkṣamārgagā (Follower of the path of the Pleiades), Ṛtulakṣaṇarūpā (Form marked by the right time), Ṛtumārgapradarśinī (Illuminator of the path of right time);

<sup>138</sup>‘Aiśvaryā (Majesty), Aiśvaryarūpā (Majestic form), Aindrī (Related to Indra), Aindranibhadyuti (Splendid like a bolt of lightning);
‘Ojasvinī (Vigorous), Oṣadhī (Herb), Ojodā (Strenghtening), Odanadāyinī (Giver of rice boiled in milk), <sup>139</sup>Oṣṭhāmṛtā (Lipnectar), Oṣṭhaunnatyā (Elevated lips), Oṣadhābhavarogahā (The herb that removes the disease of existence);

<sup>140</sup>‘Ambarasthā (Standing in the sky), Ambarā (Sky), Ambā (Mother), Ambālikā (Mother), Ambujekṣā (Lotus-eyed), Ambālā (Mother), Ambusnigdhī (Smooth as water);
<sup>141</sup>‘Aṃśumālī (Garlanded with sunbeams), Aṃśumatī (Radiant), Aṃśī (Having parts), Aṃśasambhavā (Born from a part), Andhatāmisrahādevī (Goddess that removes the deepest darkness), Atyantaśobhinasvarā (Extremely splendid sound), Artheśā (Mistress of riches), Arthadātrī (Giver of riches), Artharūpā (Form of riches), Anāhatā (Unstruck).
<sup>142</sup>‘Dear, beautiful one! Now listen to the next names, beginning with the letter k, extremely beautiful, pure, spotless and the highest abode!’
<sup>143</sup>Īśvara continued: ‘Kalyāṇī (Virtuous), Kāmyā (Desirable), Kamalotpalagandhinī (Fragrant as a lotus), Kumudvatī (Bearing a lotus), Kamalinī (Lotus), Kānti (Splendour), Kampitadāyinī (Giver of tremors), <sup>144</sup>Kāñcanākhyā (Appearing as gold), Kāmadhenu (Wish-fulfilling cow), Kīrti (Fame), Kṛtakleśanāśinī (Remover of past afflictions), Kratuśreṣṭhā (Best sacrifice), Kratukalā (Time of sacrifice), Karmapāśavināśinī (Cutter of the bonds of actions), <sup>145</sup>Kamalākṣī (Lotus-eyed), Klamaharā (Remover of fatigue), Kṛśānutapanaprabhā (Bright as sunshine), Karuṇārdrā (Tender-hearted), Kalyāṇī (Virtuous), Kalikalmasanāśinī (Remover of the stains of the Kali age), <sup>146</sup>Kāmarūpā (Form of desire), Kriyāśakti (Power of action), Kamalotpalamālinī (Garlanded with lotuses), Kūṭasthā (Standing at the summit), Karuṇā (Compassion), Kāntā (Agreeable), Karmayānī (The path of action), Kalāvatī (Having parts), <sup>147</sup>Kamalā (Lotus), Kalpalatikā (Wish-fulfilling vine), Kālī, Kalmaṣanāśinī (Remover of stains), Kamanīyajalā (Lovely water), Kamrā (Loving), Kapardipūjanapriyā (Fond of the worship of Śiva), <sup>148</sup>Kadambakusumābhāṣā (Yellow as the Kadamba flower), Kokanadekṣaṇā [p. 330] (With eyes like red lotuses), Kālindī (Yamunā river), Kelikalitā (Driven by play), Kaṇā (Minute), Kādambamālikā (Wearing a garland of Kadamba flowers), <sup>149</sup>Krāntalokatrayā (Extended over the three worlds), Kaṇḍu (Ardent desire), Kaṇḍurūpamanoharā (The delightful form of ardent desire),
‘Khaḍginī (Bearing a scimitar), Khaḍgadhārābhā (Shining as a swordsman), Khaḍgā (Scimitar), Khaḍgendudhāriṇī (Bearer of a crescent moon), <sup>150</sup>Khe khelagāminī (Wandering playfully in the sky), Khasthā (Standing in the sky), Khaḍgendutilakārcitā (Decorated with a crescent Tilaka), Khecarī (Moving in the sky), Khecarīvandyā (Praised by those moving in the sky), Khyāti (Renown), Khyātipradāyinī (Giver of renown), <sup>151</sup>Khaṇḍitāśeṣapāpaughā (The flood of all of whose sins are broken), Khalavṛddhivināśinī (Destroyer of the increase of evil men), Khātainaḥ kandasandohā (Remover of the bulbous roots of sin), Khaḍgakhaṭvāṅgavāhinī (Bearer of a scimitar and a club), <sup>152</sup>Kharasantāpaśamanī (Stiller of sharp suffering), Khaniḥ pīyūṣapāthasām (Mine for the streams of nectar).
<sup>153</sup>‘Listen next, greatest mistress, beautiful one, to the names beginning with g, always greatly beautiful!
‘Guhā (Secret), Gandhavatī (Fragrant), Gaurī (Golden), Gandharvanagarapriyā (Dear to the cities of the Gandharvas), <sup>154</sup>Gambhīrāṅgī (Whose limbs are deep), Guṇamayī (Qualified), Gatāntā (Who has reached the end), Gatipriyā (Fond of the path), Gaṇanāthāmbikā (Mother of Ganeśa), Gītā (Song), Gadyapadyaparistutā (Praised by prose and poetry), <sup>155</sup>Gāndhārī, Garbhaśamanī (Killer of the embryo), Gatibhraṣṭā (Fallen from the path), Gatipriyā (Fond of the path), Gomatī (Rich in cattle), Guhavidyā (Secret Vidyā), Gauḥ (Cow), Goptrī (Defender), Gagaṇagāminī (Gone into the sky), <sup>156</sup>Gotrapravardinī (Increaser of the family), Guṇyā (Endowed with qualities), Guṇātītā (Beyond the qualities), Guṇāgrahī (Taker of qualities), Guhāmbikā (Secret mother), Girisutā (Daughter of the mountain), Govindarasadāyinī (Giver of rasa to Govinda), <sup>157</sup>Guṇanīyacaritrā (Whose deeds multiply), Gāyatrī, Giriśapriyā (Dear to Śiva), Gūḍharūpā (Secret form), Guṇavatī (Qualified), Gurvī (Venerable), Gauravaraṅginī (Fond of venerability), <sup>158</sup>Grahapīḍāharā (Remover of affliction caused by the stars), Guptā (Concealed), Gadāghnī (Killing with a club), Gamanapriyā (Fond of intercourse).
‘Listen carefully to the names beginning with the letter c! <sup>159</sup>When you have heard this *vidyā*, sweetly smiling one, you should conceal it.
‘Cāmpeyalocanā (With eyes like Campaka flowers), Cāru (Sweet), Cārvāṅgī (Sweet-bodied), Cārurūpiṇī (Of sweet body), <sup>160</sup>Cāryacāritranilayā (Abode of sweet behaviour), Citrakṛt (Astonishing), Citrarūpiṇī (Astonishing form), Candracandanasiktāṅgī (Whose body is anointed with moonlike sandalwood paste), Carvaṇīyā (To be chewed), Cirasthitā (Longstanding), <sup>161</sup>Cārucampakamālāḍyā (Richly adorned with sweet Campaka garlands), Calitāśeṣaduṣkṛtā (Who has shaken off all bad deeds), Cidākāśaśaradvāti (The autumnal moon in the sky of consciousness), Cañcaccāmaravījitā [p. 331] (Fanned by shaking chowries), <sup>162</sup>Cāritāśeṣavṛjinā (Who has set all deceit in motion), Cāritāśeṣamaṇḍalā (The circle of all loveliness);
‘Raktacandanasiktāṅgī (Whose body is anointed with red sandalwood paste), Raktāṅgī (Redbodied), Raktamālikā (Red garland);
<sup>163</sup>‘Śuklacandanasiktāṅgī (Whose body is anointed with white sandalwood paste), Śuklāṅgī (Whitebodied), Śuklamālikā (White garland);
‘Pītacandanasiktāṅgī (Whose body is anointed with yellow sandalwood paste), Pītāṅgī (Yellowbodied), Pītamālikā (Yellow garland);
<sup>164</sup>‘Kṛṣṇacandanasiktāṅgī (Whose body is anointed with black sandalwood paste), Kṛṣṇāṅgī (Blackbodied), Kṛṣṇamālikā (Black garland);
‘Śuklavastraparidhānā (Dressed in white clothing), Śuklavastrottarīyinī (Dressed in a white upper cloth);
<sup>165</sup>‘Raktapaṭṭaparidhānā (Dressed in red silk), Rakta vasrottarīyinī (Dressed in a red upper cloth);
‘Pītapaṭṭaparidhānā (Dressed in yellow silk), Pītapa ṭṭottarīyinī (Dressed in a yellow upper silk cloth);
<sup>166</sup>‘Kṛṣṇapaṭṭaparidhānā (Dressed in black silk), Kṛṣṇa paṭṭottarīyinī (Dressed in a black upper silk cloth);
‘Vṛndāvanacarī (Roaming about Vṛndāvana), Vaṃśīnādinī (Sounding the flute), Vanamālinī (Wearing a garland of forest flowers), <sup>167</sup>Vṛndāvaneśvarī Rādhā (Rādhā, the mistress of Vṛndāvana), Kṛṣṇakāryaprasādhanī (Accomplisher of Kṛṣṇa’s tasks), Padminī, Nāgarī (Cunning), Gopī (Cowherdess), Kālindī avagāhinī (Joining the Yamunā river), <sup>168</sup>Gopīśvarapriyābhṛtyā (Dear servant of the master of the cowherdesses), Nagaramohanī (Enchanter of the cunning), Tripurādeśī (Follower of Tripurā), Tripurājñākarī (Performer of Tripurā’s orders), <sup>169</sup>Tripurāsannikarṣasthā (Who stays in the vicinity of Tripurā), Tripurā anucāriṇī (Attendant of Tripurā), Tripurāpurasaṃsthā (Dweller in Tripurā’s city), Rādhā, Padminīparā (The supreme Padminī), <sup>170</sup>Nānāsaubhāgyasampannā (Endowed with all fortune), Nānābharaṇabhūṣitā (Decorated with all kinds of decorations).<sup>ǁ</sup>
‘Because of your devotion, I have spoken this hymn of a thousand names. <sup>171</sup>Great mistress, handsome woman, if a man recites this hymn, the mantra and the Armour *kalpa* after *kalpa*, <sup>172</sup>after having engaged himself only in the Rādhikā *vidyā*, then even this minor *vidyā* will after a long time award perfection. <sup>173</sup>The *vidyā* of Padminī Rādhikā is settled among the minor *vidyās*.
‘Great mistress, the wise man should strenuously engage himself in a Mahāvidyā, <sup>174</sup>but in public, greatest mistress, beautiful one, with the Rādhā mantra. Listen, great mistress, to the name that is recommended for public use: [p. 332] <sup>175</sup>*‘ Kṛṣṇa Rādhe Govinda.*

‘This one should strenuously recite; it is a Vaiṣṇava mantra recommended everywhere. <sup>176</sup>KṚṢṆA is directly Kālikā; RĀDHĀ is Prakṛti Padminī and GOVINDA, mistress of gods, is Tripurā Sundarī herself.’

## Thirty-fourth Paṭala

*Devī said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Mighty-armed one, listen once again to my supreme words! Great god, master, please describe Hari’s name in detail. <sup>2</sup>Lord Sadāśiva, greatest master, please explain to me extensively all about that name of Hari that you earlier indicated!’

*Īśvara said:* <sup>3</sup>‘Hari’s name is twofold: the Great and the General. The General is intended for India, but the Great name is always great. <sup>4</sup>The Great name is recommended in heaven, earth and the nether worlds. That which Tripurā, the mistress of the world told Vāsudeva[^x-10] is the <sup>5</sup>General name, intended for India and honoured by men. The Great name, great mistress, contains all powers.

<sup>6</sup>*‘ Oṃ. Śivo Rāmaḥ Śivo Rāmaḥ Rāmo Rāmaḥ Śivaḥ Śivaḥ.*
‘ *Kṛṣṇaḥ Kṛṣṇaḥ Aiṃ Klīṃ Hrīṃ Śivaḥ Śivaḥ.*
*‘ Kṛṣṇaḥ Śivo Rāmo Hariḥ Śivo Rāmaḥ Śivaḥ Śivaḥ. Oṃ.*

<sup>7</sup>‘This great mantra of twenty-two names[^ba-10] is known as Hari’s name. <sup>8</sup>It is in all the Vedas considered suitable for Brahmins, Kṣatriyas and Vaiśyas. This name, great mistress, first purifies the ears. <sup>9</sup>This name, Hari’s delightful name, pervades the universe. Dear goddess, the name that consists of sixteen names and thirty-two syllables[^bb-10] <sup>10</sup>is recommended for the heretics. The one that has *Oṃ* at the beginning and end is always for the three upper classes beginning with the Brahmins, <sup>11</sup>great mistress, beautiful one, not for a Śūdra, for if he utters *Oṃ*, mistress of the gods, he will go to the Kumbhīpāka hell. <sup>12</sup>First one should take initiation and then only utter *Oṃ.*
‘The General or the Particular is intended for the Śūdra castes. <sup>13</sup>Greatest mistress, there are superior, middling and inferior Śūdras. The superior and middling Śūdras live on Rose Apple Island.[^bc-10] <sup>14</sup>The Great name is [p. 333] approved for all castes, but the General name, greatest mistress, is for the inferior ones in India. <sup>15</sup>Beautiful one, the General name is recommended for the inferior. Discarding the Seed of Violence[^bd-10] and the two kinds of Defilement,<sup>†ǁ</sup> goddess, <sup>16</sup>he should constantly recite the General Hari’s name of sixteen names in groups of ten in order to purify the ears.’

*Devī said:* <sup>17</sup>‘Greatest master, bearer of the spear! If the General name of Hari is defective, why then did mother Tripurā impart it to Vāsudeva? Please tell me this, Śaṅkara!’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>18</sup>‘The secret of Hari’s name always contains all power! Handsome woman, Tripurā told Vāsudeva the Great name <sup>19</sup>first and then only the one of sixteen names, for the metre, greatest mistress, beautiful one, that she told before, <sup>20</sup>is the metre of the Great name and does not concur with that of the General one.[^bx-8] The General name is said to have two kinds of Defilement.
<sup>21</sup>‘The secret of the Great name is very rare, mistress of the gods! In OṂ are the three gods Rudra, Viṣṇu and Brahmā. <sup>22</sup>ŚIVA is Kālikā herself and RĀMA is Tripurabhairavī, and Mahākālī Mahāmāyā takes the form of KṚṢṆA. <sup>23</sup>After ten names come three supreme Śaktis, beautiful one: Bhairavī, Kālī and Mahākālī.[^ca-10]
<sup>24</sup>‘Killer of Mahiṣa, greatest mistress! Hari’s name contains all power, but the General one of sixteen names <sup>25</sup>has two kinds of Defilement and is intended for groups of Śūdras; the General one is always intended for the inferior Śūdras. <sup>26</sup>However, a Śūdra who is initiated is known as a superior one.
‘The name of RĀMA, great mistress, always carries the power of the bow; <sup>27</sup>the name KṚṢṆA, great mistress, embodies all powers, and the name ŚIVA, mistress of the gods, destroys the sin of both the moving and nonmoving.
<sup>28</sup>‘Listen carefully once more to a Great name! [p. 334] *Oṃ Hare Kṛṣṇa Govinda Oṃ Hrīṃ Janārdana Hṛṣikeśa Hrīṃ Oṃ.*

<sup>29</sup>‘Thus have I told you the name, the most splendid name of Hari. This name, great mistress, is honoured by the goddess who dwells in the three worlds. <sup>30</sup>This name, great mistress, always increases fortune.
‘Daughter of the mountain, a Mahāvidyā should always be concealed. <sup>31</sup>For one who conceals it in this manner, mistress of the gods, for one who conceals it in a hundred and eight different ways, for him only does a Mahāvidyā award perfection.’

## Thirty-fifth Paṭala

*Devī said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Be kind and speak to me once more, great god Śaṅkara! Great Padminī Rādhā attained the abode of Tripurā, <sup>2</sup>but where did Candrāvalī and all the others go? Fortunate one, Śaṅkara, describe all of this in detail!’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>3</sup>‘Great mistress, beautiful, fair-complexioned one, all of them dissolved into the garland, into the garland given by Tripurā.[^cb-10] <sup>4</sup>Through the worship of Tripurā’s feet there are always millions of heaps of universal eggs resting in Vāsudeva’s garland. <sup>5</sup>Great mistress, Hari quickly gathered all of them together and then speedily proceeded to Dvārakā, where she who rides the lion dwells.
<sup>6</sup>‘Listen once more, impudent woman, greatest mistress! I will explain the ocean of knowledge to you: Padminī’s Gāyatrī, the supreme syllables.
<sup>7</sup>‘ *Aiṃ Hrīṃ*.
We inquire about Padminī Rādhikā, ‘We mediate on her who dwells in Tripurā’s city;
‘May this Gaurī inspire us.
<sup>8</sup>‘Because of your devotion, I have narrated this Tantra with its Gāyatrī. It is therefore a Bhāgavata Tantra, beautiful-eyed one. <sup>9</sup>Greatest mistress, wherever in the Tantras one can find the supreme part of Gāyatrī, that should be known as the Bhāgavata. <sup>10</sup>Greatest mistress, wherever in the Tantras that the complete syllables of the Gāyatrī exist, that indeed is the Bhāgavata, my dear. <sup>11</sup>A Purāṇa or supreme Tantra that contains the mere letters of Gāyatrī is not the Bhāgavata, for it is always devoid of power.
<sup>12</sup>‘Great mistress, the old mother is Kalāvatī herself. My dear, she always stays at the banks of the ocean of nectar. <sup>13</sup>In this rare form, goddess Kalāvatī Mahāmāyā always stays there in the form of a *dūtī* to attend to the needs of Kṛṣṇa. <sup>14</sup>My dear, the garland around Kṛṣṇa’s neck is Kalāvatī herself. [p. 335] <sup>15</sup>‘Goddess! Thus have I told you the incomparable Rādhā Tantra. Because of your devotion, I have narrated it. It must not be revealed to an animal.[^cc-10] Great mistress, this Tantra is advantageous and the greatest benediction; beautiful one, loving one, this Tantra should be proclaimed!’

## Thirty-sixth Paṭala

*Devī said:* <sup>1</sup>‘Be kind and speak to me once more, great god Śaṅkara, greatest master, about Padminī’s enchanting secret truth, <sup>2</sup>about the beautiful and most enchanting lotus of a thousand petals. Explain all of this in detail, great god and master!’

*Īśvara replied:* <sup>3</sup>‘Listen, delicate one, and I will tell you the enchanting truth of Vraja. When you gave up your body at Dakṣa’s sacrifice, affectionate one, <sup>4</sup>the supreme fifty Seats sprung up from the pieces of your body. Great mistress! Mathurā, the circle of Vraja, is the Seat of the hair. <sup>5</sup>I will here tell you a secret, listen carefully!
‘Great mistress, there is always a lotus in your hair. <sup>6</sup>When your hair fell down on earth, great mistress, first fell the lotus, greatest mistress, red like the the sun at dawn. <sup>7</sup>On top of the lotus is the mass of hair, perfumed with all kinds of scents, the hair beautified by bees and adorned on all sides by cuckoos – <sup>8</sup>these bees and cuckoos are all *yoginīs* and your *dūtis*.
‘Mathurā, the circle of Vraja, rests on the curls of your hair; <sup>9</sup>the curls of your hair, goddess, steal away the life of Viṣṇu. Pārvati, great mistress, the distance that the lowing of a cow traverses <sup>10</sup>should be known as a *krośa*; it is never otherwise. Sinless one, the land of yours has a circumference of eighty *krośas*.
<sup>11</sup>‘Mathurā, the circle of Vraja, rests within the curls of your hair. The lotus in your hair, red like the sun at dawn, <sup>12</sup>has one thousand petals and is furnished with millions of Gāyatrīs. Great mistress, the seed-vessel is the city of Kuṇḍalinī itself; <sup>13</sup>the lotus is constantly filled with masses of the highest bliss.
‘Wherever a limb or secondary limb of Satī fell, <sup>14</sup>there one should understand to exist a most rare Seat of perfection. The fifty different Seats are horrible and very frightening, <sup>15</sup>but the Seat of the hair, beautiful one, is gentle and enchanting to Viṣṇu. Great mistress, Hari always dwells in the Seat of the hair.
<sup>16</sup>‘Goddess, wherever Kātyāyanī Māyā, Mahāmāyā, the world itself, stays, there stay always the fifty Mātṛkās in her company. <sup>17</sup>In the middle is Kalāvatī Māyā, the mother of the world, the one who gave birth to the world. [p. 336] Goddess and greatest mistress! <sup>18</sup>The nectarean water of the Yamunā river is filled with the *rasa* of their love. My dear, Kalāvatī Māyā herself becomes manifold.
<sup>19</sup>‘Greatest mistress Pārvatī, wherever the Kadamba trees take the form of the desire tree, your Seats are Golokas, <sup>20</sup>for that which awards the four goals of life is called Goloka. It is a Seat that contains all places of pilgrimage and that gives *dharma*, *artha*, *kāma* and *mokṣa*.
<sup>21</sup>‘That which is Goloka Māthura is made of your hair. Great mistress, it is said that the lotus has fifty petals. <sup>22</sup>Greatest mistress, on each and every petal stay the Mātṛkās, one after another, <sup>23</sup>by simply the touch of which even the unqualified becomes qualified. My dear, through the trick of his yogic sleep, Vāsudeva is a great ghost, <sup>24</sup>but simply by the touch of the Mātṛkās, he is Śabdabrahman. Therefore, great mistress, Viṣṇu himself is unqualified, <sup>25</sup>but simply by the touch of the Mātṛkās, Kṛṣṇa is always qualified.
‘Pārvatī! When he is without the Mātṛkās, he is unqualified; <sup>26</sup>he remains eternally there with his club, Pārvatī. Great mistress, unqualified Viṣṇu is white, but the glow that is in the club is the highest Śakti. <sup>27</sup>In this way, Pārvatī, should you understand Brahman; Śabdabrahman is qualified simply by the touch of the Mātṛkās.’
Devī said: <sup>28</sup>‘Greatest master, rich in penance, please tell me about the most worshipable Mātṛkās, always attracting Kṛṣṇa, <sup>29</sup>and about the fifty supreme petals among the petals of the lotus, always containing all the fifty principles as well as the three qualities!’

Īśvara replied: <sup>30</sup>‘Great mistress, listen to a secret truth, most rare; the truth of the lotus, beautiful one, delightful to hear and increasing happiness, <sup>31</sup>giving pleasure, giving *mokṣa*, my dear, giving *artha* and always giving *kāma*! The lotus that decorates your hair is perfumed. <sup>32</sup>On top of it lies your enchanting hair, great mistress and goddess, called Vṛndāvana, where supreme Kātyāyanī dwells.
<sup>33</sup>‘Great mistress, listen carefully to the names of the Mātṛkās, simply by hearing which misfortunes are destroyed! <sup>34</sup>I bow to the highest mistress and speak, sweetly smiling, loving one: without the Mātṛkās everything becomes meaningless.
<sup>35</sup>‘Brahmāṇī, Caṇḍikā, Raudrī, Gaurī, Indrāṇī, Kaumārī, Vaiṣṇavī, Durgā, Nārasiṃhī, Kālikā, <sup>36</sup>Śivadūtī, Vārāhī, Maheśī, Kauśikī, Śākambharī, Jayantī, Maṅgalā, Pālikā, <sup>37</sup>Medhā, Śivarūpā, Śāmbhārī, Bhīmarūpiṇī, Śāntā, Bhrāmarī, Rudrarūpiṇī, <sup>38</sup>Ambikā, Kṣemā, Vahnirūpiṇī, Dhātrī, Bahurūpā, Svāhā, Svadhā, <sup>39</sup>Aparṇā, Mahodarī, Ghorarūpā, Mahākālī, Bhayaṃkarī, <sup>40</sup>Kṣemaṃkarī, Nāyikā, Caṇḍā, Caṇḍavatī, Caṇḍikā, Ugracaṇḍā, <sup>41</sup>Caṇḍāvatī, Mahādevī, Priyā, Nityā, Vāmarūpiṇī, <sup>42</sup>Priyaṃkarī, Sanātanī, Kalavikariṇī and Balapramathinī: <sup>43</sup>these are the Mātṛkā goddesses that stay on the fifty [p. 337] petals.[^cd-10] Listen, my dear, for I will now tell you about their qualities. They all stay on the petals, one after the other, beginning from the East.
<sup>44</sup>‘Brahmāṇī is golden and decorated with all kinds of ornaments. She stays in the middle of the Brahmāṇi petal and is full of the *rasa* of amourous love.
<sup>45</sup>‘Caṇḍikā is red like the rising sun. She stays on the Caṇḍikā petal, she carries a pot of nectar and gives Kṛṣṇa fearlessness.
<sup>46</sup>‘Raudrī is black in body and face and always wears yellow clothing. She stays in the middle of the Raudrī petal and always attends to the needs of Kṛṣṇa.
<sup>47</sup>‘Gaurī is Mahāmāyā, a Mātṛkā that enchants Kṛṣṇa. She stays in the middle of the Gaurī petal and always bewilders Kṛṣṇa.
<sup>48</sup>‘Indrāṇī is red like the rising sun and shines with a thousand eyes. She stays in the middle of the Indrāṇī petal and gives good fortune to Kṛṣṇa.
<sup>49</sup>‘Kaumārī has golden limbs and stays on the Kaumārī petal. Her limbs are smeared with various perfumes, and she gives perfumes to Kṛṣṇa.
<sup>50</sup>‘Vaiṣṇavī has a dark body. She stays on the Vaiṣṇavī petal, and she is ornamented with all kinds of ornaments, greatest mistress.
<sup>51</sup>‘Durgā has a yellow body. She constantly stays on the Durgā petal. She makes Kṛṣṇa fearless and always fulfills the *rasa* of amourous love.
<sup>52</sup>‘Nārasiṃhī has a light body and stays on the Nṛsiṃha petal. Greatest mistress, she always increases Kṛṣṇa’s strenght.
<sup>53</sup>‘Kālikā has a black body. She stays on the Kālī petals. She is dressed in the directions, and she keeps her hair loose. Beautiful one, she is Kṛṣṇa’s mother.
<sup>54</sup>‘Goddess Śivadūtī is famed as being red. My dear mistress of gods, she always stays on the Śiva petals.
<sup>55</sup>‘Cāmuṇḍā, great mistress, fair-complexioned one, is black. Loving one, mistress of gods, she always stays on the Kṛṣṇa petals.<sup>‡ǁ</sup>
<sup>56</sup>‘Goddess Vārāhī is always multicoloured. Daughter of the mountain, she always stays in the middle of the Vārāhī petal.
<sup>57</sup>‘Maheśī, great mistress, stays on the Maheśī petal. She should be understood to always be white and always eager for amourous love.
<sup>58</sup>‘Kauśikī, great mistress, has a red body and red eyes. She stays in the middle of the Kauśikī petal, and she is form of amourous love itself. [p. 338] <sup>59</sup>‘Goddess Śākambharī, sweetly smiling one, wears yellow clothes. She stays in the middle of the Śākambharī petal; she is constantly bent from the weight of her breasts.
<sup>60</sup>‘Jayantī, my dear, great mistress, is always black. She stays in the middle of the Jayantī petal and gives victory to Kṛṣṇa.
<sup>61</sup>‘Maṅgalā, great mistress, has a green form. She stays in the middle of the Maṅgalā petal and always awards auspiciousness.
<sup>62</sup>‘Pālikā, mistress of gods, sweetly smiling one, is black. She stays in the middle of the Pālikā petal and always awards bliss.
<sup>63</sup>‘Medhā, greatest mistress, is always red. Mistress of the gods, my dear, she always stays on the Medhā petals.
